Friday. Easter day, of the resurrection of the Lord, with the next following and the third, if
there be communicants. The day of the Ascension of Christ. Whitsunday, of the sending of the
Holy Ghost. The feast of the holy Trinity falleth on the next Sunday after Pentecost. Over and
above these, these feasts also shall be kept: the day of St John the Baptist, to the honour of
the holy office of preaching the Gospel of Christ; the day of the Visitation, when Mary visited
her cousin Elisabeth, because of that history of the Gospel; the day of Michael, whereon one is
to preach of the holy angels. […] One may also keep the days of the holy apostles, so that one
preach thereon before noon and, if occasion serve, hold the communion. But in the afternoon one
may well attend to the daily work; or, if one will, one may put over the Gospels and remembrance
of the holy apostles to the next Sunday that followeth, to the midday or evening sermon.
Likewise it may be done also with certain other feasts of the saints whose histories are written
in the Gospel, as are St Paul's conversion, Mary Magdalene, St John's beheading, St Stephen,
etc.

The Merseburg synodal instruction sets the two ranks side by side, and its title for the lower
rank is a phrase worth noting: "doch ahne gebotene feir", without a commanded holy day.
**Merseburg, *Synodalunterricht*, 1544** (Sehling 2, p. 20):

<!-- doc 1211 -->
> Den ganzen tag zu feiren fur und nachmittage zu predigen: Alle sontage und volgende
> evangelische festa: nativitas Christi sampt dem andern auch dem dritten tage, so man daran
> communicanten hat […] Hiruber sollen dise feirtage gehalten werden: der tag S. Johannis des
> teufers zu ehren dem heiligen predigtampt des evangelii von Christo der tag visitationis, da
> die junkfrau Maria ihre muhme Elisabet heimgesucht hat von wegen der feinen historien des
> evangelii der tag Michaelis dar von den lieben engeln zu predigen. An volgenden tagen solle
> fur mittage predigt und communion, do es fur fiele, gehalten und die dem volk auf den sontag
> zuvor doch ahne gebotene feir vorkundiget werden. Als nemlich der tag cene domini, so man den
> grunen donnerstag nennet, daran von dem abentmahl und hochwirdigen sacrament zu predigen der
> tag des leidens Christi, so der karfreitag genennet wirt, dar man die ganz historia des
> leidens Christi dem volk furtragen solt der tag Petri und Pauli und aller apostel zu ehren des
> heiligen apostelampts

To keep holy day the whole day, and to preach before and after noon: all Sundays, and the
following evangelical feasts: the Nativity of Christ with the second and also the third day, if
there be communicants thereon […] Over and above these, these holy days shall be kept: the day
of St John the Baptist, to the honour of the holy office of preaching the Gospel of Christ; the
day of the Visitation, when the Virgin Mary visited her cousin Elisabeth, because of the fine
history of the Gospel; the day of Michael, whereon to preach of the dear angels. On the
following days preaching and communion, if it so fall out, shall be held before noon, and they
shall be declared to the people on the Sunday before, yet without a commanded holy day: namely
the day of the Lord's Supper, which is called Maundy Thursday, whereon to preach of the Supper
and the most worthy Sacrament; the day of the Passion of Christ, which is called Good Friday,
whereon the whole history of the Passion of Christ should be set forth to the people; the day of
Peter and Paul and of all apostles, to the honour of the holy apostolic office

**Note.** Maundy Thursday and Good Friday stand in the lower rank here, beside the apostles. The
feasts of Christ are placed by their standing as holy days, not by their theological weight.

The Mecklenburg order of 1552 is the fullest form of the Saxon scheme, and it is the one most
often copied. **Mecklenburg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1552** (Sehling 5, pp. 200–201):

<!-- doc 1922 -->
> Von den besondern festen oder feiertagen, so man im jar halten sol. Uber die gemeinen sontage
> sollen gehalten werden die hohen heubtfest des herrn Christi, als da sind. Der tag Nativitatis
> oder der geburt Christi, samt dem andern und dritten folgenden. Der tag Circumcisionis oder
> der beschneidung Christi. Der tag Epiphanie, das ist der erscheinung oder offenbarung Christi,
> den man nennet der heiligen drei könig tag. Der tag der opferung Christi im tempel, genant
> Purificationis Mariae. Der tag Annunciationis Mariae oder Conceptionis Christi, da Christus in
> der jungfrauen leib empfangen ist. Der tag Coenae domini, den man den grönen donnerstag
> nennet, daran von des herrn abendmal sol gepredigt werden. Der tag Passionis domini oder des
> leidens und sterbens Christi, welcher der karfreitag genennet wird. Der ostertag oder Pasca,
> der tag der ufferstehung Christi, samt dem folgenden montag und dienstag. Der tag Ascensionis
> oder himelfart Christi. Der pfingstag, samt dem folgenden montag und dienstag. Der tag
> Trinitatis gefelt uff den nehesten sontag nach pfingsten. Uff diese fest sol es mit ordnung
> der lection, gesange, predigten und communio gehalten werden, wie den gemeinen sontagen,
> allein, das die introitus, sequenz und prefationes de tempore gesungen werden. […] Uber diese
> furnemsten fest sollen auch die gehalten werden. Die tage der aposteln. Der tag S. Johannis
> des teufers. Der tag Visitationis, da Maria ire mume Elisabeth heimsucht. […] Der tag
> Michaelis, an welchem die lere von den heiligen engeln dem volk sol furgetragen werden. An
> diesen tagen sol man vor mittage predigen, und so communicanten da sind, die communion halten,
> wie an den sontagen. Nach mittage aber mag man wol der gewönlichen erbeit warten. Ausgenomen
> den tag Michaelis, an welchem vor und nach mittage sol gepredigt werden. Das fest aber
> Assumptionis, oder himelfart Marie, sol hinfurder ganz abgethan sein, und an des selben stat
> das fest S. Michaelis als ein viergezeiten fest gehalten werden.

Of the special feasts or holy days which shall be kept in the year. Over and above the common
Sundays shall be kept the high chief feasts of the Lord Christ, which are these: the day of the
Nativity or birth of Christ, with the second and third following; the day of the Circumcision of
Christ; the day of the Epiphany, that is, of the appearing or manifestation of Christ, which is
called the day of the Three Kings; the day of the offering of Christ in the temple, called the
Purification of Mary; the day of the Annunciation of Mary or Conception of Christ, when Christ
was conceived in the Virgin's womb; the day of the Lord's Supper, which is called Maundy
Thursday, whereon shall be preached of the Lord's Supper; the day of the Passion of the Lord, or
of the suffering and death of Christ, which is called Good Friday; Easter day, or Pascha, the
day of the resurrection of Christ, with the Monday and Tuesday following; the day of the
Ascension of Christ; Whitsunday, with the Monday and Tuesday following; the day of the Trinity
falleth on the next Sunday after Pentecost. On these feasts it shall be done, as touching the
order of lessons, chants, sermons and communion, as on the common Sundays; only that the
introits, sequences and prefaces *de tempore* be sung. […] Over and above these chiefest feasts
these also shall be kept: the days of the apostles; the day of St John the Baptist; the day of
the Visitation, when Mary visiteth her cousin Elisabeth; […] the day of Michael, whereon the
doctrine of the holy angels shall be set forth to the people. On these days one shall preach
before noon, and if there be communicants, hold the communion, as on Sundays; but in the
afternoon one may well attend to the wonted work. Except the day of Michael, whereon there shall
be preaching before and after noon. But the feast of the Assumption, or Ascension of Mary, shall
henceforth be wholly done away, and in the stead of the same the feast of St Michael be kept as
a feast of the four times.

**Note.** Michaelmas has a place of its own in this scheme:

- It is listed with the second rank, but it is preached "before and after noon" like a feast of
  Christ.
- It takes the Assumption's place as one of the four *Vierzeiten* (§9).

The Mecklenburg text, with small changes, stands in these orders:

| Order | Sehling | Changes of note |
|---|---|---|
| Pfalz-Zweibrücken, *Kirchenordnung*, 1557 | 18, pp. 205–206 | apostles' days not named one by one (listed in the *Feiertagsordnung* of 1561, §6.2) |
| Lüneburg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1564 | 6/1, pp. 550–551 | follows Mecklenburg closely, including the prefaces *de tempore* |
| Wolfenbüttel, *Kirchenordnung*, 1569 | 6/1, pp. 151–152 | "Aber an solchen apostelntagen nach der predigt mag ein jeder seiner arbeit woll warten"; Corpus Christi explicitly abolished |
| Oldenburg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1573 | 7/2.1, pp. 1096–1097 | Michaelmas "anstadt des fests assumtionis oder himelfart Mariae als ein viergezeitenfest"; Te Deum for the harvest |
| Lüneburg (city), *Kirchenordnung*, 1575 | 6/1, pp. 660–661 | as Lüneburg 1564 |
| Lippe, *Kirchenordnung*, 1571 | 21, pp. 391–392 | prefaces *de tempore* only "auff den Viergezeiten Festagen" (§9) |
| Verden, *Kirchenordnung*, 1606 | 7/1, p. 166 | Trinity "hochfeyerlich" |
| Waldeck, *Kirchenordnung*, 1556 | 9, pp. 272–273 | Michaelmas "fur das virte heuptfest"; Good Friday "halb feierlich biß auff den mittag" (§6.1) |
| Kronstadt (Transylvania), *Agende*, 1547 | 24, p. 255 | the list itself follows the Nürnberg model (§5.7, §8.3); the closing admonition is taken from the Saxon order of 1539/40 |

### 5.3 The Württemberg family: holy days proclaimed by the magistrate

The Württemberg orders make the magistrate's proclamation the mark of a holy day (§3.5). At
first they drew only one clear line: the high feasts of Christ kept the whole day, and the rest
kept only in the forenoon.

**Württemberg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1536** (Sehling 16, pp. 105–106):

<!-- doc 651 -->
> Es sollen aber in unserm Fürstenthumb nachvolgende fest- und feyrtag gehalten werden: Erstlich
> alle Sontag, Item, alle hohe fest Christi als namlich Weyhennächt, Beschneidung, Ostertag,
> Auffarttag, Pfingstag. Und sollen dise tag doch auch ungefar gehalten und niemand sein
> gewissen damit beschwert werden […] Weiter aber sollen all pfarrer auff nachverzeichnet tag
> morgens predigen und als dann dem volck anzeigen, das sich nachmittag meniglich an sein arbeit
> verfügen möge unnd hierinn niemandt verstrickt sein sol. Namlich auff der heiligen drey künig
> oder den zwölfften tag, da soll man predigen aus dem Mattheo das ander Capitel, den grünen
> donnerstag, den karfreitag, so man den passion prediget, unser frawen verkündung tag
> Annuntiationis, unnser frawen Reynigung Purificationis, aller apostel tag, Sant Johannis des
> täuffers tag.

But in our principality the feasts and holy days following shall be kept: first all Sundays;
item, all the high feasts of Christ, namely Christmas, the Circumcision, Easter day, Ascension
day, Whitsunday. And these days also shall be kept without constraint, and no man's conscience
burdened therewith […] But further, all parsons shall preach in the morning on the days
hereafter noted, and then shew the people that every man may betake himself to his work in the
afternoon, and that herein no man shall be bound: namely on the day of the Three Kings or
Twelfth Day, whereon shall be preached out of Matthew the second chapter; Maundy Thursday; Good
Friday, when the Passion is preached; our Lady's Annunciation day; our Lady's Purification; all
apostles' days; St John the Baptist's day.

The Württemberg list follows the preamble of §3.5. **Württemberg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1553**
(Sehling 16, p. 266):

<!-- doc 671 -->
> Alle Sontag Der Christag Der nächst tag darnach Der Jars tag Der öberst, Epiphania genannt Der
> Ostertag sampt dem nächsten darnach Die himelfart Christi Der Pfingstag sampt volgendem Montag
> Die Liechtmeß Purificationis Mariae Verkündigung Mariae, genannt Annunciationis Aller Aposteln
> tag Joannis Baptisten

All Sundays. Christmas day. The next day after. New Year's day. Twelfth Day, called the
Epiphany. Easter day with the next after. The Ascension of Christ. Whitsunday with the Monday
following. Candlemas, the Purification of Mary. The Annunciation of Mary. All apostles' days.
John the Baptist's.

**Note.** In 1553 every day on the list is a holy day, with no half days. The distinction came
back in later orders of the family:

- **Pfalz-Zweibrücken 1561** added half days (§6.2).
- **Pfalz-Veldenz 1574** added half days as well (§6.2).
- **Baden 1603** has the same list but allows field-work after the morning service:

**Baden, *Kirchenordnungsmandat*, 1603** (Sehling 16, p. 559):

<!-- doc 715 -->
> Die Sontag seyndt aller dings stricte zufeyren undt alle Feldt- und andere arbeit
> einzustellen, zugleich auch die ordentliche Fest- und Aposteltag vormittag simpliciter
> gefeyret, nachmittag allein mit der discretion zur Feldtarbeit zugelaßen werden soll

The Sundays are to be kept holy altogether strictly, and all field-work and other labour to
cease; likewise the ordinary feast days and apostles' days shall be kept holy in the forenoon
without condition, and in the afternoon field-work shall be allowed only with discretion

### 5.4 The Bugenhagen family: "the three great feasts of Christ"

Bugenhagen's orders count feasts from the three great feasts of Christ. Each is kept three full
days. The rest are "full" holy days for their Gospel histories. The apostles are commemorated on
the next Sunday.

**Braunschweig, *Kirchenordnung*, 1528** (Sehling 6/1, p. 397):

<!-- doc 1983 -->
> De dre groten feste Christi, de de Christene stedes hebben geholden, alse Winachten, Paschen
> unde Pynxten, eyn jewelick fest dre dage vullen to vyren, darinne geprediget schal werden up
> den morgen, vor middage, na middage unde up den avent, darto gesungen, alse na gesecht schal
> werden. Darto nyejarsdach, Epiphanie, dat is der apenbaringe edder koningedach,
> purificationis, dat is Marien kerckgank, Marien vorkundinge, des Heren hemmelvart, Joannis
> Baptiste, Marien berchgank vullen to vyren, nicht umme der dage willen, sonder umme des
> predigens willen, dewile de historien in den evangelien begrepen synt unde bedrapen unsen
> Heren Christum etc. Item Michaelis vor eyn vehrtidenvest, to offeren dat vehrtidenoffer in de
> gemeyne schatkaste, ock vullen to vyren. […] Der hilgen apostelen unde Magdalene unde
> Laurentii, des hilgen kastenheren edder diakens, gedechtnissen scholen gelecht werden up den
> negesten Sundach na öreme kalenderdage, also dat me vlitich predigen schal dat evangelium
> vamme Sundage alse up eynen anderen Sundach unde deme jo nichts affbreken; alle sank unde
> misse schal ock syn vamme Sundage, alleyne amme ende des sermons schal de predicante seggen:
> Myne frunde in Christo, in disser weke plecht me gedenken des hilgen apostels N., van deme
> lest me so in evangelio, in Actis apo. etc.

The three great feasts of Christ which the Christians have always kept, as Christmas, Easter and
Whitsuntide, every feast to be fully kept holy three days, wherein there shall be preaching in
the morning, before noon, after noon and at evening, and singing thereto, as shall be said
hereafter. Thereto New Year's day, the Epiphany, that is the day of the manifestation or the
Kings' day, the Purification, that is Mary's church-going, Mary's Annunciation, the Lord's
Ascension, John the Baptist, Mary's going over the hills, to be fully kept holy; not for the
days' sake, but for the preaching's sake, forasmuch as the histories are contained in the
Gospels and concern our Lord Christ, etc. Item, Michaelmas for a feast of the four times, to
offer the four-times offering into the common treasure-chest, also to be fully kept holy. […]
The memorials of the holy apostles, and of Magdalene, and of Laurence the holy chest-master or
deacon, shall be laid upon the next Sunday after their calendar day; so that one shall
diligently preach the Gospel of the Sunday as upon another Sunday, and take nothing at all from
it; all chant and mass also shall be of the Sunday; only at the end of the sermon the preacher
shall say: My friends in Christ, in this week it is wont to be remembered of the holy apostle
N., of whom one readeth thus in the Gospel, in the Acts of the apostles, etc.

**Note.**

- **Why the apostles lost their holy day.** The editor records that Bugenhagen first meant to
  keep the apostles' days as feasts. He gave up the plan when it was objected that a town of
  craftsmen would then have too many holy days.
- **Hamburg 1529.** Hamburg has the same text (Sehling 5, p. 514).
- **Lübeck 1531.** Lübeck adds the list of the four times (§9).

**Pomerania, *Kirchenordnung*, 1535** (Sehling 4, p. 343):

<!-- doc 1856 -->
> Unde de historien van unserm leven heren Jesu Christo nicht vorgeten werden, wille wi de festa
> Christi alle beholden, als winachten dree dage, osteren dree dage, pinxsten dree dage, dar
> negest Circuncisionis, Epiphanie, Purificationis, Annunciationis, Ascensionis, Trinitatis,
> Johannis Baptiste to predekende, van siner entfanginge, van siner gebort, van sinem predekende
> unde amt, unde van siner enthovedinge, dat fest hört ock up Christum, unde de historie is ock
> in den evangelien geschreven. Visitationis des geliken. Item wi willen ock beholden dat fest
> Michaelis to predikende van den engeln ut der hiligen schrift.

And that the histories of our dear Lord Jesus Christ be not forgotten, we will keep all the
feasts of Christ: as Christmas three days, Easter three days, Whitsuntide three days; next
thereafter the Circumcision, the Epiphany, the Purification, the Annunciation, the Ascension,
the Trinity; John the Baptist, to preach of his conception, of his birth, of his preaching and
office, and of his beheading, for that feast also pertaineth to Christ, and the history is also
written in the Gospels. The Visitation likewise. Item, we will also keep the feast of Michael,
to preach of the angels out of holy Scripture.

The Schleswig-Holstein list follows the principle quoted in §3.3. **Schleswig-Holstein,
*Kirchenordnung*, 1542** (Sehling 23, p. 95):

<!-- doc 1576 -->
> De dre groten Feste Christi vyren wy ein ytlick dre dage lanck umme der Historien Christi
> willen. Darbeneven vyren wy ock dat Fest Circumcisionis, Epiphaniae, Purificationis unde
> Annunctiationis Marie. […] Ock vyre wy de Festa Ascensionis und Visitationis […] Thom lesten
> dat fest Johannis Baptiste, Dat fest Michaelis thor dancksegginge Unde dat fest Omnium
> Sanctorum. Der Apostel Feste schal men apentlick affkündigen und thogelick dem feste Magdalene
> unde Laurentii am negst volgenden Sondage darna Predigen.

The three great feasts of Christ we keep holy each three days long, for the histories of Christ.
Beside these we keep holy also the feast of the Circumcision, of the Epiphany, of the
Purification and of the Annunciation of Mary. […] We keep holy also the feasts of the Ascension
and of the Visitation […] Last of all the feast of John the Baptist, the feast of Michael for
thanksgiving, and the feast of All Saints. The apostles' feasts one shall openly proclaim, and
preach them, together with the feast of Magdalene and of Laurence, on the next following Sunday
thereafter.

**Note.** The editor explains the "Festa Ascensionis" as the Assumption of Mary, on 15 August.
The Danish-Holstein order of 1542 is thus one of the few Lutheran orders that keeps the
Assumption as a holy day (§8.3). The Kiel draft behind it follows the Danish *Ordinatio
ecclesiastica* of 1537.

### 5.5 Numbered classes

Some orders number their ranks outright.

Grubenhagen names three classes, and gives each its reason. **Grubenhagen, *Kirchenordnung*,
1544** (Sehling 6/2, pp. 1032–1033):

<!-- doc 2056 -->
> VI. Von den feyertagen, welche man feyeren soll. Festum nativitatis Christi, S. Stephanstagk,
> des ersten merterers, S. Johannistagk, des evangelisten, der unschuldigen kinderlein tagk,
> weil die predigt wart, festum resurrectionis Christi, den Mondagk und auch den Dinstagk,
> festum Pentecostae, Pfingsten, den Mondagk und auch den Dinstagk zu feyeren, festum Michaelis
> archangeli. Das sind also die vier hauptfest, an welchen man den vierzeitpenning sammelt. Nun
> folgen andere herliche fest, die man auch feyren soll: Festum circumcisionis Domini, f.
> epiphaniae vel trium regum, f. purificationis Mariae, f. anuntiacionis Mariae, f. visitationis
> Mariae, f. ascensionis Jesu Christi, f. S. Johannis Baptistae. Das sind also andere sieben
> festage, die auch zum hochsten von wegen der historien zu feyren nottig. 1. S. Andres tagk, 2.
> S. Thomas tagk, 3. S. Matthias tagk, 4. den grünen Donnerstagk, 5. den stillen Freytagk, 6. S.
> Philippi et Jacobi tagk, 7. S. Petri et Pauli tagk, 8. S. […] Jacobs tagk, 9. S. Bartholomei
> tagk, 10. S. Matthäi apostoli tagk, 11. S. Simonis et Judä tagk. Diese tage der lieben
> heiligen und jünger unsers Hern Jesu Christi soll man vormittage alleine, weil das ampt wart,
> schuldig sein zu feyren.

VI. Of the holy days which shall be kept. The feast of the Nativity of Christ; St Stephen's day,
of the first martyr; St John's day, of the evangelist; the day of the innocent children, while
the sermon lasteth; the feast of the resurrection of Christ, the Monday and also the Tuesday;
the feast of Pentecost, Whitsuntide, the Monday and also the Tuesday to be kept holy; the feast
of Michael the archangel. These are therefore the four chief feasts, whereon the four-times
penny is gathered. Now follow other glorious feasts, which shall also be kept holy: the feast of
the Circumcision of the Lord; the feast of the Epiphany or of the Three Kings; the feast of the
Purification of Mary; the feast of the Annunciation of Mary; the feast of the Visitation of
Mary; the feast of the Ascension of Jesus Christ; the feast of St John the Baptist. These are
therefore seven other feast days, which also are in the highest degree needful to be kept holy
because of the histories. 1. St Andrew's day, 2. St Thomas's day, 3. St Matthias's day, 4.
Maundy Thursday, 5. Good Friday, 6. St Philip and James's day, 7. St Peter and Paul's day, 8. St
[…] James's day, 9. St Bartholomew's day, 10. St Matthew the apostle's day, 11. St Simon and
Jude's day. These days of the dear saints and disciples of our Lord Jesus Christ one shall be
bound to keep holy in the forenoon only, while the office lasteth.

**Note.** The first class is defined partly by a church payment. The four chief feasts are the
days on which the four-times penny is collected (§9). The Ascension stands in the second class,
and Maundy Thursday and Good Friday in the third. The editor notes that this departs from the
Wolfenbüttel order of 1543, which moved the apostles' days to the next Sunday.

**Regensburg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1567** (Sehling 13, p. 474):

<!-- doc 450 -->
> [5.] Welche fest in unserer kirchen gefeiert werden. Uber die gemeinen sontäge werden mit
> verstand christlicher freiheit gehalten: Erstlich alle festa Christi: Nativitatis mit den
> volgenden zweien tagen, Circumcisionis, Epiphaniae, Purificationis, Annunciationis, Ostertag
> samt den zweien nachvolgenden tagen, Ascensionis, Pfingstag sampt den volgenden zweien tagen.
> Zum andern werden gehalten alle aposteltage. Zum dritten: Johannis baptistae, Michaelis
> archangeli.

[5.] Which feasts are kept holy in our church. Over and above the common Sundays are kept, with
understanding of Christian liberty: first, all the feasts of Christ: the Nativity with the two
days following, the Circumcision, the Epiphany, the Purification, the Annunciation, Easter day
with the two days next following, the Ascension, Whitsunday with the two days following.
Secondly are kept all the apostles' days. Thirdly: John the Baptist, Michael the archangel.

**Note.** Regensburg's numbering is an order of listing, not of dignity. Communion and first
vespers, which mark the rank in this order, are given only "an festen Christi, allen und jeden",
on each and every feast of Christ (p. 475; see §11.3).

Hoya has four ranks, the fourth being the abolished Assumption. **Hoya, *Kirchenordnung*, 1571**
(Sehling 6/2, p. 1186):

<!-- doc 2065 -->
> Die vier hohen fest belangende, ordnen wir, das Weynachten, Ostern, Pfingsten mit den zweyen
> folgenden tagen sollen geruhet, gefeyert und mit übung göttliches worts zugebracht werden.
> Dieweil aber das fest der himelfart Mariae ohne grund und gezeugnis der schrift durch den
> antichrist eingefüret ist, sol es in unser graffschaft hiemit ganz abgethan sein und seiner
> hinfüro nicht gedacht werden. Wir ordnen aber anstadt desselben das fest S. Michaelis und
> wollen, das die pfarrkinder den tag feyerlich halten, auch ihrem pastorn an demselben tage ihr
> opfer und gebürliche pflicht gütlich entrichten und ohne hindernis dargeben sollen. Zum andern
> ordnen wir, das nachgeschriebene und verzeichnete fest und tage in unser herrschaft genzlich
> geruhet, gefeyret und geheiliget werden sollen, als des neuen jhars tag, der heiligen drey
> könig tag, das festum purificationis Mariae, verkündigung Mariae, der himelfart Christi,
> Johannis des teufers tag, visitationis Mariae, der tag coenae Domini, an welchem man vom h.
> nachtmal predigen sol, den Kahrfreytag […] Zum dritten ordnen wir, das man nachgesetzte
> apostel- und heiligentage, biß die serrnon geendet, feyren sol: als Andreae Thomae bekerung
> Pauli Matthiae Petri et Pauli Philippi et Jacobi Magdalenen Jacobi Bartholomaei decollationis
> Johannis Matthaei Simonis et Judae aller heiligen tag.

As touching the four high feasts, we ordain that Christmas, Easter and Whitsuntide, with the two
days following, shall be rested, kept holy, and spent in the exercise of God's Word. But
forasmuch as the feast of the Ascension of Mary was brought in by the Antichrist without ground
and witness of Scripture, it shall hereby be wholly done away in our county, and no more be
mentioned henceforth. But in the stead thereof we ordain the feast of St Michael, and will that
the parishioners keep the day solemnly, and also render to their pastor on the same day their
offering and due duty kindly and without hindrance. Secondly we ordain that the feasts and days
hereafter written and set down shall in our lordship be wholly rested, kept holy and hallowed:
as New Year's day, the day of the Three Kings, the feast of the Purification of Mary, the
Annunciation of Mary, the Ascension of Christ, John the Baptist's day, the Visitation of Mary,
the day of the Lord's Supper, whereon one shall preach of the holy Supper, Good Friday […]
Thirdly we ordain that the apostles' and saints' days hereafter set shall be kept holy until the
sermon be ended: as Andrew, Thomas, the Conversion of Paul, Matthias, Peter and Paul, Philip and
James, Magdalene, James, Bartholomew, the Beheading of John, Matthew, Simon and Jude, All
Saints' day.

The Harlingerland order repeats Hoya's text, divided into titled *Sectiones*. **Harlingerland
(East Frisia), *Kirchenordnung*, 1573/74** (Sehling 7/1, pp. 735–736):

<!-- doc 2122 -->
> Der eilfte articull. Von den festen. Sectio prima. Von den vier zeiten. Die vier hohen
> festtage belangende, ordnen wir auch, daß Wihnachten, Paschen, Pfingsten mit den zween
> folgenden tagen sollen gerouwet, gefyret und mit öving deß gottlichen wordeß geheiliget
> werden. Von der himmelfahrt Mariae. Dieweile daß fest ohne grund und gezeugniß der schrift
> durch dem antechrist ist angerichtet, soll idt ganz abgetaen sein und seiner nit gedacht
> werden. Ordnen derohalben, daß der [!] letzste und vierte hochfesttagzeit solle S. Michaelis
> tag sein. […] Sectio secunda. Von den fürnembsten festtagen. […] Sectio tertia. Von den
> aposteltagen. Ordnen wir, daß man undergeschrieben aposteltage, beht der sermön geendiget,
> fihren solle

The eleventh article. Of the feasts. Section the first. Of the four times. As touching the four
high feast days, we ordain also that Christmas, Easter and Whitsuntide, with the two days
following, shall be rested, kept holy and hallowed with the exercise of the divine Word. Of the
Ascension of Mary. Forasmuch as that feast was set up by the Antichrist without ground and
witness of the Scripture, it shall be wholly done away and not be mentioned. We ordain therefore
that the last and fourth high feast-tide shall be St Michael's day. […] Section the second. Of
the chiefest feast days. […] Section the third. Of the apostles' days. We ordain that the
apostles' days underwritten shall be kept holy until the sermon be ended

Sayn has four classes, set out as a principle before the list. The margin heads the paragraph
*Festa quadrupli*. **Sayn, *Kirchenzuchtordnung*, 1582** (Sehling 19/1, p. 368):

<!-- doc 1090 -->
> Unndt konden die festen durch gantze jahr in viererley artt oder underschiedliche hauffe
> getheilt werden: 1. Etzliche festa, das seind haubtfesta also zu nennen, die man solenniter
> den gantzen tag feieren unndt darauff die gemeine wochentliche arbeit soll underlaßen werden.
> 2. Die ander seind nur mittelmeßige festa, die da halb gefeiret werden also unndt der gestalt,
> das man vormittag zur kirchen komme, predigt haltte unnd hör, nach mittag aber in
> wochentlicher abeit vortfahre. 3. Die dritte festag könnenn ohn nachteil unndt ergernus
> verlegt unndt auffgehoben werden auf den gemeinen bettag, so sonst in der wochen zur predigt
> deputirt unndt geordenet ist. 4. Die viertten festtage, so zu bestettigung offentlicher
> abgotterei im bapstumb eingefuhrt unndt gehalten worden, sollen in unserm evangelischen
> kirchen gar abgeschafft unndt underlaßen werden.

And the feasts through the whole year might be divided into four kinds or several companies:
1. Some feasts are to be called chief feasts, which men shall keep holy solemnly the whole day,
   and whereon the common weekly work shall be left off. 2. The others are but middling feasts,
   which are kept half holy, in this wise and manner, that men come to church in the forenoon,
   hold and hear a sermon, but in the afternoon go on in the weekly work. 3. The third feast
   days may without hurt and offence be removed and taken up upon the common day of prayer,
   which otherwise is deputed and ordained in the week for preaching. 4. The fourth feast days,
   which were brought in and kept in the papacy for the confirming of open idolatry, shall in
   our evangelical churches be wholly done away and left off.

The list attached to it. **Sayn, *Kirchenzuchtordnung*, 1582** (Sehling 19/1, pp. 372–373):

<!-- doc 1090 -->
> Hauptvesta, so den gantzen tagh feierlich gehalten undt daruff die gemeine wochentliche arbeit
> soll underlassen werdenn. Der heilige christagh. S. Stephani tagh. S. Joannis tag vormittagh.
> Der neue jars tag vor- und nachmittagh. Epiphania Domini oder der heiligen drey konig tag.
> Purificationis Mariae oder liechtmeß. Annunciationis Mariae oder conceptionis christi, welches
> fest, so es in der marterwoch oder nach ostern gefelt, sol es auf den sonabend palmarum stets
> gelegt werden. Der ostertag sampt den zweien nachvolgenden, montag und dienstagh. Der
> himelfartstag Christi durch auß. Der pfingstag und beide volgende, montag und dinstag in
> pfingsten. Das fest der heiligen dreyfaltigkeit, so allwege acht tage nach pfingsten volget.
> […] Der tag Johannis des täuffers. S. Michaels tag, dar man pfleget von den h. engeln zu
> predigen und gott fur den guten schutz der guten geister zu dancken. Nachvolgende tage werden
> nur halb gefeiret also und der gestalt, das man vor mittage zur kirchen komme, predigt halte
> und hore, nach mittag aber in wochentlicher arbeit fortfahre. Alle apostel tage sollen
> gehalten werden, wen undt wie sie des jars fallen, doch allein mit einer predigt vormittag
> halb feierlich. Der aposteln tage sollen gleich geachtet und fur halbe feiertag gerechnet
> werden: Der grüne donnerstag […] Der charfreitag […] Pauli bekehrungstag umb der furtreflichen
> historien willen. Mariae visitationis oder heimsuchungfest. Diese volgende und dergleichen
> tage mogen auf denn verordenten bettagen in der wochen geleget werden, als: S. Laurentii tagh.
> S. Marci, des evangelisten. S. Lucas, des evangelisten. […] Vincula Petri. Decollationis
> Joannis Baptistae. […] Aller heyligen, omnium sanctorum. Mariae Magdalenen fest umb der
> schonen text und historien willen, so daruf zue handlen verordnet, damit [sie] in gedechtnus
> bleiben. Unten verzeichnete tag sollen gar abgeschaft und underlaßen werden, als die da zur
> abgotterey und aberglauben anleitung und vorschub geben. Alle Marienfest, sie haben nahmen wie
> sie wollen, welche hiebevor nicht benennet worden. Sonderlich aber festum conceptionis,
> nativitatis, praesentationis, assumptionis Mariae sollen in unsern evangelischen kürchen gar
> auffgehoben und abe sein

Chief feasts, which shall be kept solemnly the whole day, and whereon the common weekly work
shall be left off. The holy Christmas day. St Stephen's day. St John's day in the forenoon. New
Year's day, before and after noon. The Epiphany of the Lord, or the day of the Three Kings. The
Purification of Mary, or Candlemas. The Annunciation of Mary, or Conception of Christ; which
feast, if it fall in the week of the Passion or after Easter, shall always be laid on the
Saturday before Palm Sunday. Easter day with the two following, Monday and Tuesday. The day of
the Ascension of Christ, throughout. Whitsunday and both the following, Monday and Tuesday in
Whitsuntide. The feast of the holy Trinity, which followeth always eight days after Pentecost.
[…] The day of John the Baptist. St Michael's day, whereon men are wont to preach of the holy
angels and to thank God for the good protection of the good spirits. The days following are kept
but half holy, in this wise and manner, that men come to church before noon, hold and hear a
sermon, but in the afternoon go on in the weekly work. All apostles' days shall be kept, when
and as they fall in the year, yet only with one sermon in the forenoon, half holy. With the
apostles' days shall be counted alike, and reckoned for half holy days: Maundy Thursday […] Good
Friday […] St Paul's conversion day, for the excellent history's sake. The feast of Mary's
Visitation. These days following, and the like, may be laid on the appointed days of prayer in
the week: as St Laurence's day; St Mark the evangelist's; St Luke the evangelist's; […] Peter's
Chains; the Beheading of John the Baptist; […] All Saints; Mary Magdalene's feast, for the sake
of the fair text and history appointed to be handled thereon, that [they] may remain in
remembrance. The days noted beneath shall be wholly done away and left off, as those that give
occasion and furtherance to idolatry and superstition: all feasts of Mary, be their names what
they will, which have not been named heretofore; but in especial the feasts of the Conception,
Nativity, Presentation and Assumption of Mary shall in our evangelical churches be wholly taken
up and be gone

**Note.** This is the fullest four-rank system in the corpus. The Sayn *Kirchenordnung* of 1590
(Sehling 19/1, p. 389) later reduced it to a single list of holy days, with the harvest apostles
kept in the forenoon only (§6.4).

The Aschersleben town agenda has a middle rank of eight feasts over and above the three chief
feasts. **Aschersleben, *Kirchen-Agenda*, 1575** (Sehling 2, p. 479):

<!-- doc 1260 -->
> Uber die drei heubtfeste, sollen auch in dieser christlichen gemeinde diese nachfolgende
> gehalten werden. 1. Circumcisionis domini. 2. Epiphanias domini. 3. Purificationis Mariae. 4.
> Annunciationis Mariae. 5. Ascensionis domini. 6. Johannis baptistae. 7. Visitationis Mariae,
> 8. Michaelis. In diesen festen soll es durchaus, wie an sontagen gehalten werden, allein das
> mit singen und predigen auf die fest gesehen und figurirt werde. […] Von den festis
> apostolorum. Andreae, Thomae, Matthiae, Philippi Jacobi, Petri et Pauli, Jacobi, Bartholomaei,
> Matthaei, Simonis et Judae: Sollen in unser gemeine fruehe eine predigt und das officium
> gehalten werden vormittage alleine. Die arbeitsleute aber mögen ein jeder seiner hantierung
> warten

Over and above the three chief feasts, these following shall also be kept in this Christian
congregation: 1. The Circumcision of the Lord. 2. The Epiphany of the Lord. 3. The Purification
of Mary. 4. The Annunciation of Mary. 5. The Ascension of the Lord. 6. John the Baptist. 7. The
Visitation of Mary. 8. Michael. On these feasts it shall be done throughout as on Sundays; only
that in singing and preaching regard be had to the feast, and that figural music be used. […] Of
the feasts of the apostles. Andrew, Thomas, Matthias, Philip and James, Peter and Paul, James,
Bartholomew, Matthew, Simon and Jude: in our congregation there shall be held early a sermon and
the office, before noon only. But the working people may each attend to his handiwork

### 5.6 Great feasts and small feasts: Danzig and Thorn

In Danzig the rank of a feast was marked above all by the bells (§10.8). The church fathers of
St Mary's set down the custom in 1612. Article 16 lists every feast of the year as *ein gross
fest* or *ein klein fest*.

**Danzig, St Mary's, *Kirchenordnung*, 1612** (Sehling 4, pp. 203–204):

<!-- doc 1844 -->
> Art. 14. Vom beigern auf die grossen feste. Wan ein gross fest einfellet, welches allezeit mit
> drei predigten gehalten wird, alsdan wird des tages zuvor vormittage geleutet, wie sonst auf
> den tag in der woche gebreuchlich. […] Art. 15. Wie man auf die kleinen feste beigert. Auf die
> kleinen feste, welche allzeit nur mit einer predigt gehalten werden, es sei dan, das etwa
> eines auf einen sontag einfeilet, oder vom sontage dahin verlegt wird, alsdann wird des tages
> zuvor nach mittage, wen es eins geschlagen hat, mit der Dominical und Apostolica gebeigert und
> mit der Osanna darein geleutet und dis heisset man mit der Osanna beiern […] Art. 16. Namen
> der grossen und kleinen feste, nach dem lauf des jahres, an welchem man beigert.

Art. 14. Of chiming for the great feasts. When a great feast falleth, which is always kept with
three sermons, then on the day before it is rung in the forenoon as is otherwise used on a day
in the week. […] Art. 15. How one chimeth for the small feasts. For the small feasts, which are
always kept with one sermon only, unless one of them fall on a Sunday, or be removed thither
from the Sunday, then on the day before, in the afternoon, when it hath struck one, it is chimed
with the Dominical and the Apostolica, and rung in with the Osanna; and this is called chiming
with the Osanna […] Art. 16. The names of the great and small feasts, according to the course of
the year, whereon one chimeth.

**The Danzig list of 1612** (Art. 16, p. 204). It is condensed here from the two-column print.
The first entry, a small feast before Epiphany, has lost its name in the digitized text.

| Great feasts (*ein gross fest*) | Small feasts (*ein klein fest*) |
|---|---|
| Epiphany; Purification; Annunciation; Easter day, Easter Monday, Easter Tuesday ("mit einer predigt"); Ascension; Whitsunday, Whit Monday, Whit Tuesday ("mit einer predigt"); Trinity; Visitation ("mit predigt"); Christmas, St Stephen, St John the Evangelist ("mit 1 predigt") | Matthias; Philip and James; John the Baptist; Peter and Paul; James; Bartholomew; Matthew; Michaelmas ("mit 3 predigten … ein klein fest"); Simon and Jude; Andrew; Thomas |

Two entries on the list are "no feast". The Dominicans' day (5 August) and St Laurence's day are
rung with the great bell to open and close the town's market, and the list says of Laurence:
"ist kein fest".

Thorn sets the same two ranks in prose, and also speaks of the "small or half feasts". **Thorn,
*Kirchenordnung*, 1575** (Sehling 4, p. 239):

<!-- doc 1844 -->
> Alle feste unsers herrn und erlösers, als nemlich nativitatis Christi, circumcisionis,
> epiphaniae, purificationis, annunciationis, coenae dominicae, parasceves, paschae, ascensionis
> et pentecostes sollen so wohl ordentlich als ehrlich und mit grossem ernst, gebührlicher
> reverenz zum gedächtniss und vermahnung gehalten werden, sonderlich weinachten, ostern und
> pfingsten drei tage nacheinander: die ersten zwei tage mit dreien predigten wie sonsten am
> sonntage; am dritten tage, wo er nicht auf einen sonntage gefällt, mit zween predigten […]
> Belangende aber die feste oder feiertagen conversionis Pauli, Johannis Baptistae, visitationis
> Mariae, Michaelis, samt den zwölf aposteltagen, sollen mit zwei predigten gehalten werden […]
> wann aber solche kleine oder halbe feste auf einen sonntag kommen, mögen dieselben wohl auf
> den folgenden tag, nemlich auf den sonntag, verleget und zur vesper gehalten werden.

All the feasts of our Lord and Redeemer, namely the Nativity of Christ, the Circumcision, the
Epiphany, the Purification, the Annunciation, the Supper of the Lord, the Preparation, Easter,
the Ascension and Pentecost, shall be kept as well orderly as honourably, and with great
earnestness and due reverence, for remembrance and admonition; in especial Christmas, Easter and
Whitsuntide three days one after another: the first two days with three sermons, as otherwise on
the Sunday; on the third day, where it fall not on a Sunday, with two sermons […] But touching
the feasts or holy days of the Conversion of Paul, of John the Baptist, of the Visitation of
Mary, of Michael, together with the twelve apostles' days, they shall be kept with two sermons
[…] but when such small or half feasts come upon a Sunday, the same may well be removed to the
following day, namely to the Sunday [afternoon], and be kept at vespers.

**Note.** The sentence "auf den folgenden tag, nemlich auf den sonntag" is confused in the
print. The meaning, as the next sentence shows, is that a small feast falling near a Sunday is
handled at the Sunday vespers. Compare §14.

### 5.7 Wider calendars: the conservative orders

A number of orders keep much more than the pattern of §5.1. Some are early, and some come from
princes who were cautious about change. They show where the Lutheran lists started from.

**Brandenburg (Electorate), *Kirchenordnung* of Joachim II, 1540** (Sehling 3, p. 87):

<!-- doc 1746 -->
> Folgen die festa, so furnemlich in unsern landen, ausserhalb der sontage, gehalten und
> gefeiert werden sollen. Nemlich: Der Christtag. Der tag Stefani. Der tag Johannis Evangeliste.
> Des neuen jarstag oder Circumcisionis domini. Der heiligen dreier könig tag. Der tag
> Purificationis Marie. Der tag Matthie Apostoli. Der tag Annunciationis Marie. Der Ostertag.
> Ostermontag. Der tag Philippi et Jacobi. Ascensionis domini oder himelfart Christi. Der
> Pfingsttag. Montag. Dinstag. Der sontag Trinitatis. Der tag Corporis Christi. Der tag Johannis
> Baptiste. Der tag Petri und Pauli. Der tag Visitationis Marie. Der tag Marie Magdalene. Der
> tag Jacobi. Der tag Assumptionis Marie. Der tag Bartholomei. Der tag Nativitatis Marie. Der
> tag Matthei Apostoli. Der tag Simonis et Jude. Der tag Omnium Sanctorum. Der tag Martini. Der
> tag Andree. Der tag Laurentii. Der tag' Michaelis.

There follow the feasts which, besides the Sundays, shall chiefly be kept and held holy in our
lands. Namely: Christmas day. St Stephen's day. St John the Evangelist's day. New Year's day, or
the Circumcision of the Lord. The day of the Three Kings. The day of the Purification of Mary.
The day of Matthias the apostle. The day of the Annunciation of Mary. Easter day. Easter Monday.
The day of Philip and James. The Ascension of the Lord. Whitsunday. Monday. Tuesday. Trinity
Sunday. The day of Corpus Christi. The day of John the Baptist. The day of Peter and Paul. The
day of the Visitation of Mary. The day of Mary Magdalene. The day of James. The day of the
Assumption of Mary. The day of Bartholomew. The day of the Nativity of Mary. The day of Matthew
the apostle. The day of Simon and Jude. All Saints' day. St Martin's day. St Andrew's day. St
Laurence's day. St Michael's day.

**Note.** The list goes on with Catherine and Thomas. It is a single rank that keeps Corpus
Christi, the Assumption, the Nativity of Mary, All Saints, Martin, Laurence and Catherine. It
matches the conservative character of the Brandenburg order as a whole.

Hausmann's Anhalt order has an early, one-rank list with the apostles already reduced to the
forenoon. **Anhalt, Nicolaus Hausmann's order, 1532** (Sehling 2, p. 542):

<!-- doc 1262 -->
> Festa im jar sollen frei gehalden werden, zum exempel des glaubens zu uben, damit das junge
> und grobe volk gemussiget mügen zur prediget kommen […] und sint mit namen dise: Nativitas
> Christi mit zweien tagen. Neu jar, circumcisionis. Epiphanie, wilchs heist drei könig.
> Purificationis Christi et Marie. Annunciacionis Christi. Pascha mit zweien tagen, ostern.
> Ascensionis, himmelfart Christi. Pentecostes, pfingsten mit zwei tagen. Corporis Christi, des
> heiligen warlichnames tag. Johannis baptiste. Visitacionis, conceptionis Marie. Magdalene.
> Marie. Laurencii. Assumpcionis Nativitatis Michaelis. Omnium sanctorum. Martini. Aller apostel
> tage fur mittage.

The feasts in the year shall be kept free, for the exercise of faith, that the young and rude
people may be at leisure to come to the sermon […] and they are these by name: the Nativity of
Christ with two days; New Year, the Circumcision; the Epiphany, which is called Three Kings; the
Purification of Christ and of Mary; the Annunciation of Christ; Pascha with two days, Easter;
the Ascension, the ascending of Christ; Pentecost, Whitsuntide with two days; Corpus Christi,
the day of the holy true Body; John the Baptist; the Visitation, the Conception of Mary;
Magdalene; Mary; Laurence; the Assumption; the Nativity; Michael; All Saints; Martin; all
apostles' days in the forenoon.

The editor places the Pfalz-Neuburg list between Brandenburg 1540 and Brandenburg-Nürnberg 1533.
**Pfalz-Neuburg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1543** (Sehling 13, p. 94):

<!-- doc 386 -->
> so wöllen wir, das dise nachfolgende fest und feirtag, wie vor alters her, noch gehalten und
> gefeiret werden - doch, damit die andern tag der gedechtnus Christi und der rechten, waren
> heiligen, so in der heiligen schrift oder sonst irs christlichen glaubens, lebens, wunderwerk,
> leidens und seligen absterbens gewise zeugnus haben, nicht aufgehebt noch in vergessen
> gestellt, sonder ungefeiret in gedechtnus, wie vor alters her, lassen bleiben. Die fest, so
> man feiern sol. Der Neujarstag, Circumcisionis genannt, der Oberstag, Epiphaniae genannt,
> Lichtmeß, Purificationis Mariae genannt, Matthie des zwölf boten, Verkündung, Annunciationis
> Mariae genannt, der Palmtag, der Ostertag mit montag und aftermontag darnach, Philippi und
> Jacobi der zwölf boten, der Auffartstag, Ascensionis Domini genannt, der Pfingstag mit montag
> und aftermontag darnach, der Heiligen Dreifeltigkeit, Trinitatis genannt, Johannis des
> taufers, Petri und Pauli der zwölf boten, Jacobi des zwölf boten, Unser Frauen schidung,
> Assumptionis genannt

so will we that these following feasts and holy days be yet kept and held holy, as of old; yet
so that the other days of the remembrance of Christ, and of the right and true saints who have
sure witness in holy Scripture or otherwise of their Christian faith, life, miracles, suffering
and blessed departing, be not taken away nor put in forgetfulness, but let remain unhallowed in
remembrance, as of old. The feasts that shall be kept holy: New Year's day, called the
Circumcision; Twelfth Day, called the Epiphany; Candlemas, called the Purification of Mary;
Matthias the twelve-messenger; the Annunciation, called the Annunciation of Mary; Palm Sunday;
Easter day with the Monday and the Tuesday after; Philip and James the twelve-messengers;
Ascension day, called the Ascension of the Lord; Whitsunday with the Monday and the Tuesday
after; the Holy Trinity, called Trinity; John the Baptist; Peter and Paul the twelve-messengers;
James the twelve-messenger; our Lady's departing, called the Assumption

**Note.** This order makes a second rank of its own: days that are "unhallowed in remembrance"
(*ungefeiret in gedechtnus*). Their memory is kept, but they are not holy days.

**Nassau-Dillenburg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1537** (Sehling 10, p. 74). This order keeps Corpus
Christi, as a day to teach the right use of the Sacrament, and keeps the Assumption and the
Nativity of Mary. The summer feasts are moved to Sunday for the harvest (§6.4, §8.9).

The Teschen list is one of the longest in the corpus. **Teschen, *Kirchenordnung*, 1584**
(Sehling 3, p. 461):

<!-- doc 1815 -->
> IX. Welche fest man halten soll. Uber die gemeine sontage sollen gehalten und feierlich
> begangen werden die hohen hauptfest unsers christlichen glaubens, darmitte dieselbe dem
> gemeinen volk mit vleiss vorgetragen und eingebieldet werden. Als nemlichen den tag
> Nativitatis Christi sampt dem anderen und drietten fest, den tag Circumcisionis Domini,
> Epipha., Conversionis Pauli, Purificationis Mariae, Mathiae Apost., Annunciationis Mariae,
> gruen donnerstag, charfreitag, der ostertag sampt dem nachvolgenden und drietten fest,
> Philippi et Jacobi, hiemmelfahrt Christi, pfingsten mit nachfolgenden feiertagen, demnach der
> heiligen dreifaltigkeit, Mariae gebuert, Johannis Baptistae, Petri et Pauli, Visitationis
> Mariae, Mariae Magdalenae, Jacobi Apost., Transfigurationis Domini, Bartholomei Apostoli,
> Decolationis Joannis, Mathei, Michaelis Arch., Simonis et Judae, Andreae Apostoli, Martini et
> Catharinae. An diesen beruerten festen mag man nach gelegenheit der zeit biessweilen alleine
> den vormittag feiern und nachmittag widerum ein jeder seines beruefs und handarbeit warten

IX. Which feasts shall be kept. Over and above the common Sundays shall be kept and solemnly
celebrated the high chief feasts of our Christian faith, that the same may be diligently set
forth and imprinted on the common people: namely the day of the Nativity of Christ with the
second and third feast; the day of the Circumcision of the Lord; the Epiphany; the Conversion of
Paul; the Purification of Mary; Matthias the apostle; the Annunciation of Mary; Maundy Thursday;
Good Friday; Easter day with the following and third feast; Philip and James; the Ascension of
Christ; Whitsuntide with the following holy days; thereafter the holy Trinity; the Nativity of
Mary; John the Baptist; Peter and Paul; the Visitation of Mary; Mary Magdalene; James the
apostle; the Transfiguration of the Lord; Bartholomew the apostle; the Beheading of John;
Matthew; Michael the archangel; Simon and Jude; Andrew the apostle; Martin and Catherine. On
these aforesaid feasts one may, as the season serveth, sometimes keep holy the forenoon only,
and in the afternoon every man attend again to his calling and handiwork

**Note.** Every day in Teschen is formally a "high chief feast". The real ranking is left to the
season: in time of work any of them may be a half day.

**Weißenburg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1528** (Sehling 11, p. 662):

<!-- doc 308 -->
> Die fürnemsten fest und feiertäg aber weren dise: alle sonetag, aller apostl tag, den Cristag
> mit sambt sant Steffans tag und sant Joanes tag dofuer und in einen tag gefeiert, den Neuen
> jaerstag, der heiligen Dreikönigtag, Marie Liechmeß oder Rainigung, Marie Verkündung, Ostern
> und den montag henach, Auffart Christi, Pfingstag und montag hernach, Johannis des taufers,
> Marie Heimsuchung, Marie Himelfahrt, Marie Magdalene, sant Michael und Allerheiligen tag.

But the chiefest feasts and holy days should be these: all Sundays; all the apostles' days;
Christmas, together with St Stephen's day and St John's day, kept holy therefor and in one day;
New Year's day; the Three Kings' day; Mary's Candlemas or Purification; Mary's Annunciation;
Easter and the Monday after; the Ascension of Christ; Whitsunday and the Monday after; John the
Baptist; Mary's Visitation; Mary's Assumption; Mary Magdalene; St Michael; and All Saints' day.

---

## 6. Whole holy days and half holy days

In practice the most important mark of rank was civil: whether a day was a holy day for the
whole of it, for the forenoon only, or not at all. Many orders give the working population this
information in the form of a list of "whole" and "half" days, to be declared from the pulpit on
the Sunday before. The half holy day, kept "until noon" or "until the sermon is ended", is the
Lutheran successor of the late-medieval distinction between *festa fori* and lesser feasts. It
is also the chief place where economic pressure (harvest, markets, craftsmen's work) shaped the
calendar.

### 6.1 The half holy day: "until noon"

The form appears as early as Nicolaus Hausmann's Anhalt order of 1532, with "aller apostel tage
fur mittage" (§5.7). It is then fixed by the Saxon order of 1539 and by Mecklenburg 1552 (both
in §5.2): a sermon in the forenoon, communion if there are communicants, and work after noon.
Other examples:

**Prussia, *Kirchenordnung und Ceremonien*, 1568** (Sehling 4, p. 87):

<!-- doc 1833 -->
> Die aposteltage sollen hinfürder auch, aber allein vormittag gefeiret werden, die pfarrkinder
> der alten wahren apostolischen lehr in den verordneten evangelien, item der heiligen apostel
> ihres glaubens und guten exempels erinnert und berichtet werden […] Wann die predig geschehen
> und das heilige abendmahl (so ferne communikanten sind) gehalten ist, soll jedermann diese
> tage nachmittag zu arbeiten frei sein. Es sollen aber die pfarrherren allezeit den nechsten
> sonntag zuvor die festa und aposteltag verkündigen und anzeigen, auf welchen tag in der wochen
> dieselbigen gefallen und das der aposteltag nicht lenger dann vormittag soll gefeiret werden,
> damit sich ein jeder hausvater habe darnach zu richten etc.

The apostles' days shall henceforth also be kept holy, but only in the forenoon, and the
parishioners be reminded and instructed, in the appointed Gospels, of the old true apostolic
doctrine, and also of the holy apostles' faith and good example […] When the sermon hath been
held and the holy Supper (in so far as there be communicants) celebrated, every man shall be
free to work on these days in the afternoon. But the parsons shall always, on the next Sunday
before, proclaim and shew the feasts and apostles' days, on what day in the week the same fall,
and that the apostles' day shall be kept holy no longer than the forenoon, that every
householder may order himself thereafter, etc.

The city of Osnabrück had sermons five times a week, and so needed "no special holy days".
**Osnabrück (city), *Kirchenordnung*, 1543** (Sehling 7/1, p. 259):

<!-- doc 2099 -->
> Dewyle nu der wecken viffmal geprediket wert baven die sondagesprediken, so bedarve wy nener
> besondergen virdagen, damit men dem gemeinem volke nicht orsake geve to lediggande und in de
> kröge to gan und de tidt unnütlicken tobringen etc. Idt schall ock neen fest edder hillig dag
> geholden werden dann allene, dat man Gades wort predicken höre und Gott bidde und danke vor
> syne genade dorch Christum. De aposteldage sollen gefyret wurden wente to middage.

Now forasmuch as there is preaching five times in the week beside the Sunday sermons, we need no
special holy days, that we give not the common people occasion to go idle, and to go into the
taverns, and to spend the time unprofitably, etc. Neither shall any feast or holy day be kept
save only that men may hear God's Word preached, and pray to God and thank him for his grace
through Christ. The apostles' days shall be kept holy until noon.

In Waldeck the half day extends even to Good Friday. **Waldeck, *Kirchenordnung*, 1556**
(Sehling 9, pp. 272–273):

<!-- doc 2300 -->
> der tag des leidens Christi, so der carfreitag genennet wirdt, soll halb feierlich biß auff
> den mittag gehalttenn werden, des abents aber die sepultura Christi verhandelt werden […]
> Diese vorgeschriebene Feiertage ausserhalb den außgezogenen sollen gantz unnd durchauß
> gefeieret unnd dem Volck allwege des Sontags zuvor verkündiget werden […] S. Petri [und]
> Pauli, S. Jacobi, S. Bartholomei tag sol man vor mittag heylig halten, dem volck eyne predigt
> thun und darnach an ire arbeyt gehen lassen

the day of the Passion of Christ, which is called Good Friday, shall be kept half holy until
noon, but in the evening the burial of Christ shall be handled […] These holy days aforewritten,
beside those excepted, shall be kept holy wholly and throughout, and be proclaimed to the people
always on the Sunday before […] St Peter and Paul's, St James's and St Bartholomew's day one
shall keep holy before noon, make the people a sermon, and thereafter let them go to their work

**Note.** Waldeck's text is printed by the editor with a second recension set into the
apparatus, and the two are not always easy to separate. In the other recension Michaelmas is
kept "fur das virte heuptfest", as the fourth chief feast (p. 272).

### 6.2 Tables of whole and half holy days

From the 1560s the orders of the Harz counties and of the Rhine and Main set out the whole and
half days in formal lists.

**Mansfeld, *Kirchen-agenda*, 1580** (Sehling 2, pp. 239–240):

<!-- doc 1241 -->
> Von gemeinen festen. Weil auch aus ungleichem halten der festage, viel unrichtigkeit
> einfellet, so sollen die pfarherren wissen, welche feiertage in dieser grafschaft ganz, welche
> aber nur halb gehalten werden. Diese nachfolgende fest helt man den ganzen tag durchaus
> feierlich. Den christag, sampt den zweien folgenden tagen, Stephani und Johannis. Den neuen
> jars tag. Den tag der offenbarung Christi, den man den tag der heiligen drei könige nennet.
> Den tag der reinigung Mariae. Den tag der verkündigung Mariae. Den ostertag, mit den zweien
> folgenden tagen. Den tag , der himmelfart Christi. Den tag der heiligen pfingsten, mit den
> zweien nechst folgenden tagen. Den tag Johannis des teufers. Den tag visitationis Mariae. Den
> tag Michaelis. […] Nachfolgende tage werden nur halb gefeiret. Der tag S. Andreae. Der tag des
> apostels S. Thomae. Der tag der bekerung S. Pauli. Der tag des apostels Matthiae. Der grüne
> donnerstag. Der charfreitag. Der tag Philippi Jacobi. Der tag Petri und Pauli. S. Jacobus tag.
> S. Laurentii tag. Der tag Bartholomei. Der tag Matthei. Der tag Simonis und Judae.

Of common feasts. Forasmuch also as much disorder ariseth from the unequal keeping of the feast
days, the parsons shall know which holy days in this county are kept wholly, and which but half.
These feasts following are kept holy the whole day throughout: Christmas day, together with the
two days following, Stephen and John; New Year's day; the day of the manifestation of Christ,
which is called the day of the Three Kings; the day of the Purification of Mary; the day of the
Annunciation of Mary; Easter day, with the two days following; the day of the Ascension of
Christ; the day of holy Whitsuntide, with the two days next following; the day of John the
Baptist; the day of the Visitation of Mary; the day of Michael. […] The days following are kept
but half holy: the day of St Andrew; the day of the apostle St Thomas; the day of the conversion
of St Paul; the day of the apostle Matthias; Maundy Thursday; Good Friday; the day of Philip and
James; the day of Peter and Paul; St James's day; St Laurence's day; the day of Bartholomew; the
day of Matthew; the day of Simon and Jude.

At Wintzingerode the whole days (the same list as Mansfeld) are graded again by sermons.
**Wintzingerode (Gericht Bodenstein), *Kirchen-Ordnung***
(Sehling 2, p. 256; after the Hohnstein order; undated):

<!-- doc 1243 -->
> Und soll auf einen jeden tag zweimal geprediget und von allerhand arbeit denselben ganzen tag
> stille gehalten werden. Auf den dritten tag der treien hauptfeste als auf Johannistag, in den
> heiligen weinachten, auf den oster und pfingst dinstag soll nur ein mal gepredigt werden.
> Nachfolgende tage werden nur halb gefeiret: Der tag des apostels S. Andreae. Der tag des
> apostels S. Thomae. Der tag der bekerung S. Pauli. Der tag des apostels S. Matthiae. Der grüne
> donnerstag. Der charfreitag. Der tag Philippi Jacobi. Der tag Petri und Pauli. Der tag Mariae
> Magdalenae. Der tag S. Jacobi. Der tag Bartholomaei. Der tag Mathaei. Der tag Simonis, und
> Judae. […] Es sollen auch allezeit die fest und aposteltage den sontag zuvor, darmit sich das
> volk darnach zu richten, vorkündiget und abgesaget, darneben vormeldet, welche ganz und welche
> halb solen gefeiret werden.

And on every such day there shall be preaching twice, and rest kept from all manner of work the
same whole day. On the third day of the three chief feasts, as on St John's day in holy
Christmas, and on the Easter and Whitsun Tuesday, there shall be preaching but once. The days
following are kept but half holy: the day of the apostle St Andrew; the day of the apostle St
Thomas; the day of the conversion of St Paul; the day of the apostle St Matthias; Maundy
Thursday; Good Friday; the day of Philip and James; the day of Peter and Paul; the day of Mary
Magdalene; the day of St James; the day of Bartholomew; the day of Matthew; the day of Simon and
Jude. […] Also the feasts and apostles' days shall always be proclaimed and bidden on the Sunday
before, that the people may order themselves thereafter, and withal it shall be declared which
shall be kept wholly and which half.

In Pfalz-Zweibrücken the duchy's *Statthalter* and councillors clarify the church order of 1557,
which had been read in different ways. **Pfalz-Zweibrücken, *Feiertagsordnung*, 1561**
(Sehling 18, pp. 297–298):

<!-- doc 973 -->
> Nemlich allso, daß hinfurther nachvollgennde Feyrtag fur hohe fest zuhallten, die gleich den
> Sonntagen vor- unnd nachmittag sollend gefeyret werdenn, als: […] 1. Nativitatis, daß ist der
> Christag. 2. Circumcisionis, die beschneidung Christi. 3. Epiphaniae, daß ist der hailigen
> drei König tag. 4. Purificationis, daß ist der tag der opfferung Christi. 5. Annuntiationis,
> der tag, da Christus inn der Jungkfrawen leib empfangen ist wordenn. 6. Caene Domini, der Grun
> Donnerstag. 7. Passionis Domini, der Charfreittag. 8. Ascensionis Domini, daß ist die
> Himmelfarth Christi. Waß aber die uberigen gemaine feyrtag, alsda seindt der Apostell tag,
> Nemlich Sanct Mathias, Philippi Jacobi, Petri unnd Pauli, […] Andree und Thomastag, item S.
> Johannstag, Visitationis Marie, S. Michelstag […] und die zween vollgennde nach dem Christag,
> Ostern unnd Pfingsten belanngt, an denen soll hinfurther allain morgents vor mittag Gottes
> Wortt verkhundigt werdenn. Doch dergestallt, daß ann solchen tagen vor mittag bei straff zehen
> gulden niemandts erlaubt seie, felldt […] oder anndere arbaitt anzufahen, auch sein Kram oder
> laden nit auffzuthun oder dergleichen ettwas zuhandtieren, die Predig seie dann beschehen.
> Nach gethaner Predig aber soll niemandt zufeyren gezwungen, sonnder allßdann jederman
> zuarbaitten erlaubt sein.

Namely thus: that henceforth the holy days following are to be kept for high feasts, which shall
be kept holy like the Sundays before and after noon, to wit: […] 1. The Nativity, that is,
Christmas day. 2. The Circumcision, the circumcising of Christ. 3. The Epiphany, that is, the
holy Three Kings' day. 4. The Purification, that is, the day of the offering of Christ. 5. The
Annunciation, the day whereon Christ was conceived in the Virgin's womb. 6. The Supper of the
Lord, Maundy Thursday. 7. The Passion of the Lord, Good Friday. 8. The Ascension of the Lord,
that is, Christ's going up to heaven. But as touching the other common holy days, which are the
apostles' days, namely St Matthias, Philip and James, Peter and Paul, […] Andrew's and Thomas's
day, item St John's day, the Visitation of Mary, St Michael's day […] and the two following
after Christmas, Easter and Whitsuntide: on these God's Word shall henceforth be proclaimed only
in the morning before noon. Yet in such wise that on such days before noon no man be suffered,
on pain of ten gulden, to begin field-work or other work, nor to open his booth or shop, or to
handle any such thing, until the sermon be done. But after the sermon is done, no man shall be
compelled to keep holy, but every man shall then be free to work.

**Note.** Easter and Whitsunday fall on Sundays and are not listed. The second and third days of
Christmas, Easter and Whitsuntide are half days here. So are John the Baptist, the Visitation
and Michaelmas, which Mecklenburg had kept whole or nearly so.

In the Landau ordinance the "whole" days are only four, and they are called high feasts of
Christ. **Landau, *Feiertagsordnung* of the town council, 1562** (Sehling 19/1, p. 61):

<!-- doc 1044 -->
> Und seind das die hohen fest des herrn Christi, so gleich dem sontag durchauß gefeirt werden
> sollen: Erstlich der tag nativitatis oder geburt Christi sampt dem andern volgenden tag. Der
> tag circumcisionis oder der beschneidung Christi. Der tag epiphaniae, das ist der erscheinung
> oder offenbarung Christi, den man nennet der h[eiligen] drey könig tag. Jede montag nach
> ostern unnd pfingsten. Andere feiertag, so allein biß auff mittag gehalten werdenn sollen: Der
> tag der opferung Christi, genant purificationis Mariae. Der tag annunciationis Mariae oder
> conceptionis Christi. Der tag caenae domini, der grün durstag genent, daran von des hernn
> abentmal gepredigt wirdt. […] Der tag passionis domini oder des leidens unnd sterbens Christi,
> der karfreitag genent. Der tag ascensionis oder himelfarth Christi. Item die tag der aposteln.
> Der tag S[anct] Johannis des teuffers. Der tag visitationis Mariae. Der tag Michaelis von
> wegen der leer von den heiligen engeln. Unnd an solchen apostel- und feiertagen auserhalb der
> sontag unnd hohen fest hierbeiermelt mag ein jeder burger nach mittag sein geschefft unnd
> arbeit seiner gelegenheit nach wol verrichtenn.

And these are the high feasts of the Lord Christ which shall be kept holy throughout like the
Sunday: first, the day of the Nativity or birth of Christ, with the second day following; the
day of the Circumcision of Christ; the day of the Epiphany, that is, of the appearing or
manifestation of Christ, which is called the holy Three Kings' day; each Monday after Easter and
Whitsuntide. Other holy days, which shall be kept only until noon: the day of the offering of
Christ, called the Purification of Mary; the day of the Annunciation of Mary or Conception of
Christ; the day of the Lord's Supper, called Maundy Thursday, whereon is preached of the Lord's
Supper; […] the day of the Passion of the Lord, or of the suffering and death of Christ, called
Good Friday; the day of the Ascension of Christ; item the days of the apostles; the day of St
John the Baptist; the day of the Visitation of Mary; the day of Michael, because of the doctrine
of the holy angels. And on such apostles' and holy days, beside the Sundays and the high feasts
here mentioned, every burgher may well do his business and work after noon, as it serveth him.

**Note.** In Landau even the Ascension is a half day. The high feasts that fall on weekdays,
Christmas, New Year and Epiphany, are whole days. So are the Mondays of Easter and Whitsuntide.

The Pfalz-Veldenz chapter is headed "[X.] Ordnung der Feirtage. [1. Halbe und ganze Feiertage]".
It gives a reason for keeping the half days. **Pfalz-Veldenz, *Kirchenordnung*, 1574**
(Sehling 18, p. 518):

<!-- doc 1011 -->
> so ist doch nit gerathen, die gewohneten Feirtag zu endern oder abzustellen. Sintemahl sie
> gemeiner kürchen dohin dienstlich, das sie bestimbte zeitt wiß und verursacht werde, die
> Predig und Administration der Sacramenten zu besuchen, dieweil sonnst (wie die erfahrung gibt)
> das volckh inn den wochenpredigten gar saumhafft unnd fahrlessig ist, wann ihnen erlaubt zu
> arbeitten. Auch ist ohne das nöttig, das man im jahr etliche gemeine Bettag halte für das
> gantze Volckh, darzu die verordnete halbe Feyrtage am aller bequembsten sein mögen, do sie
> ohne Superstition gehalten werden, so wehre auch nit nutzlich, dise tag wider anfangen gantz
> zu feyren, dann (wie wir sehen) ist der Mißbrauch der Feyrtag sehr groß, gar wenig besuchen
> Mittags […] Predigen, werden vilmehr mit […] dantzen, spilen, zechen und anndern
> leichtfertigen dingen zubracht. Derohalben, wölche tag bißanhero gantz oder halb gefeyrt
> worden, sollen noch also hifürter nach diser Ordnung gehalten werden, dann hierin unnötig, das
> volckh abermahl irrig zu machen.

yet it is not advisable to change or abolish the accustomed holy days, since they serve the
common Church to this end, that it may know appointed times, and be moved to resort to the
sermon and the administration of the sacraments; for otherwise (as experience sheweth) the
people are very slack and negligent at the week-day sermons, when they are suffered to work.
Besides, it is needful that certain common days of prayer be kept in the year for the whole
people, for which the appointed half holy days may be most convenient, if they be kept without
superstition. Nor were it profitable to begin again to keep these days wholly; for (as we see)
the abuse of holy days is very great: very few resort to the midday sermons, but they are rather
spent in dancing, playing, carousing and other light things. Therefore the days which hitherto
have been kept wholly or half shall be kept so henceforth according to this order; for herein it
is needless to lead the people astray once more.

The list itself makes the half days into days of prayer. **Pfalz-Veldenz, *Kirchenordnung*,
1574** (Sehling 18, p. 519):

<!-- doc 1011 -->
> Item die zwen folgende tag nach dem Christag, zwen nach Ostern und zwen nach Pfingsten. An
> disen tagen soll man allein vor Mittag predigen unnd, so Communicanten do seindt nach der
> Predig, soll man die Letaney singen oder lesen, dann dise tag sollen für gemeine Bettage dem
> Volckh verkhündiget und gehaltten werden.

Item the two days following after Christmas, two after Easter and two after Whitsuntide. On
these days one shall preach before noon only, and, if there be communicants, after the sermon
one shall sing or read the Litany; for these days shall be proclaimed and kept for common days
of prayer for the people.

**Note.** The whole days here are Christmas, Circumcision, Epiphany, Purification, Annunciation,
Maundy Thursday and Ascension. The half days are the apostles, Good Friday, John the Baptist,
the Visitation, Michaelmas, and the second and third days of the three great feasts. Like
Waldeck 1556 (§6.1), this order ranks Good Friday below Maundy Thursday.

The Ysenburg-Ronneburg list grades each day separately, by sermons, forenoon and afternoon.
**Ysenburg-Ronneburg, *Agende*, 1582** (Sehling 10, p. 583):

<!-- doc 218 -->
> Festum Circumcisionis Christi […] welchs sonst der new jahrs [tag] genänt, soll ganz gefeyert
> undt gehalten werden. Epiphania Domini oder Trium Regum soll auch vor unndt nach mittag mit
> einer predig gefeyert werden. Purificationis Mariae soll denn halben tag gefeyert werdenn.
> Dies Matthiae Apostoli auch nur ein halben tag. Annunciationis Mariae soll den halben tag
> gehalten werdenn. Nota: Weil dieses fest bisweilenn in die karwochenn gefelt, sols alsdann
> umbgangen werdenn. Grundonnerstag undt der charfreittag soll ein iglicher nur vor mittag
> gehaltenn werdenn. Der ostertag unndt ostermontag sollen beyde vor undt nach mittag mit
> predigten versehen werden, der osterdinstag nur vor mittag.

The feast of the Circumcision of Christ […] which is otherwise called New Year's day, shall be
kept holy and held wholly. The Epiphany of the Lord, or the Three Kings, shall also be kept holy
before and after noon with a sermon. The Purification of Mary shall be kept holy the half day.
The day of Matthias the apostle also but half a day. The Annunciation of Mary shall be kept the
half day. Note: because this feast sometimes falleth in Holy Week, it shall then be passed over.
Maundy Thursday and Good Friday shall each be kept before noon only. Easter day and Easter
Monday shall both be furnished with sermons before and after noon; Easter Tuesday before noon
only.

The list continues with the summer days, which are fitted to the harvest and to the market at
Gelnhausen. **Ysenburg-Ronneburg, *Agende*, 1582** (Sehling 10, p. 583):

<!-- doc 218 -->
> Der tag Johannis Baptistae soll wegen des marktags zu Gelnhausenn nur vor mittag mit einer
> predigt celebrirt werdenn. […] Petri unndt Pauli unndt der tag Visitationis Mariae fallen
> gemeiniglich in einer wochenn undt zur erndezeit, soll alsdann under den beydenn der am
> gelegestenn mit einer predigt vor mittag gehalten werdenn. Bartholomaei, Matthaei, Michaelis,
> Simonis Judae, S. Tomas, diese sollenn nur vor mittag gehaltenn werdenn. Der christtag unndt
> S. Stephans tag soll vor unndt nach mittag mit predigten verrichtet werden. S. Johanns tag
> aber, des evangelistenn, mag nur mit einer predigt vor mittag beschlossenn werdenn.

The day of John the Baptist, because of the market day at Gelnhausen, shall be celebrated only
before noon with a sermon. […] Peter and Paul and the day of the Visitation of Mary commonly
fall in one week and in harvest-time; then of the two, the one that is most convenient shall be
kept with a sermon before noon. Bartholomew, Matthew, Michael, Simon and Jude, St Thomas, these
shall be kept only before noon. Christmas day and St Stephen's day shall be performed with
sermons before and after noon. But St John the Evangelist's day may be concluded with one sermon
only before noon.

The Nassau-Weilburg text of 1576 lists twelve feasts, and in 1618 a sentence was added to divide
them. **Nassau-Weilburg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1576, with the *Agende* of 1618**
(Sehling 10, p. 228):

<!-- doc 198 -->
> Diese Feyertage, so zur gedächtnuß der wolthaten unsers Herrn Jhesu Christi verordenet seind,
> werden mit Gesang, Predigen unnd Communion gleich den gemeinen Sontagen gehalten, allein daß
> die Introitus, Sequentz und andere Geseng de Tempore für die andern gemeine Gesenge gebraucht
> werden. Der Aposteln tage aber sollen alle mit einer Predigt gehalten werden.

These holy days, which are ordained for the remembrance of the benefits of our Lord Jesus
Christ, are kept with chant, sermons and communion like the common Sundays; only that the
introits, sequences and other chants *de tempore* are used instead of the other common chants.
But the apostles' days shall all be kept with one sermon.

The 1618 *Agende* reads instead. **Nassau-Weilburg, *Agende*, 1618**
(Sehling 10, p. 228; in the editor's apparatus):

<!-- doc 198 -->
> den gemeinen Sontagen gleich und gantz feyrlich celebrirt. Der dritte Tag aber zu
> Weihenachten, Ostern und Pfingsten sampt dem Tag Visitationis Mariae, S. Iohannis Baptistae
> wie auch Michaelis und der Heyligen Apostel Tage sollen mit einer Predigt gehalten werden,
> unnd gehet das Volck nach Mittag widerumb zu seiner Arbeit. Damit aber das gemeine Volck sich
> destobaß darnach hab zu richten, so sollen unsere Kirchendiener allewegen den Sontag zuvor von
> der Cantzel verkündigen, was für Fest- und Feyertage dieselbige zukünfftige Wochen einfallen
> und ob die mit einer Predigt halb oder gantz feyerlich nach der Kirchenordnung sollen gehalten
> werden.

celebrated like the common Sundays and wholly solemnly. But the third day at Christmas, Easter
and Whitsuntide, together with the day of the Visitation of Mary, of St John the Baptist, and
also Michaelmas and the holy apostles' days, shall be kept with one sermon, and the people go
again to their work after noon. But that the common people may the better order themselves
thereafter, our ministers shall always on the Sunday before proclaim from the pulpit what feasts
and holy days fall in the same coming week, and whether they shall be kept half with one sermon,
or wholly solemnly, according to the church order.

The Nassau-Weilburg rule in its final form. **Nassau-Weilburg, *Visitationsabschied*, 1609**
(Sehling 10, p. 352):

<!-- doc 199 -->
> Von feiertagen undt wochenpredigten 1. Wir wollen unndt verordnen, daß hienfuro die gantze
> feyertage mit zwo predigten unndt andern göttlichen ubungen, der dritte tag in den weyhnacht-,
> oster- unndt pfingstfeyertagen mit einer predigt, wie auch der tag Johannis Baptistae,
> visitationis Mariae unndt Michaelis gleich den aposteltagen, halb feyerlich gehalten werden
> unndt die leute nach mittag, wann der gottesdienst verrichtet, ahn ihre arbeit gehen. […] 3.
> Wo uff den gründonnerstags, qui dies institutionis caenae dominicae est, daß nachtmal deß
> herrn zue celebriren gewönlich, darbey laßen wir es verpleiben, sonsten soll derselbige sampt
> dem charfreytag nur halb feyerlich gehalten werden

Of holy days and week-day sermons. 1. We will and ordain that henceforth the whole holy days be
kept with two sermons and other divine exercises; the third day in the Christmas, Easter and
Whitsun holy days with one sermon; as also the day of John the Baptist, of the Visitation of
Mary, and of Michael, like the apostles' days, half solemnly; and that the people go to their
work after noon, when the divine service is performed. […] 3. Where it is wont to celebrate the
Lord's Supper on Maundy Thursday, *which is the day of the institution of the Lord's Supper*, we
let it remain so; otherwise the same, together with Good Friday, shall be kept but half solemnly

Ritzebüttel has a Low German list in two columns. **Ritzebüttel, *Kirchenordnung*, 1556**
(Sehling 5, p. 559):

<!-- doc 1963 -->
> Die virdage overst, an welckeren unse gadesdienst in der kercken geholden wert, sin desse na
> beschrevene und sint den ganzen dag virlich. Alle sondage. Die ersten twe dage […] in den
> winachten Nihe jars dag. Die ersten 2 dage in den h. pfingsten. Drie könig dag. Item […]
> Johannis Baptistae dag. Marien berggang. S. Michaelen dae;. lichtmessen dag. Marien redeschop.
> Die ersten 2 dage in der heil, paschen. Der herren hemmelfart. De na vortekenden dage werden
> virlick geholden up den halven dag edder bet to middage umb der predige willen, also: Alle
> apostel dage. Dingstag in den paschen. Pingesten. Marien Magdalenen dag. Pauli bekehrunge dag.
> Aller hilligen dag.

But the holy days whereon our divine service is held in the church are these hereafter written,
and are holy the whole day: all Sundays; the first two days in Christmas; New Year's day; the
first two days in holy Whitsuntide; the Three Kings' day; item St John the Baptist's day; Mary's
going over the hills; St Michael's day; Candlemas day; Mary's message; the first two days in
holy Easter; the Lord's Ascension. The days hereafter noted are kept holy for the half day, or
until noon, for the sermon's sake, thus: all apostles' days; the Tuesday in Easter; [the Tuesday
in] Whitsuntide; Mary Magdalene's day; St Paul's conversion day; All Saints' day.

**Solms-Laubach, *Kirchenzuchtordnung*, 1603** (Sehling 9, p. 356):

<!-- doc 2311 -->
> Von den feyertagen, cap. 1 Nachvolgende tage sollen den gantzen tag feyerlich gehalten und an
> denselben keine ausserliche, grobe arbeit verrichtet, sondern mit predigen, singen, beten,
> lesen und betrachtung gottliches worts zubracht werden: Alle sontage, der tag der geburt
> Christi sambt zweien nachvolgenden tagen, der newe jars tag oder circumcisionis, der tag
> epiphaniae […] der tag der opferung Christi im tempel, genant purificationis Mariae, der tag
> annunciationis Mariae oder conceptionis Christi […] der tag coenae Domini […] der tag
> passionis Christi […] der ostertag und uferstehung des hern Christi sambt volgendem montag und
> dinstag, […] der tag ascensionis oder uffhart Christi zum hiemel, der pfingstdag sambt
> volgenden montag und dinstag. Uber das sollen volgende tage vormittage feirlich gehalten und
