> daran geprediget werden: alle aposteltage, sanct Johannis des tauffers tag, visitationis
> Mariae

Of the holy days, chapter 1. The days following shall be kept holy the whole day, and no outward
gross work be done thereon, but they be spent with preaching, singing, praying, reading and
meditating of God's Word: all Sundays; the day of the birth of Christ with two days following;
New Year's day, or the Circumcision; the day of the Epiphany […] the day of the offering of
Christ in the temple, called the Purification of Mary; the day of the Annunciation of Mary or
Conception of Christ […] the day of the Lord's Supper […] the day of the Passion of Christ […]
Easter day and the resurrection of the Lord Christ, with the following Monday and Tuesday, […]
the day of the Ascension, or of Christ's going up to heaven; Whitsunday with the following
Monday and Tuesday. Beside these the days following shall be kept holy in the forenoon, and
preaching held thereon: all apostles' days, St John the Baptist's day, the Visitation of Mary

**Leiningen-Hardenburg, *Kirchenordnungsmandat* for Dürkheim, 1584**
(Sehling 19/1, pp. 277–278):

<!-- doc 1076 -->
> Nachdem in unserer kirchenordnung versehen, das man alle Son- und hohen Festag, als den
> heyligen Oster-, Pfingst und Cristag sambt dem negst nachvolgenden tag, deßgleichen auch den
> neuen jars tag, der heyligen drei könig tag, Marie Lichtmeß, Marie verkundigung und Himelfart
> Cristi […] den gantzen tag durchaus feyren und heyligen, auch vor- und nachmittag gottes wort
> verkundigen und predigen soll […] Sovil aber der heyligen apostel tag anbelangt, die sollen
> vormittag mit verkhundigung gottliches worts gehalten und gefeirt werden, aber nach volendter
> predig mag ein jeder in sein arbait dretten.

Whereas it is provided in our church order that all Sundays and high feast days, as the holy
Easter, Whitsun and Christmas day, together with the next day following, likewise also New
Year's day, the holy Three Kings' day, Mary's Candlemas, Mary's Annunciation and the Ascension
of Christ […] shall be kept holy and hallowed the whole day throughout, and God's Word be
proclaimed and preached before and after noon […] But as touching the holy apostles' days, these
shall be kept and hallowed in the forenoon with the proclaiming of God's Word; but after the
sermon is ended every man may go to his work.

In the city of Osnabrück a local day of penance stands among the half days. **Osnabrück (city),
*Kirchenordnung*, 1613** (Sehling 7/1, p. 267):

<!-- doc 2100 -->
> Folgende tag werden nur mit einer predigt halb gefeyret: 1. Johannis evangelistae tag. 2. Der
> tag Mariae reinigung. 3. Der tag Matthiae apostoli. 4. Der eilfte tag Martii, an welchem eine
> bußpredigt wegen deß anno 1613 am 11. Martii erlittenen brandschadens gehalten wird. Item
> Osterd. und Pfingstdinstag. 5. Der tag der verkündigung Mariae. 6. Der tag Philippi und
> Jacobi. 7. Der tag Johannis baptistae. 8. Der tag Petri und Pauli. 9. Der tag der heimsuchung
> Mariae. 10. Der tag Jacobi apostoli. 11. Der tag Bartholomaei, welcher ein tag der danksagung
> ist vor die eingeerndte frücht. 12. Der tag Matthaei. 13. Der tag Simonis und Judae. 14. Der
> tag Andreae.

The days following are kept but half holy, with one sermon: 1. St John the Evangelist's day. 2.
The day of the Purification of Mary. 3. The day of Matthias the apostle. 4. The eleventh day of
March, whereon a sermon of repentance is held because of the damage by fire suffered on the 11th
of March in the year 1613. Item Easter Tuesday and Whitsun Tuesday. 5. The day of the
Annunciation of Mary. 6. The day of Philip and James. 7. The day of John the Baptist. 8. The day
of Peter and Paul. 9. The day of the Visitation of Mary. 10. The day of James the apostle. 11.
The day of Bartholomew, which is a day of thanksgiving for the fruits gathered in. 12. The day
of Matthew. 13. The day of Simon and Jude. 14. The day of Andrew.

**Note.** In Osnabrück St Bartholomew's day, not Michaelmas, is the harvest thanksgiving. The
sentence before this list puts St Stephen's day among the days "nur mit zweyen predigten ganz
gefeyret", kept wholly but with two sermons only. The editor explains that the chief feasts had
three: the catechism sermon, the chief (Gospel) sermon and the Epistle sermon.

At Tecklenburg the half day is defined by the sermon alone. **Tecklenburg, *Kirchenordnung*,
1543** (Sehling 22, p. 245):

<!-- doc 1505 -->
> Up doße apostel- und andere kleine festdage sal nemant onder dem sermone arbeiden; darvoir
> edder na tho arbeyden, sal vryg staen.

On these apostles' and other small feast days no man shall work during the sermon; to work
before or after it shall be free.

A town's report to the visitors gives the rule in two lines. **Ermsleben, *Kirchen-Ordnung*,
1564** (Sehling 2, p. 486):

<!-- doc 1260 -->
> Alle aposteltage wird gepredigt und vor mittags gefeiret. In den hohen festtagen feieret man
> den ganzen tag, predigt zweimal.

On all apostles' days there is preaching, and holy day is kept before noon. On the high feast
days holy day is kept the whole day, and there is preaching twice.

Brieg is a Silesian order in which the three Marian feasts of Christ, with John the Baptist and
Michaelmas, are whole days. **Brieg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1592** (Sehling 3, p. 445):

<!-- doc 1809 -->
> Die drei Marien-fest, als Annunciationis, Visitationis und Purificationis, item festum
> Johannis Baptae und Michaelis sollen den ganzen tag feierlich, wie sie fallen, gehalten
> werden; die apostelfest aber mit einer predigt, die da früh soll geschehen, doch dass die
> leute können zur kirchen kommen.

The three feasts of Mary, as the Annunciation, the Visitation and the Purification, item the
feast of John the Baptist and of Michael, shall be kept solemnly the whole day, as they fall;
but the apostles' feasts with one sermon, which shall be held early, yet so that the people can
come to church.

### 6.3 Counting the rank in sermons

Many orders measure a day by its number of sermons: three, two, or one. At Penig the second day
of a high feast has two sermons and the last day one.

**Penig, *Kirchenordnung*, 1575** (Sehling 1, p. 634):

<!-- doc 116 -->
> Alle hohe fest geschehen den ersten tag drei predigten zur matin, zum ampt und vesper zeiten,
> folgenden festtag zwo, zum letzten eine.

On all high feasts there are three sermons on the first day, at the times of matins, the office
and vespers; on the following feast day two; on the last, one.

In Henneberg the four chief feasts have two sermons and all other feasts one. The Ascension is
one of the four. **Henneberg, *Kirchenordnung* of Georg Ernst, 1582** (Sehling 2, pp. 312–313):

<!-- doc 1247 -->
> Von den festen. Uber die sontag sollen zu betrachtung der wolthaten Jesu Christi, unsers
> geliebten herrn und heilands, nachfolgende festtage feierlich gehalten, und darauf die
> gewönlichen evangelia mit den ceremonien, wie an den sontagen geordnet, gehandelt werden.
> Erstlich die vier heupt fest, mit zweien predigten, vor und nach mittage, als: Der erst und
> ander christag. Der erst und ander ostertag. Auffartstag. Erst und ander pfingstag.
> Desgleichen auch Circumcisionis. Trium regum. Purificationis oder lichtmess. Zum andern die
> gewöhnliche andere festa, als: Aller aposteltage. Item : Pauli bekehrung. Annunciationis.
> Donnerstag und freitag in der palmwochen. Johannis Baptistae. Visitationis. Mariae Magdalenae.
> Michaelis, von den engeln. Diese alle sollen erstlich auf den sontag zuvor verkündiget,
> darnach wenn und wie sie gefallen, […] nachfolgender gestalt mit einer predigt gehalten
> werden. […] Und sol als denn des folgenden morgends mit den ceremonien, wie auf die gemeine
> sontage, doch one orgeln, gehalten werden. Wann nun solche predigt vor mittage volbracht ist,
> sol ein jeder an seine gebürende arbeit und beruf gehen.

Of the feasts. Over and above the Sundays, for the considering of the benefits of Jesus Christ,
our beloved Lord and Saviour, these feast days following shall be kept solemnly, and thereon the
wonted Gospels handled with the ceremonies as ordained on the Sundays. First, the four chief
feasts, with two sermons, before and after noon, as: the first and second Christmas day; the
first and second Easter day; Ascension day; the first and second Whitsunday. Likewise also the
Circumcision, the Three Kings, the Purification or Candlemas. Secondly the wonted other feasts,
as: all apostles' days; item St Paul's conversion; the Annunciation; Thursday and Friday in Palm
week; John the Baptist; the Visitation; Mary Magdalene; Michael, of the angels. All these shall
first be proclaimed on the Sunday before, and thereafter, when and as they fall, […] be kept in
manner following with one sermon. […] And then on the following morning it shall be kept with
the ceremonies as on the common Sundays, yet without the organs. And when such sermon is
performed before noon, every man shall go to his due work and calling.

**Note.** In Henneberg the Annunciation is a lower-rank feast, but the Circumcision, Epiphany
and Purification go with the chief feasts. Also on the lower feasts the organ is silent.

### 6.4 Harvest, markets and the working year

The rank of the summer feasts was often cut down because of the harvest. The feasts of Peter and
Paul (29 June), the Visitation (2 July), James (25 July), Mary Magdalene (22 July), Laurence (10
August), the Assumption (15 August) and Bartholomew (24 August) are the ones most affected.

The Mediasch synod gives the only technical term for the half day in the corpus, *intercise*.
**Transylvanian Saxons, Mediasch synod, 1578**
(Sehling 24, pp. 427–428; the report printed in the apparatus from Teutsch's *Urkundenbuch*):

<!-- doc 1707 -->
> In hac synodo ex deliberatione pastorum quorundam remota fuerunt duo festa apostolorum Petri
> et Pauli et divi Iacobi propter opera rustica, quae passim in istum mensem incidunt
> disputatoque de celebratione aliorum festorum apostolis dedicatorum conclusum est, ut isti
> apostolici dies intercise, hoc […] est dimidiatim sacri haberentur et post officium matutinum
> sacrum concessum liberumque sit. cuilibet operas faciendi manuarias, dies autem domini nostri
> Iesu Christi ut Annunciationis, Ascensionis, Paschatis, Pentecostes, Nativitatis totaliter.

In this synod, by the deliberation of certain pastors, two feasts of the apostles, of Peter and
Paul and of St James, were removed because of the country works which everywhere fall in that
month; and, it being disputed of the celebration of the other feasts dedicated to the apostles,
it was concluded that these apostolic days should be held holy *intercise*, that is, by halves,
and that after the morning office it be granted and free to every man to do handiwork; but the
days of our Lord Jesus Christ, as the Annunciation, the Ascension, Easter, Pentecost, the
Nativity, wholly.

**Note.** *Intercisi dies* was the Roman calendar's term for days that were holy in the morning
and evening but lawful for business in between.

The Burzenland article is headed *De feriis hebdomadatibus*. **Burzenland (Transylvania),
*Visitationsartikel*, 1578** (Sehling 24, p. 452; German version B in the apparatus):

<!-- doc 1710 -->
> Der wochen gemeiner feuer-täge wegen ist verordnet von der geistlicher universität, daß sie
> irer alle vom cantzel sollen verkündiget werden. Doch sollen sie voriges mißbrauchs wegen nur
> biß auf den mittag gefeyert werden mit handlung des worts Gottes und der heiligen sacramente.
> Denmnach kan ein jeder seiner und gemeiner arbeit auswarten.

Concerning the common holy days of the week, it is ordained by the spiritual university that
they shall all be proclaimed from the pulpit. Yet because of the former abuse they shall be kept
holy only until noon, with the handling of God's Word and the holy sacraments. Thereafter every
man may attend to his own and the common work.

**Note.** The Latin note in the same apparatus records how the article went: it had been
abolished "sic ut nullum divorum festum celebraretur", so that no saint's feast was celebrated,
and was brought back at the synod of Birthälm in 1580.

In Mansfeld the apostles' Gospels in the harvest are moved to the Sunday, but only in the
villages. **Mansfeld, *Kirchen-agenda*, 1580** (Sehling 2, p. 240):

<!-- doc 1241 -->
> Nach dem aber in der ernte zeit die tage Petri Pauli und Jacobi gefallen, und die leute in
> solcher zeit an der arbeit nicht gerne verhindert werden, so können dieselbigen evangelia
> obgetachter tage, auf den dörfern, ein jedes auf nehest volgenden sontag verleget werden. In
> stedten aber kan man sie halten auf den tag, darauf sie gefallen oder komen, wo sie auf einen
> sonnabent kemen, auch auf die nehesten sontag transferirt werden.

But forasmuch as the days of Peter and Paul and of James fall in the harvest-time, and the
people are loath at that time to be hindered at their work, the Gospels of the said days may in
the villages be laid, each one, on the next following Sunday. But in the towns one may keep them
on the day whereon they fall or come; if they come on a Saturday, they may also be transferred
to the next Sunday.

Nassau-Dillenburg moves the whole block of summer feasts to Sunday. **Nassau-Dillenburg,
*Kirchenordnung*, 1537** (Sehling 10, p. 74):

<!-- doc 169 -->
> Sanct Johans des tauffers tag, item sanct Peter unnd Paulus, Maria haimsuchung, visitationis
> genant, sanct Maria Magdalenen tag, sanct Jacobs, des apostels, sanct Laurentius, Maria
> Hymmelfart tag. Diese alle, dieweil sie in der rechten erndten khomen, sollen auff den
> nechsten sonttag nach dem calendertag gehalten werden, sover sie nit auff den sontag gefallen.
> Eß soll auch ein jeglicher pharher auff die sontag, darauff jetztbenante feiertag verlegt,
> nach mittag fur der khinderzucht das evangelion, so auff den tag zuhalten verordent ist,
> predigen.

St John the Baptist's day; item St Peter and Paul; Mary's Visitation, called *visitationis*; St
Mary Magdalene's day; St James the apostle's; St Laurence; the day of Mary's Assumption. All
these, because they come in the very harvest, shall be kept on the next Sunday after the
calendar day, unless they fall on the Sunday. And every parson shall, on the Sundays to which
the said holy days are removed, preach after noon, before the catechising of the children, the
Gospel appointed to be kept for that day.

**Sayn, *Kirchenordnung*, 1590** (Sehling 19/1, p. 389):

<!-- doc 1090 -->
> Der Tag Petri und Pauli. Der Tag visitationis Mariae. Der Tag Iacobi Apostoli. Der Tag
> Bartholomaei. Weil gemeldte Apostel Fest in die Ernde fallen, sollen sie allein vor Mittag
> gefeyret werden.

The day of Peter and Paul. The day of the Visitation of Mary. The day of James the apostle. The
day of Bartholomew. Because the said apostles' feasts fall in the harvest, they shall be kept
holy before noon only.

To avoid too many summer holy days, Veit Dietrich moves the Visitation to 15 August, where it
takes the place of the Assumption. **Nürnberg, Veit Dietrich, *Agendbüchlein*, 1545**
(Sehling 11, p. 538):

<!-- doc 297 -->
> Weil aber gemeinlich das fest Visitationis Marie in ein wochen, mit S.Peter und Paul felt und
> der feiertage umb der feldarbeit willen dazumal zu vil wöllen sein, ists also bedacht, das
> solchs fest Visitationis Marie auf den 15.tag Augusti verschoben wer, da man sonst das fest
> von der Himelfart Marie helt, welchs doch kein grund in der schrift und zu großer abgötterei
> vil ursach geben hat. Darumb soll man auf solchen tag Assumptionis die historien halten von
> der Heimsuchung, welche in der schrift gegründet und derhalb ein nütze lehr geben kan.

But since the feast of the Visitation of Mary commonly falleth in one week with St Peter and
Paul, and the holy days would be too many at that time because of the field-work, it is thus
devised that the same feast of the Visitation of Mary be put off to the fifteenth day of August,
whereon otherwise the feast of the Ascension of Mary is kept, which yet hath no ground in the
Scripture and hath given much occasion to great idolatry. Therefore on that day of the
Assumption one shall hold the history of the Visitation, which is grounded in the Scripture and
can therefore give a profitable doctrine.

**Note.** The same text is repeated in Hohenlohe 1578 (Sehling 15, p. 296), Wolfstein 1574
(Sehling 13, p. 587) and a Schwäbisch Hall draft of 1614 (Sehling 17/1, p. 191).

---

## 7. How many days: the high feasts and their following days

The late-medieval octave, a week-long observance of a great feast, has left almost no trace. Its
place is taken by the "following days" (*nachfolgende Tage*, *Feiertage*) of Christmas, Easter
and Whitsuntide. How many of them are kept, and how fully, is itself a mark of rank.

- **Three days** in all the Bugenhagen orders (§5.4), in Saxony, Mecklenburg and their
  successors (§5.2), and in Regensburg, Thorn, Danzig, the Harz counties and Schwarzburg.
- **Two days** (the feast and the next day) in the Württemberg family (§5.3), in the
  Brandenburg-Nürnberg order of 1533 and its successors (§8.3), and in Kurpfalz.
- **A graded third day**: the third day is still a holy day but with fewer sermons, no
  communion, or no figural music.

### 7.1 Three days

**Amt Grimma, *Generalia der dorfer*, 1529** (Sehling 1, p. 576):

<!-- doc 90 -->
> Die hohe feste sollen allzeit drei tag mit predigen und andern cerimonien ganz feierlich
> gehalten, und in den messen, so communicanten vorhanden, messgewant gebraucht werden.

The high feasts shall always be kept three days, wholly solemnly, with preaching and other
ceremonies, and in the masses, where there are communicants, the mass vestment be used.

**Schwarzburg, *Ordnung der fest und feiertage*, 1587** (Sehling 2, p. 137):

<!-- doc 1230 -->
> Die drei hauptfeste weihenachten, ostern und pfingsten sollen drei tage feierlich gehalten
> werden, die ersten zween tage vor und nachmittage, der dritte aber allein vormittag mit
> predigten und do communicanten vorhanden, sollten alle drei tage das ampt gehalten werden.
> Gleichfalls sollen vor und nachmittag feierlich gehalten und solenniter celebriert werden,
> diese nechst folgende fest, als: Circumcisionis, Trium regum oder Epiphaniae, Purificationis
> […] Adnunciationis […] Mariae Virginis, Visitationis […] Johannis Baptistae, S. Michaelis.

The three chief feasts, Christmas, Easter and Whitsuntide, shall be kept solemnly three days:
the first two days before and after noon, but the third in the forenoon only, with sermons; and
where there be communicants, the office shall be held on all three days. Likewise these feasts
next following shall be kept solemnly before and after noon and celebrated with solemnity, as:
the Circumcision; the Three Kings or Epiphany; the Purification, the Annunciation and the
Visitation of the Virgin Mary; John the Baptist; St Michael.

The St Mary's order at Danzig made the third day lesser, with two early sermons. In the margin a
sixteenth-century hand wrote the contrary. **Danzig, draft *Feiertagsordnung* and St Mary's
order of 1567** (Sehling 4, p. 187):

<!-- doc 1844 -->
> Der dritte festtag soll gleich den andern zween gehalten werden.

The third feast day shall be kept like the other two.

At Regensburg all three days are kept, but the third has neither communion nor the midday
sermon. **Regensburg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1567** (Sehling 13, p. 475):

<!-- doc 450 -->
> An festen Christi, allen und jeden, wird neben den predigten frue auch das abendmahl gehalten
> und derhalben abends zuvor auch die vesper umb der communicanten willen. An Weihnachten,
> Ostern und Pfingsten wird doch das abendmahl nur die ersten zween tage gehalten und den
> dritten tag, als dem erichtag, die mittagspredig in beiden kirchen unterlassen, wie unten
> volget.

On the feasts of Christ, each and every one, the Supper is held beside the sermons in the
morning, and therefore also vespers on the evening before, for the communicants' sake. Yet at
Christmas, Easter and Whitsuntide the Supper is held only the first two days, and on the third
day, being the Tuesday, the midday sermon is left off in both churches, as followeth below.

In Prince Bernhard's Anhalt order the third day is graded by its music. **Anhalt,
*Kirchenordnung* of Prince Bernhard, 1568** (Sehling 2, p. 569):

<!-- doc 1262 -->
> Zu hohen festen soll man zwene heilige tage figural und den dritten tag den lateinischen coral
> und den sequens de tempore singen.

On high feasts one shall sing two holy days in figural music, and on the third day the Latin
plain-song and the sequence *de tempore*.

The Wintzingerode order (§6.2) and Penig 1575 (§6.3) also make the third day lesser, with one
sermon instead of two or three. Nassau-Weilburg 1609 (§6.2) makes it a half holy day.

### 7.2 Two days, and "two only"

Most Upper German orders keep the feast and the next day: "Der Ostertag sampt dem nächsten
darnach" (Württemberg 1553, §5.3). Two orders show the difference plainly.

The pastor of Goldlauter describes his practice in his report to the visitors. **Goldlauter
(Henneberg), parish order, 1566** (Sehling 2, p. 333; printed "Groldlauter"):

<!-- doc 1248 -->
> Also pfleg ich es auch zu halten auf die hohe fest, wie oben stehet, one allein die lieder von
> einem idlichen fest werden gesungen an statt der vorigen und auf weinachten feiern wir drei
> tage, auf ostern und pfingsten nur zwen wie in der agend stehet.

So am I wont to keep it also on the high feasts, as standeth above, save only that the songs of
each feast are sung instead of the former; and at Christmas we keep holy three days, at Easter
and Whitsuntide but two, as standeth in the agenda.

**Henneberg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1582** (§6.3) keeps "Der erst und ander christag. Der erst und
ander ostertag … Erst und ander pfingstag": two days at each feast, and so no third day at
Christmas either.

### 7.3 St Stephen and St John in Christmas week

In the medieval calendar 26 and 27 December were the feasts of St Stephen and St John the
Evangelist, both of high grade. Luther's *Formula missae* keeps "the office of the Nativity" on
those days (§3.1). The orders then choose among three answers:

- **They become the second and third days of Christmas**, with the Christmas Gospel and at most
  a mention of the saint. This is the common Lutheran solution.
- **They are kept as saints' days** within Christmas.
- **They are joined into one day**, as at Weißenburg 1528 (§5.7) and in one recension of Brenz's
  Hall order.

**Schwäbisch Hall, Brenz, *Kirchenordnung*, 1527**, variant B (Sehling 17/1, p. 53):

<!-- doc 755 -->
> B: den Christag mitsampt Sant Stephans tag also, das man Sant Stephans und Sant Johans,
> nachfolgenden tag, fur ein und nit zwen feyrtag halte

B: Christmas day together with St Stephen's day, so that St Stephen's and St John's, the day
following, be kept for one holy day and not two

At Wolfenbüttel the saints are joined to the "festival sermon" of the second and third Christmas
days as themes of their own. **Wolfenbüttel, *Kirchenordnung*, 1543** (Sehling 6/1, p. 62):

<!-- doc 1972 -->
> Am dage Stephani neven der festpredike schal men ock prediken van den diaken edder gemeinen
> kastendenern, darby antheen dat exempel Stephani und Laurentii, darmit den lüden ein
> sorchfeldicheit ingebildet werde vor de armen. Am dage Johannis des evangelisten is tho reden
> neven der festpredike van dem berope edder eschinge eines jeweliken mynschen.

On St Stephen's day, beside the festival sermon, one shall also preach of the deacons or common
chest-servants, and allege thereby the example of Stephen and Laurence, that a carefulness for
the poor may be imprinted on the people. On the day of John the Evangelist one is to speak,
beside the festival sermon, of the calling or vocation of every man.

**Note.**

- **Grubenhagen 1544** adds the Holy Innocents on 28 December as a fourth day of Christmas, kept
  "while the sermon lasteth" (§5.5).
- **Braunschweig 1528** tells the preacher to remember St John after the sermon of the third day
  of Christmas, and St Stephen after the sermon of the second (p. 398).

---

## 8. Individual feasts and where they stand

### 8.1 Purification and Annunciation: Marian names, feasts of Christ

Following Luther ("Festum purificationis et annunciationis pro festis Christi … habemus", §3.1),
almost every order keeps these two days and counts them among the feasts of Christ. The orders
often rename them to make the point:

- "Der tag der offenbarung Christi in dem tempel, so man nennet purificationis Mariae" (Saxony
  1539, §5.2).
- "Der tag der opferung Christi im tempel" (Mecklenburg 1552, §5.2).
- "Annunciationis Mariae oder conceptionis Christi" (Mecklenburg 1552, Sayn 1582 and others).
- "Purificationis Christi et Marie. Annunciacionis Christi" (Hausmann, Anhalt 1532, §5.7).

Their rank is still not fixed:

- **Whole holy days**: in Mecklenburg, Pfalz-Zweibrücken 1561, Mansfeld 1580 and Brieg 1592.
- **Half days**: in Landau 1562 and Ysenburg 1582 (§6.2).
- **Forenoon only**: in Württemberg 1536 (§5.3).

When the Annunciation falls in Holy Week it has to be moved, and the orders' rules for this show
how its rank was understood (§14.2).

### 8.2 The Visitation

The Visitation (2 July) was a late-medieval feast, but its Gospel (Luke 1:39–56, with the
*Magnificat*) made it acceptable. Homberg 1526 (Moderate Reformed) already keeps it (§3.2). Riga
1530 keeps it "because that was the first revelation of Christ, when he was yet in his mother's
womb" (§3.7). Saxony 1539 keeps it "because of that history of the Gospel" (§5.2).

It usually stands in the second rank, beside John the Baptist and Michaelmas. In the Nürnberg
family its date was moved to 15 August, to take the place of the Assumption and to spare the
harvest (§6.4).

### 8.3 The Assumption: abolished, replaced, or kept "for the working peasants"

No feast shows the range of the orders better than the Assumption (15 August). It has no Gospel
history. But in north Germany it was one of the four feasts on which the "four-times" offering
was paid (§9), and elsewhere it was a harvest holiday. The orders deal with it in three ways.

**1. Kept, but preached as the Visitation.**

**Brandenburg-Nürnberg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1533** (Sehling 11, p. 204):

<!-- doc 270 -->
> Unser Frauen tag irer himelfart, nit darumb das der in der heiligen schrift grund hab, sunder
> von des gemainen arbeitenden pauersvolks wegen. Doch soll an solichem fest in den kirchen die
> historien von unser frauen fest Visitationis genant, derhalben man in dem heiligen evangelio
> zeugnus hat, mit singen und lesen gehalten werden

Our Lady's day of her Ascension, not because it hath ground in holy Scripture, but for the sake
of the common labouring country-folk. Yet on that feast the history of our Lady's feast called
the Visitation, whereof there is witness in the holy Gospel, shall be kept in the churches with
singing and reading

Tecklenburg has the same text in Low German, and there the day also counts as one of the four
chief feasts. **Tecklenburg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1543** (Sehling 22, p. 245):

<!-- doc 1505 -->
> Unser leven frouwen hemelvaertdes dag, assumptionis genant, alß ein veerhochtydsfeest, nicht
> darom, dat et in der hilligen schrift grundt hebbe, […] sonder von des gemeinen arbeidenden
> werckvolcks wegen; doch sol an solchen fest in der kercken de historia visitationis Mariae,
> der men in dem evangelio tuighniße hefft, mit singen und lesen geholden werden.

Our dear Lady's Ascension day, called the Assumption, as a feast of the four high times; not
that it hath ground in the holy Scripture, […] but for the sake of the common labouring
work-folk; yet on that feast the history of the Visitation of Mary, whereof there is witness in
the Gospel, shall be kept in the church with singing and reading.

**Note.** The Stift Osnabrück order of 1543 (Sehling 7/1, p. 223) forbids the feast outright.
Its editor notes that some territories kept it as a *Vierzeitenfest* on the Nürnberg model. Veit
Dietrich's *Agendbüchlein* of 1545 moves the Visitation itself to 15 August (§6.4).

**2. Abolished, and Michaelmas made the fourth chief feast in its place.** This is the northern
solution: Mecklenburg 1552 (§5.2), Hoya 1571 and Harlingerland 1573/74 (§5.5), Lippe 1571 and
Oldenburg 1573 (§9). The Mecklenburg visitors of 1557 insisted on it.

**Mecklenburg, *Visitations-Instruction*, 1557** (Sehling 5, p. 224):

<!-- doc 1925 -->
> Auch wirt angezeigt, wie es mit den festen oder feiertagen hinfurder uber das jar
> eintregtiglich soll gehalten werden, nemlich das uber die fest Cristi allein der apostel tage
> sollen gefiert werden, wie in der kirchenordenung nach der leng ausgedruckt und insonderheit
> das man das fest Assumptionis Marie hinfurder nicht halten noch dulden will, sondern den tag
> Michaelis an desselben stadt ein vierzeiten fest sein soll.

It is also shewed how it shall be kept henceforth with one accord through the year with the
feasts or holy days: namely that, beside the feasts of Christ, only the apostles' days shall be
kept holy, as is set out at length in the church order; and in especial that the feast of the
Assumption of Mary shall henceforth be neither kept nor suffered, but that the day of Michael
shall be a feast of the four times in its stead.

**3. Simply kept.** A number of orders keep the Assumption as a holy day:

- the conservative lists of Brandenburg 1540, Pfalz-Neuburg 1543, Hausmann 1532 and Weißenburg
  1528 (§5.7);
- Nassau-Dillenburg 1537, among the harvest feasts moved to Sunday (§6.4);
- the Danish-Holstein order of 1542, "Festa Ascensionis" (§5.4).

### 8.4 The other Marian feasts

The Conception (8 December), Nativity (8 September) and Presentation (21 November) of Mary have
no Gospel history. Most orders drop them in silence. A few name them in order to abolish them,
among them Sayn 1582, "Sonderlich aber festum conceptionis, nativitatis, praesentationis,
assumptionis Mariae", and Regensburg 1567 (§3.7). A few keep one or more:

| Order | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Anhalt, Hausmann, 1532 (§5.7) | "Visitacionis, conceptionis Marie", Assumption and Nativity all kept |
| Nassau-Dillenburg 1537 (Sehling 10, pp. 74–75) | Assumption and "Mariae geburt, nativitatis genant" kept |
| Brandenburg 1540 (§5.7) | Assumption and Nativity kept |
| Wied 1564 (Reformed; §4.5) | Nativity of Mary a sermon day ("tantum fiet concio") |
| Anhalt 1568 (§4.5) | Visitation and Nativity of Mary kept *ferialiter*, with their Gospels, not as holy days |
| Teschen 1584 (§5.7) | "Mariae gebuert" among the holy days |

### 8.5 Michaelmas: the angels and the harvest

Michaelmas (29 September) rises highest of all the saints' days. Most orders give the day three
themes, as Saxony 1539 does: "daran von den heiligen engeln zu predigen":

- the doctrine of the holy angels;
- thanksgiving for the harvest;
- in the north, the fourth payment of the four-times offering (§9).

**Schleswig-Holstein, *Kirchenordnung*, 1542** (Sehling 23, p. 95):

<!-- doc 1576 -->
> De dach Michaelis ys eine gemene dancksegginge vor alle fruchte, de wy des Jars gesamlet und
> entfangen hebben. Darumme schal de gantze vorsamlinge vorth na der predigen singen tho der
> dancksegginge mit groter andacht: Te Deum Laudamus etc. Averst den gantzen dach aver, so wol
> des morges alse des namiddages, schal men Predigen van den Engeln, up dat yderman vorsta, wat
> gudes wy van Gade dorch eren denst entfangen hebben, dat wy ock Gade darvor dancken mögen.

The day of Michael is a common thanksgiving for all fruits which we have gathered and received
in the year. Therefore the whole congregation shall straightway after the sermon sing for the
thanksgiving, with great devotion, *Te Deum laudamus*, etc. But the whole day through, as well
in the morning as in the afternoon, one shall preach of the angels, that every man may
understand what good we have received from God through their service, that we also may thank God
therefor.

In Braunschweig the *Te Deum* is sung before the Gospel. **Braunschweig, *Kirchenordnung*,
1528** (Sehling 6/1, p. 397):

<!-- doc 1983 -->
> Denne schal eyn predicante, wen de epistole gelesen is, vor deme evangelio vormanen dat volk,
> Gade to danken vor alle woldät unde besondergen vor de früchte disses jares, de he uns gegeven
> hefft intosamelen […] schal he singen laten Te Deum etc., ehr me dat evangelion lest.

Then shall a preacher, when the epistle is read, before the Gospel, exhort the people to thank
God for all benefits, and especially for the fruits of this year which he hath given us to
gather in […] he shall let *Te Deum* be sung, etc., before the Gospel is read.

**Note.** Michaelmas's rank still varies from place to place:

- **A chief feast**: the fourth *Vierzeitenfest* in Braunschweig, Hamburg, Lübeck, Grubenhagen,
  Mecklenburg, Hoya, Lippe, Oldenburg and Waldeck.
- **Preached "before and after noon"**: in Mecklenburg, where it formally stands in the second
  rank.
- **A whole day with Te Deum**: in the Brunswick orders.
- **A half day**: in Pfalz-Zweibrücken 1561 (§6.2) and Nassau-Weilburg 1609.
- **A sermon day only**: in Wied 1564 (Reformed; §4.5).
- **A "small feast", though with three sermons**: in Danzig 1612 (§5.6).

### 8.6 John the Baptist

The Nativity of John the Baptist (24 June) is kept by nearly every order, usually in the second
rank. The reason given is either the Gospel history of his birth or the office of preaching: "zu
ehren dem heiligen predigampt des evangelii von Christo" (Saxony 1539, Merseburg 1544). The
Pomeranian order of 1535 counts him with the feasts of Christ: "dat fest hört ock up Christum"
(§5.4). The Wolfenbüttel order of 1543 gives his day a polemical theme.

**Wolfenbüttel, *Kirchenordnung*, 1543** (Sehling 6/1, pp. 62–63):

<!-- doc 1972 -->
> Am dage Johannis Baptistae schal men prediken van dem uthwendigen denste des göttliken wordes
> wedder de wedderdöpere, welcke […] up dem sproke stan: Se werden alle van Gade geleret werden.
> Darmede se alle scholen wüste maken.

On the day of John the Baptist one shall preach of the outward ministry of the divine Word
against the Anabaptists, who […] stand upon the saying, They shall be all taught of God;
wherewith they would lay all the schools waste.

### 8.7 Trinity

The feast of the Trinity falls on a Sunday. It is usually listed at the end of the feasts of
Christ with the note that it "gefellet auf den nehesten sontag nach pfingsten" (Saxony 1539,
Mecklenburg 1552). Its rank is shown in four ways:

- **The Kyrie.** It shares the *Kyrie summum* with Pentecost at Nördlingen and Buxtehude (§4.2).
- **The Latin Mass.** It is one of the "Hogen Festen Christi" with a Latin Mass in
  Schleswig-Holstein 1542 (§10.2).
- **The Athanasian Creed.** It is read on the day and its vigil in the Thüngen lessons of 1587.
- **Solemnity.** It is kept "hochfeyerlich" in Verden 1606.

The Brandenburg-Ansbach chapter order of 1533 notes only that its office "ist rain" (§4.4).

### 8.8 Mary Magdalene, the Conversion of Paul, the Beheading of John: kept for their Gospels

Three days that are not apostles' days are often ranked with them, because they have a story in
the New Testament. These are the Conversion of Paul (25 January), Mary Magdalene (22 July, Luke
7:36–50) and the Beheading of John the Baptist (29 August). Where they are not holy days, the
orders keep their Gospels on the nearest weekday or Sunday.

**Mecklenburg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1552** (Sehling 5, p. 201):

<!-- doc 1922 -->
> Wenn solche heilige tage gefallen, die man nicht pfleget zu feiren, und doch ire historien im
> evangelio beschrieben sind, als da ist der tag Conversionis Pauli, Marie Magdalene, S.
> Johannis entheubtung etc. mögen die prediger den selben text und historien an einem solchen
> werktage, nemlich, mitwoch oder freitage, der solchem heiligen tag am nehesten ist, in der
> predigt lesen und handlen.

When such holy days fall as are not wont to be kept holy, and yet their histories are written in
the Gospel, as is the day of the Conversion of Paul, Mary Magdalene, St John's beheading, etc.,
the preachers may read and handle the same text and histories in the sermon on such working day,
namely Wednesday or Friday, as is next to such holy day.

Solms-Laubach gives a reason for keeping the Beheading of John. **Solms-Laubach,
*Kirchenzuchtordnung*, 1603** (Sehling 9, p. 356):

<!-- doc 2311 -->
> Der tag Johannis enthauptung oder [decollatio Johannis] mag uf ein gemeinen wochenpredigttag
> (dieweil derselb eine herliche histori und zeugnus hat, wie die weldt frommen predigern pflegt
> zulohnen) verlegt werden, wie dan auch das evan[…]gelium von Marien Magdalenen, dieweil es
> eine nutzliche und tröstliche historiam inhelt.

The day of John's beheading may be laid on a common week-day sermon day (since the same hath a
glorious history and witness how the world is wont to reward godly preachers), as also the
Gospel of Mary Magdalene, since it containeth a profitable and comfortable history.

In Prussia Mary Magdalene's Gospel is given a fixed Sunday. **Prussia, *Kirchenordnung und
Ceremonien*, 1568** (Sehling 4, p. 87):

<!-- doc 1833 -->
> Das evangelium Mariä Magdalenä Luc. 7 soll ordinarie und hinfort jährlich Dominica septima
> nach Trinitatis gelesen und gepredigt werden

The Gospel of Mary Magdalene, Luke 7, shall ordinarily and henceforth yearly be read and
preached on the seventh Sunday after Trinity

### 8.9 Corpus Christi, the Holy Cross, local and new feasts

**Corpus Christi.** Most orders abolish it, and some order it to be explained so that people
will know why it has gone.

**Oldenburg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1573** (Sehling 7/2.1, p. 1096):

<!-- doc 2150 -->
> Das fest corporis Christi sol vermüge Gottes worts abgetan sein und sollen die prediger den
> Sontag zuvor das volk erinnern, was für ein grosser misbrauch und abgötterey damit getrieben
> worden, deren sich ein jeder Christ nach dem ernstlichen befehl Gottes enthalten sol.

The feast of Corpus Christi shall be done away according to God's Word, and the preachers shall
on the Sunday before remind the people what great abuse and idolatry hath been practised
therewith, which every Christian ought to shun according to the earnest commandment of God.

Two early orders keep it: Northeim for civic reasons, and Nassau-Dillenburg to teach the right
use of the Sacrament.

**Northeim, *Kirchenordnung*, 1539** (Sehling 6/2, p. 935):

<!-- doc 2047 -->
> Des Herrn waren leichnams tag hat man umb sonderlicher politischer, das ist bürgerlicher
> ursach willen müssen bleiben lassen. Aber doch also, das die processio sampt allen andern
> unchristlichen ceremonien abgeschafft und allein Gottes wort und das nachtmal des Herrn zu
> handeln und halten gestatet werden sol.

The day of the Lord's true Body men have had to let stand for special political, that is, civil
causes; yet so that the procession, with all other unchristian ceremonies, be done away, and
only God's Word and the Lord's Supper be suffered to be handled and held thereon.

**Nassau-Dillenburg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1537** (Sehling 10, p. 74):

<!-- doc 169 -->
> Unnsers herren leichnams tag, corporis Christi genant, soll darumb werden behalten, das man
> daran von dem rechten gebrauch unnd frucht des hohen hailigen sacraments des leibs unnd bluts
> Christi richtig lere unnd predige. Es soll aber das sacrament weder an dem noch an keinem
> anndern tagk im jar außgestelt noch umbgetragen werden

Our Lord's Body day, called Corpus Christi, shall be retained to this end, that one may rightly
teach and preach thereon of the right use and fruit of the high holy Sacrament of the body and
blood of Christ. But the Sacrament shall be neither set out nor carried about, on that nor on
any other day in the year

**The Holy Cross.** Luther's "Festa S. Crucis anathema sunto" (§3.1) is echoed in Sayn 1582,
"die beiden creutz tage, inventionis und exaltationis crucis" (§5.5). No order keeps them.

**Local and civic feasts.** Some towns kept days of their own, and the orders treat them in
different ways:

- **Braunschweig.** St Autor had been honoured with a civic procession under a vow. It was
  turned into alms and a Sunday thanksgiving (Sehling 6/1, p. 398).
- **Wied 1564** (Reformed). Patronal feasts are kept with a sermon (§4.5).
- **New days.** Some towns added days of their own: a day of penance for the Osnabrück fire of
  1613 (§6.2); a thanksgiving in Nördlingen for the town's deliverance from a planned surprise
  attack in 1440 (Sehling 12, pp. 387–388); and in Regensburg a dedication festival in October
  for the beginning of evangelical preaching in 1542 (Sehling 13, p. 475).

**A new feast of Christ.** Bugenhagen proposed a feast the medieval calendar did not have.

**Braunschweig, *Kirchenordnung*, 1528** (Sehling 6/1, p. 380):

<!-- doc 1983 -->
> Id is wunder, dat me nicht hefft gehat eyn fest van der döpe Christi, dewile me doch so vele
> unnutte feste hefft gehat, to vorderve lives unde der selen. […] Dat also de
> Vastelavendessundach by uns moge heten dat fest der döpe Christi, me mach id denne nömen, wo
> me wil.

It is a wonder that there hath been no feast of the baptism of Christ, seeing there have been so
many unprofitable feasts, to the destruction of body and soul. […] So that the Shrovetide Sunday
may with us be called the feast of the baptism of Christ; one may then name it as one will.

**Note.** Wolfenbüttel 1543 keeps this "feast of the baptism" on the Sunday before Lent, with
the Gospel of Matthew 3 preached "den ganzen dach över", all day long (Sehling 6/1, p. 62).

---

## 9. The *Vierzeiten*: the four chief feasts and the four offerings

In north and central Germany the highest rank of feasts had a name that came from church
finance, not liturgy. These were the *vier Zeiten* (Low German *veer tide*), or the four
*Hochzeiten*. They were the four feasts on which the "four-times penny" (*Vierzeitenpfennig*,
*veertide pennink*) or offering was paid to the pastor or the church chest. Three of the four
were fixed: Christmas, Easter and Whitsuntide. The fourth varied by region, as the editors'
notes show. It was most often the Assumption (so at Minden, Sehling 21, p. 138). The Bergedorf
order names St John the Baptist's day instead
(Sehling 5, p. 389, cited by the Buxtehude editor, Sehling 7/1, p. 79). At St John's in
Halberstadt the penny was collected quarterly, on John the Baptist's day, Michaelmas, and the
Tuesdays of Christmas and Easter (Sehling 2, p. 488).

The Lutheran orders kept the payment, and with it the rank. Most of them replaced the Assumption
with Michaelmas, which fell in the same season (after the harvest) and had a lawful Gospel theme
(the angels). The *Vierzeiten* are the Lutheran north's equivalent of the *summa festa*. A
*Vierzeitenfest* is a whole holy day, often with first vespers and a sermon on the eve, and with
a Latin Mass.

### 9.1 The four times named

Bugenhagen's Lübeck order describes the treasure-chest. **Lübeck, *Kirchenordnung* (Bugenhagen),
1531** (Sehling 5, p. 362):

<!-- doc 1949 -->
> In desse caste schal kamen de veer tide pennink ut allen caspelen. De veer tide sint: paschen,
> pinxten, michaelis, winachten. Denne schal ein grot becken stan in allen kerken up dem altare
> vor dem chore. Twe kerkvedere schölen dar acht up hebben und nicht verne dar van sin , und
> alle lüde van twelf iaren und dar baven iunk und olt, schölen dar bringen den tide pennink.

Into this chest shall come the four-times penny out of all the parishes. The four times are
Easter, Whitsuntide, Michaelmas, Christmas. Then shall a great basin stand in all churches on
the altar before the choir. Two churchwardens shall have an eye thereon and not be far from it;
and all folk of twelve years and above, young and old, shall bring thither the times-penny.

A pre-Reformation police ordinance of Waldeck names the four *Hochzeiten* first among the days
on which attendance at Mass is enforced. **Waldeck, *Landordnung*, 1525** (Sehling 9, p. 179):

<!-- doc 2300 -->
> Auch setzen, ordiniren und wollen wir, ernstlich gebietthende, das ein ider unßer undersaße,
> so zu seinen mundigen jharen khommen, uff die vier hochzeittliche feste und sonntage, auch uff
> alle unßer lieben frawen- und apostelltage in seiner pfarkirchen das ampt der heiligen meß und
> das heilige evangelium von ihrem pfarherrn heeren sollen

We also set, ordain and will, earnestly commanding, that every one of our subjects that is come
to his years of discretion shall, on the four high-tide feasts and Sundays, and also on all our
Lady's and apostles' days, hear in his parish church the office of the holy mass and the holy
Gospel from their parson

The Pomeranian order sets out the due itself. **Pomerania, *Kerckenordeninge*, 1569**
(Sehling 4, p. 414):

<!-- doc 1862 -->
> De veertiden pennink, alle quartal van jeder person bauen 12. jar, ein veerken, schal gegeven
> werden, entwedder dem pastori edder der kercken, wo idt jeders ordes gebrücklick.

The four-times penny, every quarter, from every person above twelve years, a farthing, shall be
given either to the pastor or to the church, as is the custom in every place.

The same dues are found in the lower Rhineland, in the Erasmian Catholic order of Jülich-Cleves,
where they are called the "four high-tide offerings". **Jülich-Cleves-Berg,
*Visitationsinstruktion*, 1559** (Roman Catholic humanist; Sehling 21, p. 87):

<!-- doc 1425 -->
> yedoch das einem yeden die vier hochzeitoffer unnd -broder, wie hiebevor verordent, gegeben
> werden

yet so that to every one the four high-tide offerings and loaves be given, as heretofore
ordained

### 9.2 Michaelmas in the Assumption's place

The replacement was made by Braunschweig 1528 ("Item Michaelis vor eyn vehrtidenvest", §5.4) and
Hamburg 1529. It became standard through Mecklenburg 1552 (§5.2) and the visitation of 1557
(§8.3). The later orders write it into their feast lists.

**Lippe, *Kirchenordnung*, 1571** (Sehling 21, pp. 391–392):

<!-- doc 1462 -->
> Der tag Michaelis, welcher an statt des Festes Assumptionis Mariae in unsern Kirchen als ein
> viergezeiten Fest feirlich gehalten […] und darauff die notwendige tröstliche Lere von den
> lieben heiligen Engeln der Gemeine Gottes fürgetragen und gehandelt wirdt. Auff diesen Festen
> sol es mit Ordnung der Lectionen, Gesengen, Predigten und Communion gehalten werden wie an den
> gemeinen Sontagen, Allein, das die Introitus, Sequentz und auff den Viergezeiten Festagen die
> Praefationes de tempore, Item die Teutsche Alte Festliedlein, wie oben ermelt und bey uns
> gewöntlich, gesungen werden.

The day of Michael, which in the stead of the feast of the Assumption of Mary is kept solemnly
in our churches as a feast of the four times […] and whereon the needful comfortable doctrine of
the dear holy angels is set forth and handled to the congregation of God. On these feasts it
shall be done, as touching the order of the lessons, chants, sermons and communion, as on the
common Sundays; only that the introits and sequences, and on the four-times feast days the
prefaces *de tempore*, item the old German feast-songs, as above mentioned and customary with
us, be sung.

**Note.** Lippe ranks the *Vierzeiten* above the other feasts of Christ by one liturgical mark,
the proper preface. The same order calls them "die herrliche, schöne, heilige, fürnembste
viergezeiten Fest", the glorious, fair, holy, chiefest four-times feasts (p. 392).

**Oldenburg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1573** (Sehling 7/2.1, pp. 1096–1097):

<!-- doc 2150 -->
> Der tag Michaelis, welcher anstadt des fests […] assumtionis oder himelfart Mariae als ein
> viergezeitenfest gehalten, an welchem die lere von den heiligen engeln dem volk sol
> fürgehalten werden. Desgleichen sol auch an demselbigen fest nach verlesener epistel ein
> gemeine danksagung geschehen für die empfangene früchten und darzu das Te Deum laudamus
> gesungen werden.

The day of Michael, which is kept as a feast of the four times in the stead of the feast […] of
the Assumption or Ascension of Mary, whereon the doctrine of the holy angels shall be set forth
to the people. Likewise also on the same feast, after the epistle is read, a common thanksgiving
shall be made for the fruits received, and the *Te Deum laudamus* be sung thereto.

In the Harlingerland order the payment is the reason given for Michaelmas taking the place.
**Harlingerland, *Kirchenordnung*, 1573/74** (Sehling 7/1, p. 735):

<!-- doc 2122 -->
> Ordnen derohalben, daß der [!] letzste und vierte hochfesttagzeit solle S. Michaelis tag sein.
> Und sollen die kaspelßleute dem pastorn am selben tag öhre opfer, gebuhrlichen pflicht und
> landheur guedtlichen entrichten und ohne hinderung dorgeben und bezahlen.

We ordain therefore that the last and fourth high feast-tide shall be St Michael's day. And the
parishioners shall on the same day render kindly to the pastor their offering, due duty and
land-rent, and give and pay it without hindrance.

### 9.3 The *Vierzeiten* as a liturgical rank

At Wolfenbüttel a sermon at first vespers is allowed only on the four. The same words stand in
Hildesheim 1544 (Sehling 7/2.1, p. 858). **Wolfenbüttel, *Kirchenordnung*, 1543**
(Sehling 6/1, p. 62):

<!-- doc 1972 -->
> In den grösten festen edder veer tyden: Wynachten, Paschen, Pinxsten und Michaelis mach men
> thor ersten vesper eine stunde prediken. In den andern festen is dat nicht van nöden.

On the greatest feasts, or four times, Christmas, Easter, Whitsuntide and Michaelmas, one may
preach for an hour at the first vespers. On the other feasts it is not needful.

Hoya has the Mass in Latin throughout. **Hoya, *Kirchenordnung*, 1571** (Sehling 6/2, p. 1151):

<!-- doc 2065 -->
> VI. Von den vierzeitenfesten. Auf den vierzeitenfesten sol man das meßampt in unsern kirchen,
> da man es haben kan, durchaus latine, mit dem introitu, Kyrie eleyson, gloria in excelsis,
> collecten, episteln, sequentien, evangelien etc. halten, doch das die gewönlichen deudsche
> psalm, welche auf die fest verordnet (damit auch die kirche singe), nicht vergessen werden.

VI. Of the four-times feasts. On the four-times feasts one shall hold the office of the mass in
our churches, where it can be had, throughout in Latin, with the introit, Kyrie eleison, *Gloria
in excelsis*, collects, epistles, sequences, Gospels, etc.; yet so that the wonted German psalms
which are appointed for the feasts (that the church also may sing) be not forgotten.

In Lauenburg the *hohe Vierzeitenfeste* are the days for the alms collection, and the meal the
pastor used to give the churchwardens on those days is abolished. **Lauenburg, *Kirchenordnung*,
1585** (Sehling 5, p. 421):

<!-- doc 1953 -->
> Zum vierzehenden sollen sie alle sontage und auf den feiertagen und hohen vierzeiten festen
> die bete von den leuten unter der predigt fleissig samlen, aufschreiben und beilegen […] Und
> letzlich sol genzlich abgeschaffet sein auf den hohen vierzeiten festen die collation und
> frass, welchen der pastor bis daher den kirchgeschworen auf solchen festen hat geben müssen.

Fourteenthly, they shall on all Sundays, and on the holy days and the high four-times feasts,
diligently gather the collection from the people during the sermon, write it down and lay it by
[…] And lastly there shall be wholly done away on the high four-times feasts the collation and
feasting which the pastor hath hitherto had to give the churchwardens on such feasts.

In the Neumark the churchwardens are to take the collection plate round on the high feasts
generally, not only on the four. **Neumark (Brandenburg), instruction of Margrave Johann for the
visitors, 1540** (Sehling 3, p. 36):

<!-- doc 1745 -->
> Dieselben gottesleute sollen auch alle hohe feier feste, nicht allein die vier zeiten mit der
> tafel umbgehen in der kirchen

The same churchwardens shall also go about in the church with the plate on all high holy feasts,
not only on the four times

**Note.** Grubenhagen 1544 defines its first rank of feasts by this payment ("die vier
hauptfest, an welchen man den vierzeitpenning sammelt", §5.5). Henneberg 1582 has four chief
feasts, but they are Christmas, Easter, Ascension and Whitsuntide (§6.3). It shows that "four
chief feasts" could be understood liturgically as well as fiscally.

---

## 10. Keeping the feast in church: ceremonial marks, abolished ceremonies and the seasons

Beyond whole and half holy days, the orders mark the higher feasts with a set of ceremonial
additions. None of them is new. Each carries on, in reduced form, a feature of the medieval
feast grades. Listed from the commonest to the rarest:

| Mark | Higher feasts | Lower feasts and ordinary days | Examples |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sermons | two or three, sometimes four; a sermon at first vespers | one sermon, in the forenoon | §6.3; Wolfenbüttel 1543 (§9.3); Danzig (§5.6) |
| Latin chant | introit, *Gloria*, Alleluia, sequence, *Patrem*, preface, Sanctus, *Agnus Dei* in Latin | German hymns | §10.1 |
| Kyrie grade | *summum*, *paschale*, *festivale* | *dominicale*, *angelicum*, *apostolicum*, *feriale* | §3.2, §10.2 |
| Proper preface | preface of the feast (*de tempore*, *solemnis*) | common preface, exhortation, or none | §10.3 |
| Sequence | kept on the great feasts | dropped | §10.4 |
| Figural music and organ | figural music, organ | plain-song or German; no organ | §10.5 |
| Vestments, elevation | mass vestment, elevation | choir robe or none | §10.6 |
| Bells | more bells, chiming (*beiern*) the day before | one bell | §10.7 |
| Creed and *Te Deum* | *Athanasianum*, Latin *Te Deum* | — | §10.8 |
| Communion | communion and first vespers with confession | no communion on apostles' days | §10.9 |
| Civil marks | no weddings, markets or guild feasts | — | §14.3 |

### 10.1 Latin chant on the high feasts

The Saxon *Unterricht der Visitatoren* of 1528 set the pattern. Latin is kept for the higher
feasts "because it is unseemly always to sing one song".

**Saxony (Ernestine), *Unterricht der Visitatoren*, 1528** (Sehling 1, pp. 165 and 169):

<!-- doc 9 -->
> An hohen festen, als christag, ostern, auffart, pfingsten, oder der gleichen, were gut, das
> zur messe etliche lateinische gesenge, die der schrift gemes, gebrauchet würden. Denn es ist
> ein ungestalt, imerdar ein gesang singen. […] Mit den festen sol es auch fridlich gehalten
> werden, also, das wo etliche schlechte feier abgangen sind, das man davon nicht viel zanks
> mache. Dieweil es auch ein ungestalt ist, das die gesang gar gleich sind an allen festen, were
> gut, das man an den herrlichsten festen sünge, die lateinische introitus, gloria in excelsis
> deo, halleluia, die reinen sequenz, sanctus, agnus dei.

On high feasts, as Christmas, Easter, the Ascension, Whitsuntide, or the like, it were good that
certain Latin chants, agreeable to Scripture, were used at the mass. For it is an unseemly thing
evermore to sing one song. […] With the feasts also it shall be done peaceably: that where
certain plain holy days have fallen away, men make not much strife thereof. And forasmuch as it
is also unseemly that the chants be all alike on all feasts, it were good that on the most
glorious feasts there were sung the Latin introits, *Gloria in excelsis Deo*, Alleluia, the pure
sequences, Sanctus, *Agnus Dei*.

In Ernestine Saxony the rule depends on the place as well as the day. **Saxony (Ernestine),
*Gemeine Verordnung*, 1533** (Sehling 1, p. 192):

<!-- doc 14 -->
> In steten und flecken da knabenschulen und leut sind, die lateinisch versteen, mag man an
> hohen festen lateinisch, sonst deutsch mess halten, auf den dorfern aber do niemands
> lateinisch versteet, soll man durch aus deutsch mess halten […] Zu merken, wen man gleich an
> hohen festen die mess lateinisch heldet, so liset man dennoch die epistel, das evangelion, die
> wort des sacraments, das vater unser alles deutsch und laut

In towns and market towns, where there are boys' schools and folk that understand Latin, one may
hold mass in Latin on high feasts, otherwise in German; but in the villages, where no man
understandeth Latin, one shall hold mass in German throughout […] Note: even when mass is held
in Latin on high feasts, yet the epistle, the Gospel, the words of the Sacrament and the Lord's
Prayer are all read in German and aloud

In Schleswig-Holstein the high feasts are named, and Trinity is one of them.
**Schleswig-Holstein, *Kirchenordnung*, 1542** (Sehling 23, p. 91):

<!-- doc 1576 -->
> In den Hogen Festen Christi, alse dar sint Winachten, Paschen, Pingesten und Trinitatis, schal
> men yn den steden singen up latin de Introitus, Gloria yn Excelsis, Alleluia mit reinen
> sequentien, Item latinische prefatien, Welcker de Prester also anhevet: Dominus vobiscum etc.,
> Sursum Corda etc., Darna Sanctus, thom lesten Pater noster Mit den wörden des Avendmals, doch
> de stedes yn düdescher sprake

On the high feasts of Christ, which are Christmas, Easter, Whitsuntide and Trinity, one shall
sing in the towns in Latin the introits, *Gloria in excelsis*, Alleluia with pure sequences;
item Latin prefaces, which the priest beginneth thus: *Dominus vobiscum*, etc., *Sursum corda*,
etc.; thereafter the Sanctus; lastly the *Pater noster* with the words of the Supper, yet these
always in the German tongue

At Buxtehude the *Gloria* follows the rank. **Buxtehude, *Kirchenordnung*, 1552**
(Sehling 7/1, p. 74):

<!-- doc 2082 -->
> Volgende schall gesungen werden dat Kyrie eleison na gelegenheit, Gloria in excelsis unde dat
> [Et] in terra, in den hogen festen stedes lattinisch, upp den Sondagen und sunst dudesch edder
> latinisch, wo idt de tydt liden will.

Next shall be sung the Kyrie eleison as occasion serveth, *Gloria in excelsis* and the *Et in
terra*: on the high feasts always in Latin, on the Sundays and otherwise in German or in Latin,
as the time will allow.

Thorn has the reverse rule. On Sundays the chants before the sermon are in Latin, but the small
feasts are wholly German. **Thorn, *Kirchenordnung*, 1575** (Sehling 4, p. 238):

<!-- doc 1844 -->
> Sonsten die gesänge, collecten und lectiones für der predigt werden alles lateinisch gehalten.
> An den aposteltagen und anderen kleinen festen, die man mit halber feier zu halten pfleget,
> wird das ganze officium deutsch gehalten, beide in der alten und neuenstadt

Otherwise the chants, collects and lessons before the sermon are all held in Latin. On the
apostles' days and other small feasts, which are wont to be kept with half solemnity, the whole
office is held in German, both in the Old and the New Town

### 10.2 Chant on the apostles' days

The lower feasts have their own marks in several orders. They are enough to set them off from
working days without matching Sundays.

The Pomeranian *Agenda* gives the rule for the apostles' days. **Pomerania, *Agenda*, 1569**
(Sehling 4, p. 469):

<!-- doc 1865 -->
> Dise navolgenden aposteldage schal men viren vor middage. Des sondages to vörne schölen se de
> prediger affkündigen, jederman vormanen, dat he an disen dagen tor kercke kame, des avendes to
> vörne schal men festivaliter lüden edder beieren, unde vesper holden, des morgens desgeliken
> festivaliter lüden, to 7. edder to 8. predigen, jedoch neen testament utdele, doch mach men
> vor dem sermone dat amt vor dem altare holden, gloria, collecta, epistel unde evangelium cum
> tono lesen, up dat tüschen disen dagen unde gemenen werkeldagen underscheed si. Wennt in der
> kercke ut is, geit ein jeder to siner arbeit.

These following apostles' days one shall keep holy before noon. On the Sunday before, the
preachers shall bid them and exhort every man to come to church on these days. On the evening
before one shall ring or chime *festivaliter*, and hold vespers; in the morning likewise ring
*festivaliter*, preach at seven or eight, yet deal out no testament [no communion]; but one may
hold the office before the altar before the sermon, read the *Gloria*, collect, epistle and
Gospel with the tone, that there may be a difference between these days and the common working
days. When church is out, every man goeth to his work.

The Aschersleben agenda sets out the apostles' day Mass and vespers. **Aschersleben,
*Kirchen-Agenda*, 1575** (Sehling 2, p. 480):

<!-- doc 1260 -->
> Von der vesper an der apostel abend. Die vesper soll gehalten werden wie am sonnabende, doch
> das man de festo singe und auch die collecten de festo lese. Von der messe an der apostel
> tage. 1. Nach dem geleute, welches geschehen soll, wie an einem werkeltage, hebt der cantor
> alspalt mit dem introitu an. 2. Darauf folget das kyrie apostolicum. 3. Nochmals wird das
> teutsche „Allein gott in der höhe“ etc. gesungen. 4. Collecta de festo apostoli, darauf die
> epistel gelesen. […] Von der vesper an der apostel tage. Die vesper wird gesungen ohne den
> organisten wie sonsten am werktage, dieweil keine predigt gehalten.

Of vespers on the apostle's eve. Vespers shall be held as on the Saturday, yet so that one sing
*de festo* and read the collects *de festo* also. Of the mass on the apostles' days. 1. After
the ringing, which shall be done as on a working day, the cantor straightway beginneth the
introit.
2. Thereupon followeth the *Kyrie apostolicum*. 3. Then is sung the German "Allein Gott in der
   Höh", etc. 4. The collect of the feast of the apostle, and thereafter the epistle is read.
   […] Of vespers on the apostles' days. Vespers are sung without the organist, as otherwise on
   a working day, since no sermon is held.

The Riga order gives the melody of the Kyrie. **Riga, *Kirchenordnung*, 1530**
(Sehling 5, p. 15):

<!-- doc 1897 -->
> Auf den introit, singt man das kyrie eleison, mit wenig noten (ausgenommen auf die hohen fest,
> da man notam paschalem nemen mag)

After the introit one singeth the Kyrie eleison with few notes (save on the high feasts, when
one may take the paschal note)

### 10.3 Proper prefaces

Proper prefaces belonged only to the great feasts, and the orders use them to mark those days.

The same text is in Hamburg 1529 (Sehling 5, p. 528). **Braunschweig, *Kirchenordnung*, 1528**
(Sehling 6/1, p. 441):

<!-- doc 1983 -->
> Darnä keret he sick tome altare unde in den groten festen, de sunderge prefatien hebben, unde
> sus, wen he wil, up etlike Sundage mit der prefatie trinitatis (de wedder de Arrhianer alse
> ock dat symbolum nicenum gemaket is), schal he anheven latinisch de prefatie Dominus vobiscum
> unde singen se bet an dat ende, darup singe dat chör eyn latinisch Sanctus.

Thereafter he turneth him to the altar, and on the great feasts which have special prefaces, and
else, when he will, on certain Sundays with the preface of the Trinity (which was made against
the Arians, as was the Nicene Creed also), he shall begin in Latin the preface *Dominus
vobiscum* and sing it to the end; whereupon let the choir sing a Latin Sanctus.

In Ottheinrich's order the preface itself has two grades. **Pfalz-Neuburg, *Kirchenordnung* of
Ottheinrich, second printing 1547** (Sehling 14, p. 109):

<!-- doc 473 -->
> Es seind aber auf etliche hohe fest, Osterfest, Auffart, Pfingsten, Trinitatis etc., besondere
> prefation, von idem fest zwo, ein solemnis und eine dominicalis, die mag ein priester
> außnotiren oder außschreiben und auf dem pult alwegen neben der kirchenordnung haben

But for certain high feasts, the feast of Easter, the Ascension, Whitsuntide, Trinity, etc.,
there are special prefaces, two for each feast, a *solemnis* and a *dominicalis*; these a priest
may note out or write out, and have always on the desk beside the church order

**Note.** The *solemnis* and *dominicalis* tones of the preface are the melodic grades of the
medieval preface: the festal tone for the feast itself, the ferial or Sunday tone for the days
within its season. The order prints the Trinity preface as its example.

At Hatzkerode there is an exhortation on ordinary days and the preface on the great feasts.
**Hatzkerode (Harzgerode, Anhalt), *Kirchenordnunge*, 1534(?)** (Sehling 2, p. 587):

<!-- doc 1263 -->
> Als dan sol sich der priester umb keren und die exhortatio, wie die in der agende stehet, inen
> vorlesen. Auf den grossen festen aber sol an des stat die prefatio de festo gesungen werden
> mit dem gewonlichen sanctus latinisch.

Then shall the priest turn him about and read unto them the exhortation, as it standeth in the
agenda. But on the great feasts, in the stead thereof, the preface of the feast shall be sung,
with the wonted Sanctus in Latin.

**Thorn, *Kirchenordnung*, 1575** (Sehling 4, p. 237):

<!-- doc 1844 -->
> Nach der predigt folget ein deutsch lied, darauf die priester in hohen festen die praefation
> de tempore auf gewöhnliche noten singen, aber in gemeinen sonntagen und feiertagen ohne
> dieselbige bald nach dem deutschen gesange sich gegen dem volke wendet

After the sermon followeth a German hymn; thereupon the priests on high feasts sing the preface
*de tempore* to the wonted notes; but on the common Sundays and holy days [the priest], without
the same, turneth to the people straightway after the German hymn

Kurland allows German proper prefaces for the high feasts, "for variety". **Kurland,
*Kirchenordnung*, 1570** (Sehling 5, p. 89):

<!-- doc 1907 -->
> Dar man aber auf die hohe fest zur verendrung (wie an dem fürstlichen hofe geschicht) diese
> nachverzeichnete und notirte praefationes für die quotidian singen wil, sol einem jedern
> kirchendiener frei sein

But where one will sing on the high feasts, for a change (as is done at the prince's court),
these prefaces hereafter set down and noted, instead of the daily one, it shall be free to every
minister

**Note.** Saxony 1539 prints Latin prefaces for Christmas, Epiphany, Easter, Ascension,
Pentecost and Trinity, and a *Praefatio quotidiana* for all other days
(Sehling 1, p. 274, editor's summary). The Mecklenburg family allows "prefationes de tempore" on
the feasts of Christ (§5.2). Lippe 1571 restricts them to the *Vierzeiten* (§9.2).

### 10.4 Sequences

In Schleswig-Holstein sequences are dropped except on the three great feasts, and each great
feast keeps its own sequence. **Schleswig-Holstein, *Kirchenordnung*, 1542**
(Sehling 23, p. 90):

<!-- doc 1576 -->
> De Sequentien unde prosen scholen alle underlaten unde nicht gesungen werden, uthgenamen yn
> dren groten Festen Christi, alse van Wynachten wente tho Lichtmissen: Grates nunc omnes, mit
> synem düdeschen gesange, Van Pa[…]sehen wente tho Pingesten: Victime Paschali, mit synem
> düdeschen gesange, Den yn den Pingesten: Veni, Sancte Spiritus, mit synem düdeschen gesange.

The sequences and proses shall all be left off and not be sung, save on the three great feasts
of Christ: as from Christmas unto Candlemas, *Grates nunc omnes*, with its German song; from
Easter unto Whitsuntide, *Victimae paschali*, with its German song; then in Whitsuntide, *Veni,
Sancte Spiritus*, with its German song.

**Note.**

- **Seasons, not days.** The sequence is kept through the whole season of each great feast, from
  Christmas to Candlemas and from Easter to Whitsuntide.
- **Aschersleben 1575.** The cantor "die sontage nach diesen festen die sequentien in der
  kirchen singen" (p. 479).
- **The Mecklenburg family.** Sequences are "de tempore" on the feasts of Christ (§5.2).

### 10.5 Figural music and the organ

**Hamburg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1556** (Sehling 5, p. 551):

<!-- doc 1963 -->
> Ock schal de figuralgesank in den vornemblikesten festen ordentlick in einem carspel sowol als
> in den andern gewachtet und gesungen werden.

Also the figural song shall on the chiefest feasts be orderly observed and sung in one parish as
well as in the others.

The Hof order gives the title of its collection of polyphonic music in the table of contents;
the collection itself begins on p. 456. **Hof, *Ordo ecclesiasticus*, 1592**
(Sehling 11, p. 477):

<!-- doc 294 -->
> XVI. Ordo suavissimarum et optimarum ex prastantissimis artificibus tum veteribus tum
> recentibus selectarum cantionum, quae in figurali musica cantore duce decantantur ad praecipua
> festa totius anni directus

XVI. An order of the sweetest and best songs, chosen out of the most excellent masters both old
and new, which are sung in figural music under the leading of the cantor, directed to the chief
feasts of the whole year

In the Pomeranian *Agenda* there is no figural music or organ in Rogation week, except on the
Ascension and its vigil. **Pomerania, *Agenda*, 1569** (Sehling 4, p. 466):

<!-- doc 1865 -->
> Up disen sondach unde in der weke schal men kenen figural sang singen, ock nicht orglen,
> utgenamen den dach Ascensionis domini, mit der in vigilia

On this Sunday and in the week one shall sing no figural song, nor play the organ, save the day
of the Ascension of the Lord, with its vigil

**Other examples:**

- **Anhalt 1568**: figural music on the first two days of the high feasts, Latin chorale on the
  third (§7.1).
- **Regensburg 1567**: figural music at the vespers before, and the communion on, the feasts of
  Christ (p. 475).
- **Henneberg 1582**: lower feasts are kept "one orgeln" (§6.3).
- **Schwarzburg 1587**: the organ is used on the apostles' days in the forenoon
  (Sehling 2, p. 137).
- **Aschersleben 1575**: the vespers of apostles' days are sung "ohne den organisten" (§10.2).

### 10.6 Vestments and elevation

In Prince Bernhard's Anhalt order the elevation of the Sacrament is kept only on the high
feasts. **Anhalt, *Kirchenordnung* of Prince Bernhard, 1568** (Sehling 2, p. 570):

<!-- doc 1262 -->
> Weil die elevation des hochwirdigen sacraments des waren leibes und blutes Christi in einer
> kirchen gefallen, in der andern aber nicht, so sollen die hern kirchendiener in hohen festen
> eleviren, ausserhalb denselben ist hochgedachter fürst Bernhart zu frieden, das es nach
> pleibe, desgleichen soll des sontags zu allen dreien predigten der chorrock gebraucht , die
> wochen uber auch nachgelassen werden.

Forasmuch as the elevation of the most worthy Sacrament of the true body and blood of Christ
hath fallen away in one church but not in another, the ministers shall elevate on high feasts;
beyond these the said Prince Bernhard is content that it be left off. Likewise on Sunday the
choir robe shall be used at all three sermons, and through the week it may be left off.

**Note.**

- **Grimma 1529.** The mass vestment is required on the three-day high feasts (§7.1).
- **Querfurt.** According to Sehling's account of the visitation, mass vestments were worn at
  communion and, for a time, in the pulpit on the three chief feasts (Sehling 2, p. 460).

### 10.7 Bells

**Altenburg, castle chapter, *Verordnung der ceremonien*, 1533** (Sehling 1, p. 516):

<!-- doc 56 -->
> Zum dritten, so soll man auch hinfur die unterscheid halden, das man an gemeinen werktagen mit
> einer grossen glocken und am sontags und andern festen mit mer glocken zum heiligen ampt der
> predigt zu unterscheid der zeit leüte.

Thirdly, one shall also henceforth keep this difference: that on common working days one ring to
the holy office of the sermon with one great bell, and on Sundays and other feasts with more
bells, to distinguish the time.

The fullest bell-code is the Danzig one of 1612 (§5.6). It names five bells:

- Osanna;
- Apostolica;
- Dominical;
- Ferial;
- Sybilla.

The great feasts are rung in with the great bell and the three larger bells chimed together
(*beiern*). The small feasts are chimed "with the Osanna".

### 10.8 The Athanasian Creed and the *Te Deum*

The Mecklenburg convent order gives the rule for the convents' matins. **Mecklenburg,
*Klosterordnung*, 1572** (Sehling 5, p. 258):

<!-- doc 1926 -->
> Auf die hohen feste, als weinacht, epiphania, ostern, himmelfahrt, pfingsten, trinitatis, soll
> nach dem benedictus also balt das symbolum Athanasii teutsch gesungen werden.

On the high feasts, as Christmas, Epiphany, Easter, Ascension, Whitsuntide and Trinity, the
Athanasian Creed shall be sung in German straightway after the Benedictus.

For the *Te Deum* in Latin "in maioribus festis" see Transylvania 1547 (§4.3). For the *Te Deum*
as the Michaelmas thanksgiving see §8.5.

### 10.9 Communion and confession

The orders do not give the Supper by rank as such. Communion is held whenever there are
communicants. But rank shows in the preparation and in the days on which communion is normally
held.

- **Regensburg 1567.** Communion and vespers on the eve go with every feast of Christ, but not
  with the third day of the great feasts (§7.1).
- **Pomerania 1569.** No communion on apostles' days (§10.2).
- **Stade, *Kirchenordnung*, c. 1620/22** (Sehling 7/1, p. 54). On the three high feasts
  confession is heard after the afternoon sermon of the first day, and the Supper is given on
  the second day, "welches am ersten und letzten festage nicht geschicht", which is not done on
  the first and the last feast day.
- **First vespers.** The vespers of the eve of the higher feasts were also the time for private
  confession of the next day's communicants (for example Leiningen 1584, p. 278: "an den hohen
  festagen am abent zuvor ein vesper").

### 10.10 Festal ceremonies abolished

**The plays and processions of the year.** The late-medieval feasts had their own acted
ceremonies:
- the Palm Sunday ass;
- the crucifix laid in the "grave" on Good Friday;
- the image of the risen Christ carried round on Easter Day;
- the figure of Christ drawn up through the church roof on Ascension Day;
- the dove let down on Whitsunday;
- the rocking of the Christ-child's cradle at Christmas;
- processions round the church with holy water, crosses and the sacrament.

The south German orders abolish them by name. They also abolish the blessings of the year: water
and salt on Sundays, candles at Candlemas, ashes on Ash Wednesday, palms on Palm Sunday, and the
Easter candle, font and fire on Holy Saturday. **Hohenlohe, *Kirchenordnung*, 1553**
(Sehling 15, p. 77):

<!-- doc 539 -->
> Ebenmeßig sollen auch underwegen pleyben alle spectacula und schawspil, so man mit bildern
> getrieben hat, als am Palmentag mit dem eßel, am Karfreytag mit dem crucifix und grab, am
> Ostertag mit umbtragung des bilds der urstehnde Cristi, am Uffertag mit ufziehung deßelben, am
> Pfingstag mit herablasung der tauben, am Christag mit dem kindleinwiegen und was dergleychen
> mher im prauch gewesen ist. Item alle proceßion und umbgange umb die kirchen mit dem
> weyhewaßer und creutzen, auch mit umbtragung des heyhgen sacraments in der monstrantzen etc.

In like manner shall also be left off all spectacles and shows which have been practised with
images: as on Palm Sunday with the ass, on Good Friday with the crucifix and the grave, on
Easter Day with the carrying about of the image of the resurrection of Christ, on Ascension Day
with the drawing up of the same, on Whitsunday with the letting down of the dove, on Christmas
Day with the rocking of the little child, and whatever more of the like hath been in use. Item,
all processions and goings about the church with holy water and crosses, and also with the
carrying about of the holy sacrament in the monstrance, etc.

The same list stands in Pfalz-Neuburg 1543 (Sehling 13, pp. 95–96) and the Palatine *Bedenken
zur Reformation der Stiftskirchen* of 1546 (Sehling 14, p. 93). The Corpus Christi procession
went with the feast itself (§8.9).

**What stayed.** What the orders keep for the feasts is the reading and preaching of the feast's
history, its hymns and sequences, its proper preface, and in many places its Latin chant
(§10.1–10.4). The *Leisen* sung from the pulpit at the high feasts are treated in
[`HYMN_PRACTICE_GUIDE.md`](HYMN_PRACTICE_GUIDE.md) §10.2.

### 10.11 Seasonal changes in the service

**Advent and Lent.** Some orders mark the penitential seasons by plainer chant and by leaving
out the *Gloria*. The Pomeranian order of 1542 gives the old Kyrie grades by season:
*dominicale* in Advent and Lent, *paschale* from Easter to Whitsun, *summum* on the higher
feasts. In Lent the *Gloria* may be left out altogether. **Pomerania, *Kirchenordnung*, 1542**
(Sehling 4, p. 356):

<!-- doc 1859 -->
> Darup dat kyrie dominicale, efft me mach ock up die sondage, kyrie angelicum, de martiribus,
> effte confessoribus, efft virginibus, wo man sie genomet hefft singen, dominicale im advent
> und in der vasten, dat paschale tuschen pingsten und paschen, dat summum up die hohern fest,
> effte dat minus summum. Darup singe die priester dat gloria in excelsis […] in der vasten mach
> me dat gloria wol gar ute laten, effte wenn idt will to lange werden.

Thereupon the Kyrie *dominicale*; or one may also on the Sundays sing the Kyrie *angelicum*, *de
martyribus*, or *confessoribus*, or *virginibus*, as they have been named; the *dominicale* in
Advent and in Lent, the *paschale* between Pentecost and Easter, the *summum* on the higher
feasts, or the *minus summum*. Thereupon the priest singeth the *Gloria in excelsis* […] in Lent
one may well leave out the *Gloria* altogether, or when it will be too long.

Other seasonal changes in the orders:
- **The Creed in Advent.** At Schweinfurt "Wir glauben all" replaces "Nun bitten wir" on the
  three Sundays of Advent (Sehling 11, p. 646).
- **The Litany.** Days kept "plainly, *dominicaliter*, as in Advent and Lent" may have the Latin
  Litany (Pomerania, *Karcken ordening*, Sehling 4, p. 354).
- **Hymns.** The hymn tables change with the seasons: Advent, Christmas to Candlemas, Easter to
  Whitsun (see [`HYMN_PRACTICE_GUIDE.md`](HYMN_PRACTICE_GUIDE.md) §7.5 for the *Gloria* in
  Advent and Lent, and §10.3 for the seasonal hymns before the sermon).
- **Luther's warning.** Luther himself would not have Lent, Holy Week or Good Friday shown off
  "with other rites" than the rest of the year (§12.1).

---

## 11. Keeping holy days in common life

The orders kept the calendar by law as much as by liturgy. A holy day was a day on which work,
trade and amusement were restricted, and the restrictions were enforced by the magistrate. This
section covers the rest from work (§11.1), its enforcement (§11.2), weddings, guild feasts and
markets (§11.3), the folk festivals of the year (§11.4), and one feast created to replace them
(§11.5). Harvest exemptions are treated in §6.4.

### 11.1 Rest: the whole day, the forenoon, or the time of the sermon

**Not a commandment, but good order.** The first Saxon visitors already gave the principle. The
ceremonies of the church, the holy days among them, are not commanded by God. They are kept "for
the sake of good order and peace", as each parish has the custom. **Ernestine Saxony,
*Unterricht der visitatoren an die pfarrherrn*, 1528** (Sehling 1, p. 164):

<!-- doc 9 -->
> Denn solche stücke sind mehr von nöten, denn am freitag fleisch essen und der gleichen,
> wiewohl dasselb für gott und im gewissen recht ist. Doch sollen die leute dennoch unterrichtet
> werden, bescheidenlich von solchen kirchenordnung zu reden, denn etliche kirchenordnung sind
> gemacht umb guter ordnung und fridens willen, wie S. Paulus spricht in der 1. zun Corinthern
> am 14.: Es sol alles ordentlich in der kirchen geschehen. Darumb sollen die feiertag, als
> sontag und etliche mehr, wie jeder pfarr gewonheit ist, gehalten werden.

For such things are more needful than to eat flesh on Friday and the like, although that is
right before God and in the conscience. Yet the people shall nevertheless be taught to speak
modestly of such church orders; for some church orders are made for the sake of good order and
peace, as St Paul saith in the first to the Corinthians, the fourteenth: Let all things be done
in order in the church. Therefore the holy days, as Sunday and some more, shall be kept, as is
the custom of each parish.
