**Degrees of rest.** In practice the orders set three degrees:
- **Sunday and the high feasts**: no work all day;
- **the half holy days**: no work until noon, or until the sermon was over (§6.1);
- **the other holy days**: no work during the service only.

A visitation recess for an Oldenburg parish states the difference between Sunday and the other
holy days in one sentence. It lists the work forbidden: carting turf, hay and sheaves,
ploughing, harrowing, mowing and digging. The penalty is that the offender's horses are
unharnessed until he pays. **Oldenburg, *Visitationsabschied für Bardenfleth*, 1609**
(Sehling 7/2.1, p. 1201):

<!-- doc 2151 -->
> damit auf Sontagen gänzlich, die andern feiertage uber aber unter der predigt, mit torf-,
> heuw-, garbenfuhren, pflugen, eggen, meyen schwelen, graben und in andere wege mit der
> handarbeit eingehalten, auch bey wehrendem gottesdienst niemand bier oder wein gezapfet, und
> die muetwilligen verbrecher der gebuer mit auspannung der pferde biß zum abtrag gestraffet […]
> werden

that on Sundays wholly, but on the other holy days during the sermon, there be refraining from
carting turf, hay [and] sheaves, from ploughing, harrowing, mowing, [and] digging, and in other
ways from handiwork; also that during divine service no one tap beer or wine; and that the
wilful transgressors be punished as is due by unharnessing of the horses until [the fine be]
paid […]

**Work allowed after the sermon.** Pfalz-Zweibrücken allowed field work on all holy days except
Sunday and the three high feasts, "especially in harvest and autumn", on condition that the
people had first heard the sermon. The reason given was that "some holy days hinder working
people in their living", while giving the idle occasion "to feast, carouse and drink wine".
**Pfalz-Zweibrücken, *Generalartikel*, 1560** (Sehling 18, p. 293):

<!-- doc 971 -->
> Und nachdem die underthonen uff dem Land hiebei auch clagsweiß furbracht, das ettlich feuertag
> arbeitsame leuth an irer Narung hindern, den andern aber, so one das ursach zum Mussiggang
> suchen, zu fressen, sauffen und weindrincken glegenheit geben, so mögen wir leiden, daß
> ausserhalb der dreyer hoher fest und dem heyligen Sontag die ubrigen Feiertag, sonderlich inn
> Erntt- und herbstzeith, unsere underthanen ire nottwendige Veldt- und andere arbeith besuchen
> mögen, Doch daß sie zuvor denselbigenn Dag gottes wortt und die Predig besucht haben.

And whereas the subjects in the country have also brought forward complaint hereupon, that some
holy days hinder working folk in their living, but give to others, who without that seek
occasion for idleness, opportunity to gorge, carouse and drink wine, we are content that, apart
from the three high feasts and the holy Sunday, on the other holy days, especially in harvest
and autumn time, our subjects may attend to their necessary field and other work; yet so that on
that same day they have first attended God's word and the sermon.

**"Loose holy days".** Where the lesser feasts had been kept by halves, the people treated them
as working days altogether. A Henneberg pastor complained that on the days "which the common man
is wont to call loose holy days" the villagers did their road-mending and timber-carting, and
went hunting after the early sermon. **Wasungen, *Kirchen-Ordnung*, 1566**
(Sehling 2, pp. 356–357):

<!-- doc 1250 -->
> dann dieweil man an andern orten dise feirtag zum theil abgeschaffet und das geschrei gangen,
> sie sollen bald gar fallen, hat man auf solche feirtag, die der gemein man lose feirtag pflegt
> zu nennen, allerlei gemeine und eigene arbeit fürgenomen, als gemeine weg machen, in die
> landwer geen, bauholzfüren und dergleichen mer […]. Zu frue thut man eine predigt. Darnach
> jage man oder erbeit jederman was er will.

for because in other places these holy days have been in part abolished, and the cry hath gone
forth that they shall soon fall altogether, men have undertaken on such holy days, which the
common man is wont to call loose holy days, all manner of common and private work, as making the
common ways, going to the land-defences, carting building timber and more of the like […]. Early
a sermon is done; thereafter one hunteth, or every man worketh what he will.

### 11.2 Enforcement: gates, fines and taverns

**The town gates shut.** The Brandenburg visitors told town councils to shut the gates on
holy-day mornings, so that no one could go out to work in the fields. **Cölln on the Spree,
*Visitations-Abschied*, 1540** (Sehling 3, p. 194):

<!-- doc 1759 -->
> Es ist den visitatorn glaubwirdig vorkommen, das viele ungehorsamer leute sein, die den
> feiertag mit fahren und arbeiten, auch sonst verunheiligen, soll derwegen ein erbar rath die
> thore des heiligen tages vor mittage zuhalten, niemands daraus zur arheit gestadten

It hath come credibly before the visitors that there are many disobedient people who profane the
holy day with carting and working, and otherwise also; therefore the honourable council shall
keep the gates shut on the holy day before noon, [and] suffer no one [to go] out of them to work

The same order recurs in the visitation recesses for Salzwedel 1579 and Stendal 1578
(Sehling 3, pp. 285, 300, 331).

**Fines.** The town mandates fix the fine. Strasbourg (1534, Moderate Reformed) forbade every
kind of handiwork on Sunday: stacking wood, unloading wood or sand, bucking and washing. The
fine was thirty shillings. Milk and bread might be sold, and in Lent fish, until the last bell
for the sermon. **Strasbourg, *Mandat zur Sonntagsheiligung*, 1534**
(Moderate Reformed; Sehling 20/1, p. 246):

<!-- doc 1296 -->
> Erstlich, die weill der Sontag zu hörung Götlichs wortz verordnet, das niemantz in der stat
> Straßburgk oder deren burckban uf die Sontag on sonder dapfer, redlich ursach oder
> erheischende notturfft kheinerley handarbeit thun solle, es sey mit holtz legen, holtz oder
> sant uffschiessen, bauchen oder ußweschen oder andern arbeitten, wie die sind, bey peen
> dreissig schilling pfennig.

First, since Sunday is ordained for the hearing of God's word, that no one in the city of
Strasbourg or its precincts shall on Sunday, without special weighty [and] honest cause or
pressing need, do any handiwork whatsoever, be it with stacking wood, unloading wood or sand,
bucking or washing, or other works, whatever they be, on pain of thirty shillings pfennig.

The discipline orders of the Wetterau counts set fines for each offence: for walking in the
fields during the sermon, for trading, for sitting in the wine-house or working in the field
(Hanau-Münzenberg, *Reformationsmandat für das Amt Rodheim*, 1596, Reformed, Sehling 10, p. 414; see
[`SERMONS_GUIDE.md`](SERMONS_GUIDE.md) §12 for the fines for absence and for disturbing the
sermon). Taverns were to stay shut during divine service on Sundays and holy days alike.

### 11.3 Weddings, guild feasts and markets

Wedding feasts, guild banquets and markets were moved away from the high feasts, so that they
would not draw people from the sermon. The closed seasons for weddings in Advent and Lent are
treated in §12.6.

**Mansfeld, *Kirchen-agenda*, 1580** (Sehling 2, p. 240):

<!-- doc 1241 -->
> Von den hochzeiten auf die heuptfeste. Weil es auf die heuptfest sehr unbequem ist, das in den
> selbigen wirtschaften gehalten werden, so sollen hinfurt dieselben auf solche feste genzlich
> ein gestellet sein, und auf weinachten acht tage zuvor, und für dem sontage nach trium regum,
> desgleichen in ostern, für dem sontage quasimodogeniti, auch in pfingsten, für dem sontage
> trinitatis keine hochzeit gestattet werden

Of weddings at the chief feasts. Forasmuch as it is very unmeet that wedding-feasts be held at
the chief feasts, the same shall henceforth be wholly stayed at such feasts: and at Christmas,
eight days before, and until the Sunday after the Three Kings; likewise at Easter until the
Sunday Quasimodogeniti; and at Whitsuntide until the Sunday Trinity, no wedding shall be allowed

**Hadersleben, *Zuchtordnung*, 1573** (Sehling 23, p. 244):

<!-- doc 1599 -->
> Es sollen auch die gilden auf den hohen festen und sonderlich dem Pfingstdage nicht mehr
> gehalten, besundern auf den andern tag verschoben werden.

The guild-feasts also shall no more be held on the high feasts, and especially on Whitsunday,
but be put off to another day.

**Brandenburg, *Visitation- und Consistorialordnung*, 1573** (Sehling 3, p. 129):

<!-- doc 1748 -->
> Die obrigkeiten sollen auch verordnen, das die wochenmerkte, so an hohen festen gefallen, bis
> nach mittage oder auf den andern folgenden tag verschoben, auf das gottes wort dadurch nicht
> möge verhindert werden.

The magistrates also shall ordain that the weekly markets which fall on high feasts be put off
until after noon, or to the next day following, that God's Word be not hindered thereby.

### 11.4 Fastnacht, Kirchweih, May Day and Midsummer

**"Heathen" festivals.** The folk festivals of the year were attacked as "heathen" in mandate
after mandate:
- Shrovetide (*Fastnacht*) with its masks, dances and begging for eggs and meat;
- the church-dedication fair (*Kirchweih*, *Kirmes*);
- May Day and Walpurgis night, with the May-tree;
- Whitsun ales, the *Pfingstbier*;
- the Midsummer fires of St John's Eve;
- the *Hagelfeier*, a day of processions against hail.

The Hessian *Reformationsordnung* of 1572 (Philippist) forbids "Sunday dances, especially during
the sermon and the catechism", and all such "frivolous wantonness". **Hesse,
*Reformationsordnung*, 1572** (Philippist; Sehling 8, p. 401):

<!-- doc 2272 -->
> Also auch sollen die sontagstänze, sonderlich under der predigte und kinderlehr, darzu auch
> alle andere leichtfertige üppigkeiten, so nach heidnischer weis zur fastnacht, Walpurgis,
> Pfingsten, Joannistag und andern zeiten mehr durchs jar vom gemeinen man geübt und vorgenommen
> werden, genzlichen verboten sein und die uberfahrer jedermals nach gestalt der geübten
> leichtfertigkeit durch unsere beampten ernstlichen gestraft und uns die straf einbracht
> werden. Wann aber hochzeiten seind, mag man zimlich tanzen

So also shall the Sunday dances, especially during the sermon and the catechism, and also all
other frivolous wantonness which is practised and undertaken by the common man after heathen
manner at Shrovetide, Walpurgis, Whitsuntide, St John's day and at other times more through the
year, be wholly forbidden; and the transgressors shall each time be earnestly punished by our
officers according to the form of the wantonness practised, and the fine brought in to us. But
when there are weddings, one may dance modestly

The same list recurs in Nassau-Weilburg 1576
(with the "*Hagel* holy days" and the Walpurgis and St John's fires, Sehling 10, p. 310), Solms
1603 (Sehling 9, p. 351) and Ysenburg (Sehling 10, p. 707). Separate *Fastnacht* mandates were
issued in Waldeck (1547, 1563, 1589/90), Ysenburg (1590, 1612, Reformed) and Baden (1610)
(Sehling 9, pp. 222, 297, 301; 10, pp. 593, 693; 16, p. 572). Kurpfalz banned church-dedication
fairs, Shrovetide and mummery outright in its police legislation
(Sehling's introduction, Sehling 14, p. 66), and Waldeck abolished the *Kirchweih* in 1583
(Sehling 9, p. 299). The Franconian order of Thüngen named Corpus Christi and the *Hagelfeier*
together as feasts "whereby God is blasphemed" (Sehling 11, p. 737).

### 11.5 Shrove Sunday turned into a feast of baptism

**A new feast in place of an old one.** Bugenhagen's Hamburg order turned the Sunday before
Lent, the beginning of Shrovetide, into a day for preaching on baptism. The Gospel was the
baptism of Christ (Matthew 3). He remarks that there had never been a feast of Christ's baptism,
though there had been "so many useless feasts". **Hamburg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1529**
(Sehling 5, p. 507):

<!-- doc 1959 -->
> Darumme is vor gudt angeseen, den vastelavendes sundach besundergen to bruken to predike van
> der dope. Des morgens schallme in allen karspelkarcken prediken dat evangelion Matth. 3, wo
> Christus gedopet in der Jordane von sunte Johannes. Dat is eine trostlike und gnadentrike
> historie van dem heren Christo, de jo nicht vorschwegen schall werden. Idt is wunder, dat me
> nicht hefft gehat ein fest van der dope Christi, dewile me doch so vele unnutte feste hefft
> gehadt tho vorderve lives und der selen.

Therefore it is thought good to use the Shrove Sunday especially for preaching of baptism. In
the morning there shall be preached in all the parish churches the Gospel of Matthew 3, how
Christ was baptized in the Jordan by St John. That is a comfortable and gracious history of the
Lord Christ, which surely shall not be kept silent. It is a wonder that there hath not been a
feast of the baptism of Christ, whereas there have been so many unprofitable feasts, to the ruin
of body and soul.

The Schleswig-Holstein order of 1542 has the same rule (Sehling 23, p. 94). The Pomeranian
*Agenda* of 1569 explains its purpose: the people are to be reminded of their baptism, "that by
God's Spirit they may be the more led away from the heathenish, unchristian Shrovetide doings"
(Sehling 4, p. 473).

---

## 12. The seasons of the year

The evangelical orders kept the shape of the church year: Advent, Christmas, Lent, Holy Week,
Easter, Ascension, Whitsun and the Trinity season. What they changed was what each season meant
in practice. Lent lost its fast (§13) but kept its preaching. The seasons with the most extra
services were Lent and Holy Week. Their additions were sermons and catechism, not Masses. This
section covers Luther's starting point (§12.1), Advent (§12.2), Lent (§12.3), Holy Week (§12.4),
Rogation week and the catechism seasons (§12.5), the closed seasons for weddings (§12.6) and the
Ember days (§12.7).

### 12.1 Luther: no special show for Lent

**The *Formula missae*.** Luther would not mark Lent, Holy Week or Good Friday with rites that
set them apart from the rest of the year. To do so, with the half-Mass of the Presanctified and
the denial of the cup, would be "to mock and laugh at Christ". **Luther, *Formula missae et
communionis*, 1523** (Sehling 1, p. 5):

<!-- doc 2 -->
> Porro gradualia quadragesimalia et similia, quae duos versus excedunt, cantet quisquis velit
> in domo sua. In ecclesia nolumus tedio extingui spiritum fidelium. Sed nec ipsam quadragesimam
> sive maiorem hebdomadam aut sextam feriam penosam aliis ritibus ostentare decet, quam alias
> quascunque, ne semimissa et altera sacramenti parte Christum amplius ludere et ridere velle
> videamur. Alleluia enim vox perpetua est ecclesiae, sicut perpetua est memoria passionis et
> victoriae eius.

Further, the Lenten graduals and the like, which exceed two verses, let whosoever will sing at
home. In the church we will not have the spirit of the faithful quenched with tedium. Neither is
it meet to display Lent itself, or the Greater Week, or the Friday of the Passion, with other
rites than any other [days], lest we seem to wish to mock and laugh at Christ any longer with a
half-Mass and with one part of the sacrament. For the Alleluia is the perpetual voice of the
church, even as the memory of his passion and victory is perpetual.

In 1526 he said the same of Holy Week, which "shall be even as other weeks", with the Passion
preached an hour a day (*Deutsche Messe*, Sehling 1, p. 16; see
[`SERMONS_GUIDE.md`](SERMONS_GUIDE.md) §4.4). The later orders did not all follow him in the
chant. Some left the *Gloria* out in Lent and used the plain Kyrie (§10.11).

### 12.2 Advent

**The coming of Christ.** Advent is kept as a season of preaching on the prophecies of Christ's
coming and on the Last Day. The Pomeranian *Agenda* gives weekday sermons for it. **Pomerania,
*Agenda*, 1569** (Sehling 4, p. 463):

<!-- doc 1865 -->
> Wo men överst im advente up de werkeldage schal predigen, de prophecien van der tokümpst
> Christi, unde des herren Christi predigen vam jüngsten dage, dat volget herna.

But how one shall preach in Advent on the working days, the prophecies of the coming of Christ
and the Lord Christ's sermons of the Last Day, that followeth hereafter.

Advent was also a season for the catechism. At Allstedt (1533) the pastor was to examine the
children "three times in the year, in Advent, in Lent and after the harvest"
(Sehling 1, p. 511). The Niederwildungen catechism order of 1533 (Waldeck) held the catechism
three days a week in Advent (Sehling 9, p. 191).

### 12.3 Lent: repentance, the Passion and the catechism

**Preaching.** Lent kept its old name, *die Fasten* or *Quadragesima*, but its content became
preaching. The Württemberg order sets the themes: repentance through Lent; on Palm Sunday the
whole Passion read to the young people in three parts; on Maundy Thursday and Good Friday the
Passion expounded. **Württemberg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1553** (Sehling 16, p. 267):

<!-- doc 671 -->
> In der Fasten, Quadragesima genannt, soll von der rechten Christlichen buß gepredigt werden.
> Auf den Palmtag ist es nutzlich, neben der historien des Palmtages dem jungen volck die
> historien des gantzen Passions zu dreien malen, Nämlich morgens, nach mittag und zur Vesper
> zeit außgetheilt, fürzulesen, Und hernach dieselb historien auff den grünen Donnerstag unnd
> Charfreitag der gmeinen kirchen außzulegen und iren nutz und gebrauch mit sonderlichem ernst
> und fleiß zuerklären.

In Lent, called *Quadragesima*, there shall be preaching of right Christian repentance. On Palm
Sunday it is profitable, beside the history of Palm Sunday, to read to the young people the
history of the whole Passion, divided into three times, namely morning, afternoon and at Vesper
time; and thereafter to expound the same history to the common church on Maundy Thursday and
Good Friday, and to explain its profit and use with special earnestness and diligence.

The same text stands in Pfalz-Neuburg 1554, Pfalz-Zweibrücken 1557, Pfalz-Veldenz 1574 and
Kurpfalz 1556 (Sehling 18, pp. 207, 520; 14, p. 163). The division of the Passion into a series
of Lenten sermons is described in [`SERMONS_GUIDE.md`](SERMONS_GUIDE.md) §4.4.

**More weekday sermons.** In Lent the weekday sermons were increased. In the Corvey village of
Bruchhausen there was a sermon one day a week through Lent and two in the week before Easter.
**Corvey, *Kirchenordnung für das Corveyer Stiftsdorf Bruchhausen*, 1603** (Sehling 21, p. 243):

<!-- doc 1455 -->
> Uber diese itzt ermehlte Feyer- und Bettage soll auch in der Fasten Wochentlich einen tag in
> der Wochen, aber für Ostern zwey tage, des Donnerstages und Freytages, predige gehalten und
> dem Volck die Historia von dem Leiden Christi fürgehalten werden.

Over and above these holy days and prayer days now named, there shall also in Lent be preaching
weekly one day in the week, but before Easter two days, the Thursday and Friday, and the history
of the Passion of Christ be set before the people.

**Daily catechism instead of the *Salve Regina*.** The commonest daily service of Lent was the
catechism, held at the hour when the *Salve Regina* had once been sung. **Gotha, *Artikel durch
die hern visitatores einem erbarn rathe zu Gotha zugestellet*, 1555** (Sehling 1, p. 569):

<!-- doc 88 -->
> Alle tage in der fasten, als bald den ersten sontag anzufahen, da man im bapstumb das
> abgottische salve regina gesungen, wird der catechismus alle tage bis auf den sontag palmarum,
> mit den kindern gehalten, wird ein psalm als der 8. 16. 22. 69. 110. etc., die der passion
> Christi gedenken, neben dem stuck des catechismi, das da sol gehandelt werden, gesungen,
> darnach wird ein lection, ausm siracide, proverbiis Salomonis oder was sonst von pfarhern fur
> eine lection angeordnet, gelesen

Every day in Lent, beginning straightway on the first Sunday, [at the hour] when in the papacy
the idolatrous *Salve Regina* was sung, the catechism is held with the children every day until
Palm Sunday. A psalm is sung, as the 8th, 16th, 22nd, 69th, 110th etc., which remember the
Passion of Christ, beside the part of the catechism which is to be handled; thereafter a lesson
is read out of Sirach, the Proverbs of Solomon, or whatever other lesson is appointed by the
pastor

Calenberg 1542 gives the same rule (Sehling 6/2, p. 804). The Upper Lusatian orders of Zittau
and Herwigsdorf had the catechism preached every weekday in Lent "at Salve time"
(Sehling 3, p. 379).

**The Easter communion and the examination.** Lent ended in the Easter communion, which drew the
young people "in great number" (Regensburg 1572, Sehling 13, p. 509). The catechism was
therefore examined on the Sundays of Lent. **Albertine Saxony, *Ordnung* of Duke August, 1580**
(Sehling 1, p. 424):

<!-- doc 44 -->
> Zum andern sol der pfarrer auf den sontag Esto mihi etc. der gemein verkündigen, das hinfüro
> auf die sontage in der fasten nach der mittags predigt das examen mit den kindern und
> hausgesind gehalten werde, derhalben die eltern ihre kinder und gesinde darzu schicken und
> anhalten wollen, darmit sie aus ihrem catechismo rechenschaft ihres glaubens geben können.

Secondly, the pastor shall proclaim to the congregation on the Sunday *Esto mihi*, etc., that
henceforth on the Sundays in Lent, after the midday sermon, the examination shall be held with
the children and household servants; wherefore the parents shall send and hold their children
and servants thereto, that they may give account of their faith out of their catechism.

There was no daily Lenten Mass. The orders hold the Supper when there are communicants (§10.9).
In Lent the extra services are sermons, catechism and examination.

### 12.4 Holy Week

**Maundy Thursday and Good Friday.** The two days kept their themes: the institution of the
Supper and the Passion. In Hamburg the morning service on Maundy Thursday was held "as on a
Sunday, with the ceremonies", with a sermon on the sacrament. On Good Friday the Passion was
preached. **Hamburg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1556** (Sehling 5, p. 544):

<!-- doc 1962 -->
> In der vasten up den guden donnersdach schall ein itlick pastor to achte schlägen predigen vam
> sacramente des lives und blodes des heren Christi, wente vormiddage schall idt in der karcken
> alse sunsten up einen sondag mit den ceremonien geholden werden, alse wente nu her geschehen
> iss. Am guden freidage schall ein idtlicker pastor de passion des heren predigen to achte
> schlägen, doch schal idt einem iedern pastoren freiestahn, desülvige in der vasten antoheven,
> wenehr eme dat belevet, lank oder kort to tracterende.

In Lent, on Maundy Thursday, every pastor shall preach at eight o'clock of the sacrament of the
body and blood of the Lord Christ; for in the forenoon it shall be held in the church as
otherwise on a Sunday, with the ceremonies, as hath been done until now. On Good Friday every
pastor shall preach the Passion of the Lord at eight o'clock; yet it shall be free to every
pastor to begin the same in Lent, when he pleaseth, to treat it long or short.

Most orders keep Maundy Thursday and Good Friday as holy days, at least in the forenoon (§6.2).
The old Holy Week ceremonies (palms, the ass, the grave, the Easter fire) were abolished
(§10.10). Weddings were not held in Holy Week (§12.6).

### 12.5 Rogation week and the catechism seasons

**Processions abolished.** The Rogation processions through the fields (*Kreuzgänge*,
*Bittgänge*) went with the other processions (§10.10), and the *Hagelfeier* against hail was
condemned (§11.4).

**The week kept for the catechism.** Bugenhagen kept Rogation week (*Krützeweke*) as one of four
seasons for preaching the catechism through in eight sermons, by the superintendent and his
adjutor. During these weeks the other preachers rested from their weekday sermons.
**Braunschweig, *Kirchenordnung*, 1528** (Sehling 6/1, p. 378):

<!-- doc 1983 -->
> In der Advente twe wekene des Mandages, Dynxtedages, Donredages unde Frydages. In den ersten
> vullen twen weken in der vastene ock so. In der krützeweke mit der navolgenden weke ock so,
> ane des Heren hemmelvartdach. Twe wekene na der ernte, ehr me den hoppen afnympt, ock so.
> Darumme moten de beyden predicanten den catechismum kort unde vorstendich vaten vor de
> simpelen, dat me den kan uthpredigen in den achte prediken.

In Advent two weeks, on Monday, Tuesday, Thursday and Friday. In the first two full weeks of
Lent likewise. In Rogation week with the week following likewise, except the Lord's Ascension
Day. Two weeks after the harvest, before the hops are taken down, likewise. Therefore the two
preachers must frame the catechism briefly and understandably for the simple, that it can be
preached through in the eight sermons.

Hamburg 1529 and Lübeck 1531 repeat the plan (Sehling 5, pp. 505, 352). The four catechism
seasons stand where the Ember weeks had stood (§12.7).

### 12.6 Closed seasons: no weddings in Advent and Lent

**The old rule kept.** Most orders kept the medieval closed seasons for weddings: Advent, Lent
(often from mid-Lent) and the high feasts. They gave a new reason: the wedding feast would draw
people from the sermon. **Schwäbisch Hall, *Kirchenordnung*, 1543** (Sehling 17/1, p. 155):

<!-- doc 762 -->
> Es sollen die Hochzeiten nit auff die hohe Fest, als auff Weyhenachten, Ostern, Pfingsten
> etc., Auch nit im Advent und in der Fasten, sonderlich nach Mitfasten, gelegt und gehalten
> werden, damit die Leuth dardurch von der Predigt deß Göttlichen Worts und dem H. Gottesdienst
> nit abgeführet

Weddings shall not be laid and held on the high feasts, as on Christmas, Easter, Whitsuntide,
etc., nor yet in Advent and in Lent, especially after mid-Lent, that the people be not thereby
led away from the preaching of God's word and the holy service

Strasbourg states the closed seasons exactly and adds that "we do not distinguish the times
after the popish, superstitious manner". **Strasbourg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1598**
(Sehling 20/1, p. 636):

<!-- doc 1344 -->
> Wiewol wir aber die Zeit nit auff Bäpstische Abergläubische weise unterscheiden, Jedoch, weil
> alles ding seine Zeit hat und durch etlicher weniger Freude und Kurtzweil des grössern hauffen
> Andacht nit solle verhindert werden, So ist bei uns gebräuchlich, Das von dem dritten Sontag
> des Advents biß auff den ersten Sontag nach dem Newen Jahrs tag keine Hochzeiten außzuruffen
> oder einzusegnen angenommen werden, Deßgleichen auch nit von Laetare oder Mitfasten biß auff
> Ostern.

Although we do not distinguish the times after the popish, superstitious manner, yet, because
everything hath its time, and the devotion of the greater number shall not be hindered by the
joy and pastime of a few, it is the custom with us that from the third Sunday of Advent until
the first Sunday after New Year's day no weddings are accepted to be proclaimed or blessed;
likewise also not from *Laetare* or mid-Lent until Easter.

The same ban, with dispensations "for great causes", is in Saxony 1580, the Upper Palatinate
1579, Grubenhagen 1581, Lüneburg 1575, Stendal 1551, Marienhafe (East Frisia) 1593 and Oldenburg
(Sehling 1, p. 436; 13, p. 316; 6/2, p. 1065; 6/1, p. 672; 3, p. 316; 7/1, p. 718; 7/2.1, p. 1180).
Mansfeld extended it to the octaves of the high feasts (§11.3). Hoya forbade weddings and other
feasting through Lent and Holy Week, "that every one may prepare himself by a sober, moderate
life" for the memory of Christ's passion (Sehling 6/2, p. 1200).

### 12.7 The Ember days: from fast days to quarter days

**The fast gone, the date kept.** The Ember days (*Quatember*, *Fronfasten*, Low German
*quatertemper*) were the Wednesday, Friday and Saturday after Invocavit, Whitsunday, Holy Cross
(14 September) and St Lucy (13 December). The orders do not keep them as fasts (§13). They keep
them as the four quarter days of the church year. Their uses in the orders:
- **Payments.** The communicants' offering penny was paid "every Ember time", as were
  schoolmasters' fees, salaries and poor-relief accounts. This is the commonest use by far.
- **Catechism sermons.** The catechism was preached through in each Ember season. **Torgau,
  *Ordnung der Visitatoren*, 1529** (Sehling 1, p. 676):

<!-- doc 139 -->
> Der katecismus soll vier mal des jars gepredigt werden, nemlich in den vier quatember. Ein
> itzliche quatember zwu oder drei wochen dazu zu nemen und dieweil der catecismus weret, die
> andern predigten ruhen lassen.

The catechism shall be preached four times a year, namely in the four Ember seasons. Each Ember
season two or three weeks shall be taken for it, and while the catechism lasteth the other
sermons shall rest.

- **The Litany.** Albertine Saxony had the Litany sung "on the four Ember seasons for a week"
  (Sehling 1, p. 272), and Mansfeld "especially on the four Ember days, when the people are
  together" (Sehling 2, p. 239).
- **Ordinations.** In Pomerania candidates were examined and ordained "at the time of the Ember
  days, especially in Lent and at Michaelmas, when the catechism is repeated", so that the
  ordinands might hear how the catechism was preached. **Pomerania, *Kerckenordeninge*, 1569**
  (Sehling 4, p. 395):

<!-- doc 1862 -->
> Dat examen in der lere schal gescheen ut dem examine ordinandorum, locis communibus, ut dem
> catechismo unde im texte der hiligen schrift, unde sölckes up de tidt der quatertemper,
> sunderlick in der vasten unde up Michaelis, wenn de catechismus repeteret wert, dat de
> ordinanden vort hören de form, den catechismum to predigen.

The examination in doctrine shall be done out of the *Examen ordinandorum*, the *loci communes*,
the catechism and the text of holy Scripture, and that at the time of the Ember days, especially
in Lent and at Michaelmas, when the catechism is repeated, that the ordinands may straightway
hear the form of preaching the catechism.

- **Ordinances read from the pulpit.** The forbidden degrees of marriage and the list of
  offences to be reported were read from the pulpit at every Ember season in Nassau
  (Sehling 10, pp. 95, 115). The Pfalz-Neuburg edicts were to be read "four times a year at the
  Ember times" (Sehling 13, p. 115).
- **Marriage sermons.** Henneberg 1582 held a half-hour sermon on marriage on the Sunday after
  each Ember season (Sehling 2, p. 314).

---

## 13. Fasting

The medieval Church kept about 160 fast and abstinence days a year (Sehling's note, Sehling 11,
p. 92):
- the forty days of Lent, on the weekdays from Ash Wednesday to Holy Saturday;
- the Friday abstinence, and in many places the Saturday;
- the Ember days;
- the vigils of the great feasts.

On these days meat, and in Lent also eggs and dairy, were forbidden on pain of mortal sin. The
evangelical orders removed the obligation, kept the praise of fasting, and replaced the fixed
fasts with occasional days of fasting and prayer. This section covers the principle (§13.1), the
call to voluntary fasting (§13.2), the orders that kept the old fasts (§13.3), fasts kept for
civil reasons (§13.4), the days of fasting and prayer (§13.5) and fasting before communion
(§13.6).

### 13.1 Not a law, and not a merit

**Fasting set among human ordinances.** The visitors of 1528 list "appointed fasts, eating no
flesh on Friday" among the ordinances made "to earn grace for sins", which may therefore "be let
fall" (see [`OFFICES_GUIDE.md`](OFFICES_GUIDE.md) §3.4 for the passage). Eating meat on Friday
is "right before God and in the conscience", and less needful to preach than the commandments
(§11.1).

**Fasting good in itself.** The Lüneburg articles of 1527 put the evangelical teaching in one
sentence. Fasting is "useful and good, to tame the body". But it is not to be kept so that
anything is earned "by difference of food on appointed days". No pastor is to compel anyone "to
a difference of times or of food"; the people are to be led to "a willing fasting and a moderate
life". **Lüneburg, *Artikelbuch*, 1527** (Sehling 6/1, p. 500):

<!-- doc 1996 -->
> De achte artikel. Wo me syck ym vastende schal h o 1 d e n. Nademe ock vastent nütte unde gudt
> ys, den lycham tho temmen, jodoch nicht also to holdende, dat dorch underschedt der spyse an
> uthgetekeden dagen sunderlykes wes vordenet edder unhulde Goddes, so men ydt nicht helde,
> vorwerket wörde, sunder dewyle ydt Christus unde syne apostele, ock eine lange tydt darna de
> gemene christlicke kercke fryg gelaten, dat ein ytlick kerckhere nemande dwynge tho
> underschede der tydt edder spyse, sunder mer den wyllen Goddes se underrichte, dat ene solckes
> fryg gelaten tho etende ym namen des Heren, wat ene Got vorlenet, jodoch mit stedtlikem
> anholdende gödtlyckes wordes dem unvorstande unde frevel begegene, dat se nicht yn averfloth
> varen, sunder mer ein wyllich fastent unde metich levent annemen.

The eighth article. How one shall conduct himself in fasting. Forasmuch as fasting is useful and
good to tame the body, yet not to be so kept that by difference of food on appointed days
anything special be merited, or God's displeasure incurred if one kept it not; but since Christ
and his apostles, and also for a long time thereafter the common Christian church, left it free,
[it is ordered] that every pastor compel no one to a difference of times or of food, but rather
instruct them in the will of God, that it is left free to them to eat in the name of the Lord
what God granteth them; yet, with constant urging of God's word, he shall meet ignorance and
wantonness, that they fall not into excess, but rather take up a willing fasting and a moderate
life.

**"Who ever taught that Christians should not fast?"** The Calenberg order of 1542 answers the
charge that the evangelicals "teach nothing but to gorge on flesh". The answer has four points.
Fasting is to last the whole of life, not to be bound to fixed times. It is not to be drawn into
justification. It is not to be commanded on pain of mortal sin. And the difference of foods is
not to be suffered, "because Christ hath freed us from such ordinances". **Calenberg-Göttingen,
*Kirchenordnung*, 1542** (Sehling 6/2, p. 772):

<!-- doc 2033 -->
> denn wer hat jemals geleret odder geschrieben, das die Christen nicht fasten sollen? Man
> leret, was recht fasten sey, item, das nach der lehre Pauli solch fasten an keine bestimpte
> zeit gebunden sein, sonder die zeit unsers lebens weren solle, item, das solch werk in die
> justification nicht müsse gezogen oder bey einer todsunde, die gewissen damit zu bestricken,
> geboten werden, item, das man auch den unterscheid der speise hie nicht leiden könne, weil uns
> von Sollen satzungen Christus gefreiet habe

for who hath ever taught or written that Christians should not fast? We teach what right fasting
is; item, that according to the doctrine of Paul such fasting is not bound to any fixed time,
but shall last the time of our life; item, that such a work must not be drawn into
justification, nor be commanded on pain of a mortal sin, to ensnare the consciences therewith;
item, that the difference of food cannot be suffered here, because Christ hath freed us from
such ordinances

The Mecklenburg *Klosterordnung* of 1572 and the Braunschweig-Wolfenbüttel *Klosterordnung* of
1569 explain the old fish days as a piece of the monastic "house order", which had wrongly been
turned into "a special service of God" (Sehling 5, p. 252; 6/1, p. 307).

### 13.2 Fasting exhorted, but free

**The transitional orders.** The earliest orders of the 1520s and 1530s keep the old fast days
as a recommended custom. They tell the preachers to exhort the people to keep them, and to live
moderately every day, but they forbid any command under the ban or mortal sin.
**Brandenburg-Ansbach-Kulmbach, *Landtagsabschied*, 1526** (Sehling 11, pp. 92–93):

<!-- doc 259 -->
> Von fasten und fleischessen. Sollen die pfarherrn und prediger das volk vermanen, das sie nit
> allein in der virzigteglichen fasten, den quottembern und andern bisher gehaten fastagen,
> sunder teglich in meßigkeit und abbruch oder on uberfluß essens und trinkens leben, damit sie
> ire fleischliche leib zemen und geschickt machen, Gottes willen und werk zu volbringen und zu
> üben […]. Und dieweil man sich nach ordnung etlicher heiligen veter und concilien ein lange
> zeit an den bestimpten fasttegen fleisch, eir und anders zu essen Got zu lob und ere enthalten
> hat, wiewol on zweifel derselbigen heiligen veter und concilien meinung nit gewesen ist,
> jemand dardurch in geferlichkeit seiner seelen oder leibs zu setzen oder einen strick der
> sünden zu legen oder ein neue todsünd zu machen, sunder mer damit den menschen zu christlicher
> casteiung seins leibs zu furdern

Of fasting and eating flesh. The pastors and preachers shall exhort the people that they live
not only in the forty days' fast, the Ember days and the other fast days kept hitherto, but
daily in moderation and abstinence, or without excess of eating and drinking, that they may tame
their fleshly bodies and make them fit to fulfil and practise God's will and work […]. And
whereas, by the ordinance of certain holy fathers and councils, men have for a long time
abstained on the appointed fast days from flesh, eggs and other things, to the praise and honour
of God, although without doubt it was not the meaning of the same holy fathers and councils
thereby to put any one in danger of his soul or body, or to lay a snare of sin, or to make a new
mortal sin, but rather therewith to further men to Christian chastening of their body

Nassau-Dillenburg 1537 and Jülich-Kleve 1525 (Roman Catholic humanist) have the same text
(Sehling 10, p. 77; 21, p. 71).

**Before the Supper.** Oldenburg 1573, following Urbanus Rhegius, asks why Christians will not
fast "once or twice in the year" before coming to the Lord's table, when "the first godly
Christendom a thousand years ago kept the forty fast days, each according to his occasion".
**Oldenburg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1573** (Sehling 7/2.1, p. 1081):

<!-- doc 2149 -->
> Auch wenn er etwas ernstlichs von Gott bitten wil oder sich zu einer ernstlichen danksagung
> ins Herrn nachtmal schicken wil, so wird er auch zuvor durch anregung des Geistes Christi
> fasten oder messiglich und nüchtern leben. Wo ers nicht tut, so ist er noch gar fleischlich
> und ein titelchrist. Die erste frome christenheit vor tausent jaren hielte die vierzig
> fasttage, ein jeder nach seiner gelegenheit. Was sind wir für Christen, das wir im jar nicht
> ein- oder zweymal wollen fasten, wenn wir zu Gottes tisch gehen sollen?

Also, when he will ask anything earnest of God, or will prepare himself for an earnest
thanksgiving in the Lord's Supper, he will beforehand, by the moving of the Spirit of Christ,
fast, or live moderately and soberly. If he do it not, he is yet wholly carnal and a Christian
in name. The first godly Christendom a thousand years ago kept the forty fast days, each one
according to his occasion. What manner of Christians are we, that we will not fast once or twice
in the year, when we are to go to God's table?

### 13.3 Orders that kept the old fasts

**Waldeck 1528 and Baden.** A few orders kept the fasts as before. The articles for the Waldeck
synod at Mengeringhausen in 1528 say "all fast days shall be kept as hitherto", with abstinence
from meat "at the forbidden times". **Waldeck, *Beschlüsse für die Synode von Mengeringhausen*,
1528** (Sehling 9, p. 185):

<!-- doc 2300 -->
> Alle fasttage sollen gehalten werden wye bißher. Fleysch zu essen sol man sich enthalten uff
> verpotten zeyten. Sontag, apostelntag, unser frawentag etc. soll man feyren umb des worts
> willen, aber doch in der zeytt der ernd etc. nymandt gewissen zu machen.

All fast days shall be kept as hitherto. One shall abstain from eating flesh at the forbidden
times. Sunday, the apostles' days, Our Lady's days, etc., shall be kept holy for the Word's
sake, but yet in the time of harvest, etc., no one's conscience shall be burdened.

The Margrave Philipp of Baden, in his mandate of 1531, still required "the forty days and other
fast days brought down in the common Christian church, together with the Fridays and Saturdays",
with abstinence from meat except in need or bodily weakness (Sehling 16, p. 507). The Colmar
council, still Catholic (the city's Reformation came in 1575), complained in 1560 that the
Lenten fast and "other fast days through the year" were kept with little discretion "among young
and old, sick and healthy" (Sehling's introduction, Sehling 20/2, p. 481).

### 13.4 Fasting by police order

**Meat days for the price of meat.** Where a magistrate ordered abstinence, the reason given was
economic, and the order says so. The Nordhausen police order of 1549 forbids meat on Fridays,
Saturdays and in Lent because the price of meat was rising. The old, the sick, hard labourers,
young children and women in childbed are exempt. It declares that no one is to think it a sin.
**Nordhausen, *Polizei-Ordnung der Stadt Nordhausen*, 1549** (Sehling 2, p. 396):

<!-- doc 1254 -->
> Von fleisch essen und der fasten. Und deweil wir befinden, das der fleischkauf je lenger je
> mehr in grosse theurungen erwechst, so wollen wir, dass sich ein ider die wochen zwene tage,
> freitages und sonnenabends, auch die fasten des fleischessen enthalte, ausgeschlossen alte
> kranke leute, hartarbeiter, junge kinder, kindbetterin, oder den es auf befel der erzte
> erlaubt, doch das hierinne kein frevel oder missbrauch gesucht. Doch sol dieser artikel von
> niemand vorstanden werden, als ob es sünde und von gott vorboten were, die abgemelten zeit
> kein fleisch essen, welches auch von uns gar nicht gemeint, sondern wir wollen alles das, so
> gott der herr frei und ohne sünde zu gebrauchen gelassen hat, frei und ohne sünde bleiben
> lassen, und hierinne nicht anderst denn gehorsam und gut ordnunge, policei und messigkeit
> gesucht und gemeinet haben.

Of eating flesh and of Lent. And whereas we find that the buying of flesh groweth ever more into
great dearness, we will that every one abstain from eating flesh two days in the week, Fridays
and Saturdays, and also in Lent; excepted old [and] sick people, hard labourers, young children,
women in childbed, or those to whom it is allowed at the bidding of the physicians; yet so that
no wantonness or abuse be sought herein. Yet shall this article be understood by no one as
though it were sin and forbidden by God not to eat flesh at the aforesaid time, which is in no
wise meant by us; but we will let all that the Lord God hath left free and without sin to be
used remain free and without sin, and herein have sought and meant nothing else than obedience
and good order, police and moderation.

**The fish market.** Strasbourg's Sunday mandate (1534, Moderate Reformed) lets milk and bread
be sold before the sermon, "and in Lent the fish" (Sehling 20/1, p. 246; §11.2). Lent still
governed the market.

### 13.5 Days of fasting and prayer

**The new fast day.** In place of the fixed fasts, the orders appoint days of fasting and prayer
(*Buß-*, *Bett-* and *Fasttage*) in times of war, plague, dearth and threat to the church. Some
were monthly, some extraordinary. On them feasting and weddings were stopped, and the people
were called to fasting as well as prayer. The Palatine mandate of 1610 (Reformed), issued as the
Catholic League formed, tells the preachers to teach that "at dangerous, sorrowful times even
permitted joys are to be laid aside". It also tells them to correct the false idea that "with
the popish superstitious fasting the rightful Christian fasting also was done away". **Kurpfalz,
*Mandat zu den Bußtagen*, 1610** (Reformed; Sehling 19/2, p. 971):

<!-- doc 1204 -->
> 1. Wie daß zu gefehrlichen, trübseligen zeitten auch die erlaubte freuden einzustellenn. 2.
> Von dem gebrauch der kirche Gottes, sich durch fasten undt casteien des leibs zu einem
> eiferigen gebett vorzuberaitenn, da ihnen die falsche wahn zu benemmen, alß wann mit dem
> bäpstischen aberglaubigen fastenn auch das rechtmeßige christliche fasten were aufgehabenn.

1. How that at dangerous, sorrowful times even the permitted joys are to be laid aside. 2. Of
   the custom of the church of God, to prepare itself by fasting and chastening of the body for
   earnest prayer; wherein the false conceit is to be taken from them, as though with the popish
   superstitious fasting the rightful Christian fasting were also done away.

A Nassau-Weilburg text printed with the order of 1576 makes the monthly prayer days fast days,
on which betrothal feasts, guild banquets and all weddings in the week were stopped
(Sehling 10, p. 239). Hesse (Moderate Reformed) and Schleswig-Holstein ordered prayer days with
fasting in the 1550s and 1560s (Sehling 8, p. 263; 23, p. 270). Kurland mentions extraordinary
"fast days" set by edict in time of war (Sehling 5, p. 92).

**Fasting at the choice of ministers.** The Hessian order of 1566 (Moderate Reformed) and the
Reformed orders follow Acts 13 and 14: the church is to prepare "with fasting and prayer" for
the election of a superintendent or minister. **Micron, *Christliche Ordinancien* (1554; German
edition 1565)** (Reformed; Sehling 7/1, p. 588):

<!-- doc 2116 -->
> Wenn die gemeine eines dieners oder mehr dürftig ist, so wird alsdenn nach der ordnung Gottes
> ein gemeiner gewisser fasttag durch die eltisten angestellet, welcher tag darnach von der
> kanzel der ganzen gemeine verkündiget wird

When the congregation is in need of one minister or more, then, according to the ordinance of
God, a common, certain fast day is appointed by the elders, which day is thereafter proclaimed
from the pulpit to the whole congregation

The Hessian rule (Moderate Reformed) is at Sehling 8, p. 203, and the same provision is in
Pfalz-Veldenz 1574 (Sehling 18, p. 560).

### 13.6 Fasting before communion

**Sober for the sacrament.** The one fast the orders still expected regularly was the
communicant's. The Hessian order of 1566 (Moderate Reformed) defends receiving the sacrament
fasting with Augustine: "it hath pleased the Holy Ghost that, in honour of this most worthy
sacrament, the body of the Lord should enter the Christian's mouth before other food"
(Sehling 8, p. 309). The Saxon order of 1580 forbids banquets on the eves of Sundays and feasts,
so that the household might come to the sacrament sober. **Albertine Saxony, *Ordnung* of Duke
August, 1580** (Sehling 1, p. 430):

<!-- doc 44 -->
> Sollen derwegen auf die sonn und hoher fest abend nicht allein alle gastungen abschaffen,
> sondern auch ein jeder hausvater sein ganzes hausgesinde zu einem nüchtern leben, vornemlich
> aber wenn sie zum hochwirdigen sacrament gehen, anhalten, auf das sie nüchtern, mit lust und
> andacht an folgendem sonn oder festtage gottes wort hören, die communion wirdiglich gebrauchen

They shall therefore not only do away with all banquets on the eves of Sundays and high feasts,
but also every householder shall hold his whole household to a sober life, chiefly when they go
to the most worthy sacrament, that they may hear God's word soberly, with delight and devotion,
on the following Sunday or feast day, [and] use the communion worthily

**Who fasted, and how.** The orders give no general fasting rules for clergy or laity. What they
show:
- **Communicants.** Communicants were expected to come fasting and to keep the eve soberly
  (§13.6 above).
- **Everyone on prayer days.** The whole congregation was called to fast on the appointed days
  of fasting and prayer, and to give up feasts and weddings (§13.5).
- **Under police orders.** Where the magistrate ordered abstinence for economic reasons, the
  old, the sick, labourers, children and women in childbed were exempt (§13.4).
- **No separate clergy fast.** No order lays a separate fast on the clergy. The monastic fasts
  went with the monastic rules: Württemberg's convent order of 1535 counts the bonds of human
  ordinances "in fasting, silence, confessing, wearing cowls and tonsures" among what is cast
  off (Sehling 16, p. 79).

---

## 14. Occurrence and transfer

The medieval rubrics had detailed tables of "occurrence" (two feasts on the same day) and
"concurrence" (the vespers of one feast meeting the first vespers of the next). A feast of
higher grade displaced one of lower grade, which was commemorated or transferred. The Lutheran
orders keep the principle, but apply it to three practical cases:

- a feast that falls on a Sunday;
- the Annunciation in Holy Week;
- a lesser feast that falls on a Saturday, or in the same week as another.

Their rules show how the ranks of §5 were understood: Sunday against feast, feast of Christ
against Passiontide, apostle against working week.

### 14.1 A feast on a Sunday

The general rule is that a feast of Christ displaces the Sunday Gospel, while an apostles' day
yields to it.

**Rostock, *Conformitas ceremoniarum*, c. 1560** (Sehling 5, p. 288):

<!-- doc 1936 -->
> So aber grosse feste auf den sonntag fallen, als weinachten, S. Stephani, S. Joannis
> evangelistae, den neuen jars, der heiligen könige, purificationis M., annunciationis M.,
> Joannis Baptista, visitationis M. und Michaelis, sol man die evangelia von den festen
> behalten. […] 7. Wen aber uff den sontage der apostel fest fallen, sol man die sontags
> evangelia behalten und die evangelia so uff die apostel fest fallen, auf den nachmittage
> predigen.

But if great feasts fall on the Sunday, as Christmas, St Stephen, St John the Evangelist, New
Year's, the Three Kings, the Purification of Mary, the Annunciation of Mary, John the Baptist,
the Visitation of Mary and Michaelmas, one shall keep the Gospels of the feasts. […] 7. But when
the apostles' feasts fall on the Sunday, one shall keep the Sunday Gospels, and preach the
Gospels that fall on the apostles' feasts in the afternoon.

**Note.** St Stephen and St John count here as "great feasts", as days of Christmas, while the
apostles do not.

Mansfeld sets out the apostles' days by the day of the week on which they fall. **Mansfeld,
*Kirchen-agenda*, 1580** (Sehling 2, p. 240):

<!-- doc 1241 -->
> Felt ein apostel tag auf einen sontag, so mag er gehalten werden wie er gefellet, doch das man
> in den stedten in den kirchen, da man des sontags zwei oder drei mal predigt, auf derselbigen
> predigt eine, das sontags evangelium auch auslege. Felt ein apostel tag auf einen montag,
> dienstag, mitwoch, donnerstag, oder freitag, kan man in auf den tag, darauf er gefellet in den
> stedten, wie es gebreuchlich ist, halten, da es aber breuchlich ist, denselben auf den
> nechstfolgenden tag, darinne man sonst wochen predigten helt, zuverlegen, da mag es bei
> solcher gewonheit bleiben. Felt aber ein aposteltag auf einen sonnabend, sol er auf den
> nechstfolgenden sontag verleget und gehalten werden.

If an apostle's day fall on a Sunday, it may be kept as it falleth; yet so that in the towns, in
the churches where there is preaching two or three times on Sunday, the Sunday Gospel also be
expounded in one of the same sermons. If an apostle's day fall on a Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday,
Thursday or Friday, it may be kept in the towns on the day whereon it falleth, as is customary;
but where it is customary to remove it to the next following day whereon the week-day sermons
are otherwise held, it may abide by that custom. But if an apostle's day fall on a Saturday, it
shall be removed to the next following Sunday, and kept thereon.

**Note.**

- **Wintzingerode.** The order (Sehling 2, p. 256) has the same rules. There the apostle's
  Gospel goes to the Sunday afternoon.
- **Thorn 1575.** Small feasts falling near a Sunday go to its vespers (§5.6).
- **Danzig 1612.** A small feast falling on a Sunday is rung like a great one (§5.6).
- **Nördlingen 1579.** A local thanksgiving day falling on a Sunday is to be kept "solenniter
  wie andere hohe festa" (Sehling 12, p. 388).

### 14.2 The Annunciation in Holy Week

The Annunciation (25 March) often falls between Palm Sunday and Low Sunday. The orders count it
as a feast of Christ. It cannot simply be dropped, and it may not displace the Passion and
Easter. The orders give at least six solutions:

| Solution | Orders |
|---|---|
| The Saturday before Palm Sunday (*Palmabend*) | Hadersleben 1528; Schleswig-Holstein 1542; Merseburg 1544; Anhalt 1568; Pomerania 1569; Sayn 1582 |
| Palm Sunday itself, in the forenoon | Aschersleben 1575; Schwarzburg 1587; Wintzingerode (only if it falls Wednesday to Saturday, "per anticipationem") |
| A weekday of the week before Palm Sunday | Waldeck 1556 (Wednesday after Judica); Hoya 1571 and Harlingerland 1573/74 (Thursday after Judica) |
| The Sunday after Easter ("nach alter gewonheit") | Prussia 1526, 1544 and 1568 |
| Maundy Thursday | Burzenland chapter, 1573 (a proposal of the dean for that year) |
| Omitted that year | Ysenburg 1582 |
| A convenient day agreed with the neighbouring churches | Thorn 1575 |

**Hadersleben, *Artikel*, 1528** (Sehling 23, p. 64):

<!-- doc 1576 -->
> Wnser frowen vorkundinge dach (unnd wen de up den Gronen Donnersdach valt effte darnha, ßo sal
> me ohn eintrechticklich up den Sonnavent vor Palmarum holden, sunst sal me ohn nicht
> transfereren)

Our Lady's Annunciation day (and when it falleth on Maundy Thursday or thereafter, it shall be
kept with one accord on the Saturday before Palm Sunday; otherwise it shall not be transferred)

**Merseburg, *Synodalunterricht*, 1544** (Sehling 2, p. 20):

<!-- doc 1211 -->
> Mit verlegung aber der fest, das annunciationis Marie, do dis in die marterwoche gefiele, auf
> den palmabent gelegt wurde. Der andern feste verlegunge solle durch das consistorium bestimbt
> und idem superattendenten den pfarhern in synodis angezeiget werden.

But as touching the removing of the feasts: that the Annunciation of Mary, if it should fall in
the week of the Passion, be laid on the eve of Palm Sunday. The removing of the other feasts
shall be determined by the consistory, and shown to the parsons in the synods by the
superintendent.

**Pomerania, *Agenda*, 1569** (Sehling 4, p. 469):

<!-- doc 1865 -->
> Wenn de dach der vorkündiginge Marie velt in de stilleweke, idt si weckeren dach idt si, schal
> men dat fest vorlegen up palmavend unde sölckes am sondage Iudica to vörne affkündigen.

When the day of the Annunciation of Mary falleth in the still week, be it what day it be, one
shall lay the feast on the eve of Palm Sunday, and bid the same beforehand on the Sunday Judica.

**Wintzingerode (Gericht Bodenstein), *Kirchen-Ordnung*** (Sehling 2, p. 256):

<!-- doc 1243 -->
> So das fest annunciationis Mariae in der marterwochen auf einen montag oder dienstag
> einfellet, so soll es auf dieselbigen gehalten werden. Fellet es aber auf den mittwochen,
> grünen donnerstag, charfreitag oder oster abent, soll mans (wie es die alten nennen) per
> anticipationem zurück auf den palm-sontag feiern.

If the feast of the Annunciation of Mary fall in the week of the Passion on a Monday or Tuesday,
it shall be kept on the same. But if it fall on the Wednesday, Maundy Thursday, Good Friday or
Easter Eve, one shall keep it holy (as the ancients call it) *per anticipationem*, back upon
Palm Sunday.

**Note.** *Per anticipationem* is the old term for moving a feast back, before its date. "As the
ancients call it" shows that the orders knew they were using the medieval rules of transfer.

**Schwarzburg, *Ordnung der fest und feiertage*, 1587** (Sehling 2, p. 137):

<!-- doc 1230 -->
> Wenn aber das fest Adnunciationis, wie bisweil geschicht, in die marter wochen gefiel, soll er
> auf den sontag palmarum gelegt und vormittag solenniter gehalten, das evangelium aber
> desselben sontags nachmittag geprediget werden

But if the feast of the Annunciation, as sometimes happeneth, fell in the week of the Passion,
it shall be laid on Palm Sunday and kept solemnly in the forenoon, but the Gospel of the same
Sunday be preached in the afternoon

**Waldeck, *Kirchenordnung*, 1556** (Sehling 9, p. 272):

<!-- doc 2300 -->
> Es soll aber diß fest (ergerliche ungleicheit zue verhuten) mittwochen nach Judica gehaltenn
> werden, wens auf den palmtag oder in palmwochen fellet, sonst sol es auff den tag, [auf den]
> es kompt, gehalttenn werdenn

But this feast shall be kept (to avoid offensive inequality) on the Wednesday after Judica, when
it falleth on Palm Sunday or in Palm week; otherwise it shall be kept on the day whereon it
cometh

**Prussia, *Kirchenordnung und Ceremonien*, 1568** (Sehling 4, p. 87):

<!-- doc 1833 -->
> Wann der tag Conceptionis Christi oder Annunciationis Mariae auf den palmtag oder in der woche
> vor ostern oder dieselbige folgende wochen in den ostertagen gefellt, soll er den nechsten
> sonntag nach ostern gehalten und lauts der vorigen kirchenordenung transferirt werden, damit
> dieselbige zeit vom einreiten des herrn Christi und sein heiliges leiden sammt der historien
> der auferstehung unverhindert nach nothdurft müge geprediget werden.

When the day of the Conception of Christ or the Annunciation of Mary falleth on Palm Sunday, or
in the week before Easter, or in the same following week in the Easter days, it shall be kept on
the next Sunday after Easter, and be transferred according to the former church order; that the
same time may be preached, without hindrance and as need requireth, of the riding in of the Lord
Christ and his holy Passion, together with the history of the resurrection.

**Note.** The rule goes back to the Prussian order of 1526 (Sehling 4, p. 38). That order keeps
the day after Easter "nach alter gewonheit", after the old custom, which is the medieval
practice of transferring the Annunciation to the week after Low Sunday.

In 1573 Easter fell on 22 March, so the Annunciation fell on the Wednesday after Easter. The
dean of the Burzenland chapter writes. **Burzenland chapter (Transylvania), resolutions of 25
February 1573** (Sehling 24, p. 371):

<!-- doc 1694 -->
> 6. In veteri ecclesiae computo festum Annuntiationis Mariae sive Conceptionis Christi
> assignatum est 25 Martii, qui cadit in diem Mercurii post resuscitatum Christum, ubi alia
> plura festa concur[…]runt. Si tunc celebraverimus illud, ingluviem et intemperantiam auxerimus
> in nostratibus. Calendariorum vulgarium huius anni authores retraxerunt in diem sabbathi ante
> Palmarum. Is dies profanis mercaturis plus quam celebrationi festorum dicatus est. Ego igitur
> festum illud celebrabo ad feriam 5 ante Pascha, quae dicitur dies Coena Domini.

6. In the old reckoning of the Church the feast of the Annunciation of Mary, or Conception of
   Christ, is assigned to the 25th of March, which falleth on the Wednesday after Christ's
   rising again, when several other feasts concur. If we celebrate it then, we shall increase
   gluttony and intemperance among our people. The makers of the common calendars of this year
   have drawn it back to the Saturday before Palm Sunday. That day is more given to profane
   marketing than to the celebration of feasts. I will therefore celebrate that feast on the
   fifth day before Easter, which is called the day of the Lord's Supper.

The Thorn order admits that practice varies. **Thorn, *Kirchenordnung*, 1575**
(Sehling 4, pp. 239–240):

<!-- doc 1844 -->
> Weil es aber mit erwehlung eines bequemen tages nicht in allen orten gleichförmig gehalten
> wird, wie in denen alten und neuen kirchenordnungen zusehen, so ist es am sichersten, auch am
> bequemsten, dass man sich mit denen um und nahe gelegenen kirchen und städten vergleiche, das
> fest mii ihnen auf einen tag zuhalten, ob sie gleich nicht […] alle in der religion mit uns
> zustimmen, damit nur der schwachen etlicher massen wahrgenommen werde.

But because in the choosing of a convenient day it is not held alike in all places, as is to be
seen in the old and new church orders, it is surest and also most convenient that one agree with
the churches and towns lying round about and near, to keep the feast with them on one day,
though they do not […] all agree with us in religion; only that the weak may in some measure be
cared for.

### 14.3 Saturdays, harvests and two feasts in one week

Henneberg gives a rule for the "common feasts" of the second rank. **Henneberg,
*Kirchenordnung*, 1582** (Sehling 2, p. 313):

<!-- doc 1247 -->
> Wenn aber ein gemein fest auf einen sontag gefiele, sol desselbigen sontags verordnetes
> evangelium fort geprediget, das fest verkündiget, und, das auf nechstfolgenden mitwochen die
> historien oder fests evangelium zu predigen verlegt sei, dem volk angezeigt werden. So aber
> zwei oder mehr gemeine fest in eine wochen fielen, sol das erste auf den tag, darauf es
> gefellt, gehalten, des andern fests historia oder evangelium aber auf den folgenden mittwoch
> der andern wochen ordentlich gehandelt, und solches gleichwol allwege auf den sontag zuvor
> verkündiget werden.

But if a common feast should fall on a Sunday, the Gospel appointed for the same Sunday shall be
preached as usual, the feast proclaimed, and it shall be shewn to the people that the history or
Gospel of the feast is removed to be preached on the next following Wednesday. But if two or
more common feasts fall in one week, the first shall be kept on the day whereon it falleth; but
the history or Gospel of the other shall be orderly handled on the following Wednesday of the
next week; and this nevertheless shall always be proclaimed on the Sunday before.

**Aschersleben, *Kirchen-Agenda*, 1575** (Sehling 2, p. 479):

<!-- doc 1260 -->
> Wan aber das fest auf einen sonnabent felt, so wird es vorlegt bis auf den sontag nachmittage.
> Weil auch der tag Jacobi apostoli in der ernten kompt, pflegt man das fest, wan es auf einen
> freitag oder sonnabent gefelt, auf den nechsten sontag darnach nachmittage zuhandeln.

But when the feast falleth on a Saturday, it is removed until the Sunday afternoon. Also,
because the day of James the apostle cometh in the harvest, it is the custom, when it falleth on
a Friday or Saturday, to handle the feast on the next Sunday after, in the afternoon.

In Schwarzburg apostles' days are not moved, except from a Saturday. **Schwarzburg, *Ordnung der
fest und feiertage*, 1587** (Sehling 2, p. 137):

<!-- doc 1230 -->
> die festa der aposteln, sollen ohne unterlassung und ohne einige vorlegung auf den tag, do sie
> gefallen, feierlich gehalten werden, vormittag dan auch die orgeln, wo man sie hat, gebraucht
> werden sollen […] doch wan ein solch apostel fest auf einen sonnabend gefellet, soll es den
> sonntag hernach zur vesper volgen und gehalten werden

the feasts of the apostles shall be kept solemnly, without omission and without any removing, on
the day whereon they fall; in the forenoon also the organs, where there be any, shall be used
[…] yet when such an apostle's feast falleth on a Saturday, it shall follow on the Sunday after,
at vespers, and be kept then

### 14.4 Moving not allowed

Against these rules for moving feasts, several orders forbid pastors to move feasts on their own
authority, whether for markets or to save themselves work.

**Salzwedel (Altstadt), *Visitations-Abschied*, 1579** (Sehling 3, p. 275):

<!-- doc 1779 -->
> darum verordenen die visitatores, das der pfarherr und caplene allhie die festage, an den
> tagen, daran sie gefallen, inhalts der kirchen und visitationordenunge christlich und
> solemniter halten und nicht nach ihren gefallen verschieben oder verlegen, noch einige fest
> auslassen sollen, ungeachtet, do die gleich auf die marktage gefielen

therefore the visitors ordain that the parson and chaplains here shall keep the feast days on
the days whereon they fall, according to the church and visitation orders, in Christian manner
and solemnly, and not put them off or remove them at their pleasure, nor leave out any feast,
notwithstanding they should fall on the market days

**Sayn, *Kirchenordnung*, 1590** (Sehling 19/1, p. 389):

<!-- doc 1090 -->
> Es soll auch kein Pfarrherr Macht haben, dieselbige Fest oder Feyertage zuverlegen auff andere
> Tag, sondern sich genügen lassen zu feyren auff die Tag, darauff sie fallen, damit kein
> Ungleichheit gehalten werde zum Ergerniß und Nachrede, etc.

Neither shall any parson have power to remove the same feasts or holy days to other days, but he
shall content himself to keep them holy on the days whereon they fall, that no inequality be
held, to offence and evil report, etc.

The Kosd and Schelken statutes continue after the sentence quoted in §3.7. **Kosd and Schelken
chapters (Transylvania), statutes, 1577** (Sehling 24, p. 400):

<!-- doc 1704 -->
> Si quis vero in gratiam suorum colonorum aliquem diem festum celebraverit, qui in cathalogo
> festorum non est, aut festum in alium diem celebrandum reiecerit, poena capituli punietur

But if any, to please his peasants, shall have celebrated any feast day which is not in the
catalogue of feasts, or shall have put off a feast to be celebrated on another day, he shall be
punished with the chapter's penalty

---

## 15. The short calendars: Upper German towns and the Reformed

At the other end from the conservative lists of §5.7 stand the orders that kept only Sunday and
a handful of high feasts. In these the question of rank hardly arises: there is Sunday, and
there are the few feasts of Christ that remain. They are mostly Upper German cities under
Zwinglian or Bucerian influence (Reformed or Moderate Reformed), and later Reformed territories.
Their history shows how unstable the minimum was.

Mülhausen's first step (Reformed) keeps four "high-tides" with the Marian feasts and the
apostles, and abolishes the local and lesser days. Among them is "der achtest Sannt Steffans",
the octave of St Stephen, the only octave named in any list of abolished feasts. **Mülhausen
(Alsace), council decree on processions and holy days, 1524** (Reformed; Sehling 20/2, p. 196):

<!-- doc 1377 -->
> Das unnd anders angesehen, ist geordent, das man hinfur den heiligen Sonntag vorab unnd darzu
> die hohen fest: die vier hochzitt, Unnser lieben frawen unnd der appostel tag eeren unnd began
> sol, wie ge[…]wonheit ist. Aber dise nachbestimpten tag, mit namen: der Kindlin tag, der
> achtest Sannt Steffans, Marcus, die zwen heilig crutztag, keyser Heinrich […] Magdalena, Sant
> Steffan im Ougsten, Laurentius, kalte kilby, Gallus, Lucas, Katherina, Nicolaus, Ostermitwoch
> unnd Pfingstmitwoch, die alle sollen hinfur nit mer gebotten werden zu feyren noch fur feyrtag
> gehalten; aber nütdestminder sollen deren gedechtnuß uff yedes tag in der kilchen beganngen
> werden, vorab mit verkhundung des gotsworts unnd darnach mit den emptern der mesß, wie
> gewonheit ist

This and other things considered, it is ordained that henceforth men shall honour and keep first
the holy Sunday, and thereto the high feasts, the four high-tides, our dear Lady's days and the
apostles' days, as the custom is. But these days hereafter appointed, by name: the Children's
day, the eighth day of St Stephen, Mark, the two holy Cross days, the Emperor Henry […]
Magdalene, St Stephen in August, Laurence, the cold church-hallowing, Gallus, Luke, Catherine,
Nicholas, Easter Wednesday and Whitsun Wednesday: these shall all henceforth be no more
commanded to be kept holy, nor held for holy days; but nevertheless their memory shall be kept
on each day in the church, first with the proclaiming of God's Word and thereafter with the
offices of the mass, as the custom is

**Note.** The "cold church-hallowing" (*kalte kilby*) is the dedication feast of Basel Minster,
11 October. The "four high-tides" at Mülhausen are Christmas, Easter, Ascension and Pentecost
(see the next quotation).

Five years later Mülhausen adopted the Basel order (both Reformed). **Mülhausen, opinion on the
Basel and Strasbourg orders, 1529** (Reformed; Sehling 20/2, pp. 214–215):

<!-- doc 1382 -->
> Item mit den feyertagen sols noch Baßler ordnung gehalten werden, die nit meer dann den
> Winachsdag, den Uffart-, den Ostertag und Pfingstag mit […] sampt den Sontagen zu fyren
> verordnet haben.

Item, with the holy days it shall be kept after the Basel order, which hath ordained to keep
holy no more than Christmas day, Ascension day, Easter day and Whitsunday, together with the
Sundays.

**Augsburg, *Feiertagsordnung* of the council, 22 July 1537**
(Moderate Reformed; Sehling 12, p. 84):

<!-- doc 339 -->
> so hat ein rat derhalben angesehen und erkennet, das furohin allein die sonntäg - und alle
> abent der sambstäg um drei ur nach mittem tag anzufahen desgleichen der Christag, Ostertag und
> Pfingstag (ußerhalb einiger nachfeir), […] dazu der Neujarstag, der tag der Auffart Christi
> und der tag der Verkundigung Mariä gefeiret werden, dardurch alle und jede andere feirtag
> alhie, keinen ausgenommen, hiemit aufgehebt sein sollen, also das meniglich an allen und jeden
> andern tagen außerhalb der obbemelten feirtäg wol arbeiten und in schwais des angesichts die
> narung suchen mag.

therefore the council hath considered and resolved that henceforth only the Sundays (and every
Saturday evening, to begin at three of the clock after midday), likewise Christmas day, Easter
day and Whitsunday (without any after-holiday), […] thereto New Year's day, the day of the
Ascension of Christ and the day of the Annunciation of Mary, be kept holy; whereby all and every
other holy day here, none excepted, shall hereby be taken away, so that every man may on all and
every other day, beside the holy days aforesaid, well work and seek his living in the sweat of
his face.

**Note.** "ußerhalb einiger nachfeir", "without any after-holiday", abolishes the second and
third days of the great feasts. The Saturday evening, from three o'clock, is treated as part of
the Sunday.

The Esslingen council (Moderate Reformed) first abolished everything but Sunday and Christmas
(22a), then brought the apostles' days back (22b). **Esslingen, council decrees of 1537**
(Moderate Reformed; Sehling 17/2, pp. 389–390):

<!-- doc 883 -->
> Dweil aber ein ersamer rath vermerckt, das an solichen feirtagen das gemein volck meher zu
> offen zechen unnd anderer leichtfertigung dann zuverkhindung des gotlichen worts geneigt,
> damit dann solich ubel, so uß dem miessiggang entsteen, abgestelt und ain jeder zu theglicher
> arbeit getzogen, hat ein ersamer rath alle feirtag ausserhalb das deß heiligen sontags unnd
> weihenacht tag gentzlich abgeschafft und will, das der selbigen khainer meher gefeirt,
> besonder ain jeder sich an solichen tagen zu seiner teglichen arbeit geschickt mache. […] und
> aber yetzo augenscheinlichen befunden, das, gleich so wol uff die gute montag allso hievor uff
> die feirtag geschehen, alle fillerei unnd unzucht, sonderlich bey dem jungen folck jern
> fürganng haben, aber allein dem hailsamen Gottes wort kein zeit noch weil gegebenn werden mag
> […] so thut ir ersam […] weißhait auß disen hievor erzellten unnd anndern mehr beweglichen
> ursachenn ordnen und will, das hinfiro nebenn denn gepannen sontägen der hailligen apostel tag
> widerumb gefeirt und an denselben das Gottes wort verkhint unnd gehört werde

But forasmuch as an honourable council perceiveth that on such holy days the common people are
more given to open carousing and other lightness than to the preaching of the divine Word; that
such evil, which ariseth out of idleness, may be put down, and every man drawn to his daily
work, an honourable council hath wholly done away all holy days, beside the holy Sunday and
Christmas day, and willeth that none of the same be kept holy any more, but that every man
betake himself on such days to his daily work. […] and now it is found plainly that on the "good
Mondays", even as heretofore on the holy days, all drunkenness and unchastity have their course,
especially among the young folk, while no time nor leisure can be given to the wholesome Word of
God alone […] therefore their honourable wisdom, for these causes aforesaid and more other
weighty causes, ordaineth and willeth that henceforth, beside the commanded Sundays, the holy
apostles' days be kept holy again, and God's Word be preached and heard thereon

**Note.** The "gute Montage" were the "blue Mondays" that craftsmen took off. The Esslingen
council learned that abolishing holy days did not abolish holidays, and restored the apostles'
days in order to have days for preaching.

At Strasbourg (1544, Moderate Reformed) only Sunday is a holy day in the city. The saints are
remembered in the sermon, and the countryside may keep its half and whole days. **Strasbourg,
synodal decrees, 1544** (Moderate Reformed; Sehling 20/1, pp. 342–343):

<!-- doc 1312 -->
> Weil inn der stat allein der Sonnetag gefeiret würt, sollen die prediger das volck offt und
> mit allem ernst ver-[…]manen, das sie solche feyre dem Herren gentzlich heiligen. Sollen auch
> auff die selbigen, wann inn der wochen die tag fallen, auff […] welche man sunst der fürnemen
> werck Gottes, so er an seinem lieben son oder auch den apostolen und martyren gewürcket,
> gedechtnüssen und feire haltet, solcher gedechtnüssen, historien, wan man die inn der Schrifft
> oder sunst inn bewerten bücheren hat, dem volck fürtragen […] Wa aber auff dem land solche
> gedechtnüssen noch ire feire den gantzen oder halben tag haben, da solle man sie christlich
> behalten.

Because in the city only the Sunday is kept holy, the preachers shall oft and with all
earnestness exhort the people that they hallow such rest wholly unto the Lord. They shall also
on the same [Sundays], when the days fall in the week whereon otherwise memorials and holy days
are kept of the chief works of God which he hath wrought in his dear Son, or also in the
apostles and martyrs, set forth to the people the memorials and histories of the same, when they
are had in the Scripture or else in approved books […] But where in the country such memorials
still have their holy day, the whole or the half day, there they shall be kept in Christian
wise.

The Nassau-Dillenburg order after the county turned Reformed. **Nassau-Dillenburg (Reformed),
*Kirchenordnung*, 1582** (Sehling 10, p. 163):

<!-- doc 184 -->
> 48. Omnia festa sanctorum et abusus feriarum abrogentur et soli Dominici dies atque festa
> Christi retineantur.

48. Let all feasts of the saints and the abuses of holy days be abrogated, and let the Lord's
    days only and the feasts of Christ be retained.

**Note.** The same county's Lutheran order of 1537 had kept Corpus Christi, the Assumption and
the Nativity of Mary (§5.7, §8.9).

The Palatine order of 1556 had followed Württemberg (§5.3). Its Reformed successor keeps only
the three great feasts, New Year and Ascension. **Kurpfalz (Reformed), *Kirchenordnung*, 1563**
(Sehling 14, p. 397):

<!-- doc 504 -->
> Ordnung der feiertägen. An den feyertagen soll es gehalten werden wie am Sontag. Diese
> feyertag aber sollen gehalten werden: Alle Sontag. Der Christag sampt dem nechsten tag
> hernach. Der Jarßtag. Der Ostertag sampt dem nechsten tag hernach. Die Himelfahrt Christi. Der
> Pfingstag sampt volgendem Montag hernach.

Order of the holy days. On the holy days it shall be done as on Sunday. But these holy days
shall be kept: all Sundays; Christmas day with the next day after; New Year's day; Easter day
with the next day after; the Ascension of Christ; Whitsunday with the Monday following after.

**Note.** The editor notes that the 1556 order "hatte noch zusätzlich: Epiphanias, die Marien-
und Aposteltage, Johannis Baptistae und Michaelis": it still had Epiphany, the Marian and
apostles' days, John the Baptist and Michaelmas.

When Anhalt turned Reformed, the prince ordered a new agenda on the Palatine model. **Anhalt,
Prince Johann Georg to the superintendent, 4 March 1599** (Reformed; Sehling 2, p. 535):

<!-- doc 1261 -->
> eine algemeine agenda dieses fursten thums kirchen, auf und nach der pfälzischen gerichtet und
> derselben gleichförmig, zuvorfassen, und dass in derselben der apostel und andere festa
> eingestellet, und in den dreien haubt festen mehr nicht dann zwene tage gefeiert, der dritte
> aber gar abgeschaffet

to draw up a general agenda for the churches of this principality, directed upon and after the
Palatine one and conformable to the same; and that therein the apostles' and other feasts be
discontinued, and in the three chief feasts no more than two days be kept holy, but the third be
wholly abolished

**Note.** With these reductions, the ranking of §5 falls back to the two ranks of Luther's
*Formula missae*: Sunday and the feasts of the Lord.

---

## 16. Concordance of the orders quoted

Every order quoted or cited in this guide is listed below by region. The table gives:

- **Sehling**: the volume and pages in Sehling's edition.
- **Doc**: the `eko.db` document that holds the text, for full-text lookup. Note that a single
  database record may hold several orders.
- **Notes**: a short description of the order's system, or of what it contributes to the guide.
- **§**: the sections where the order is quoted.
